# Flagship effects comparison and implementation checklist

Research snapshot: **2026-11-27**. This compares manufacturer-documented categories and representative features for the named flagship processors. It is a focused documentation check, not a fresh audit of every algorithm in every firmware or of the whole effects catalogue. Product names below identify documented examples; TabletFX implementations should use project-authored names, controls, and signal paths rather than copy proprietary presets or imply circuit emulation.

## Fractal FM9-family Resonator block (2026-11-27)

The current November 2026 Fractal Blocks Guide, linked from FM9 Downloads, describes four parallel feedback delays and four corresponding bandpass filters (printed p. 92). It documents Manual frequencies of 100–10,000 Hz, Chordal mode with named major/minor/dominant/diminished/augmented qualities, and a 50–5,000 Hz chord-root Frequency. It also names Master Frequency/Level/Pan/Feedback/Q, Input Mode, Frequency 1–4, Feedback 1–4, Filter Location 1–4, Filter Q 1–4, Level 1–4, Pan 1–4, and Mix-page controls. The guide describes resonant/metallic harmony from non-melodic sources; this does not establish a pitch-tracked synth. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 Downloads and firmware applicability](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

TabletFX adds a project-authored four-voice Resonator at picker ID 122 in **Filters & wah**, disabled by default. It uses selectable Manual/Chordal voicings, four feedback-fed resonances, and project-selected level/pan/filter controls; Chordal selects a voicing and does not track the guitar’s chord. Project defaults and unlisted bounds remain project choices. The current guide's Mix page lists an opt-in Global Mix switch; TabletFX adds a project-authored Off-by-default switch and ±50 percentage-point offset that adjusts the stored Mix and clamps the effective value to 0–100%. This combination is a project interpretation, not a Fractal setting or parity claim. The effect is a resonant filter, not a pitch tracker or generated-instrument synth. Native tests, the focused local-guitar browser/WASM Play and reset checks, the full browser/WASM suite, and the Windows MinGW core/offline build pass. The latest 48 kHz stereo/128-frame benchmark measured Manual p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max at 0.287/0.290/0.407/0.588/4.340%, Chordal at 0.287/0.384/0.494/0.695/2.394%, and Global Mix enabled at 0.290/0.401/0.481/0.719/2.215% of a 2.667 ms callback; all had 0/20,000 deadline misses. An earlier repeat had one Chordal maximum outlier above the deadline, which did not recur. Native state is 31,216 B; browser effect state is 31,188 B in a fixed 100,663,296 B chain memory. The effect adds zero fixed latency and process-time allocations. The full browser/WASM run now reaches and passes this effect smoke; worklet message barriers make its reset-and-capture comparisons deterministic. Target-device timing and listening remain open. Fractal firmware v12.0 is listed for FM9 Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo by name; device firmware is unknown.

## Fender Tone Master Pro Studio Delay pan modes (2026-11-28)

The exact-model Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 50, lists Studio Delay controls `Time`, `Tap Division`, `Feedback`, `Mix`, `Low Cut`, `High Cut`, `Mod Rate`, `Mod Depth`, `Mod Mode`, `Pan Rate`, `Pan Depth`, `Pan Mode`, and `Spillover`. Firmware v1.6.34 documents Time as 30–2,000 ms; later notes through v1.8.87 (2026-11-23) name no Studio Delay changes. Installed firmware is unknown. The guide does not document DSP topology or the controls’ default values. [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds a project-authored Studio Pan Delay in the Delay category at picker ID 123. It implements a fixed-time stereo delay with panned repeats and Sine/Triangle/Envelope pan modes. It does not implement tap division/tempo, delay-time modulation, Low/High Cut, or Spillover, and its Envelope mode is a project interpretation. Default values and ranges other than the documented 30–2,000 ms Time span are project choices; no Fender DSP/settings/sound parity is claimed.

Its fixed 2-second stereo ring is allocated only when enabled: the bypassed browser/WASM instance reports 208 B; active reports 768,224 B including the 768,016 B ring. Native active memory is 768,288 B. `process()` is allocation-free and fixed latency is zero. Full native and browser/WASM tests pass; local stereo Guitar-TECHS DI Play produced a wet/dry RMS difference of 0.227378 and reset replay difference of 0. Callback percentiles and mode-specific maxima are recorded in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). These desktop results do not establish target-device timing or Fender parity.
## Line 6 Helix Stadium Dynamics Sidechain (2026-11-27)

The current [Stadium Effects manual](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) selector lists v1.4 Rev D, v1.3 Rev B, and v1.2. Its common Dynamics parameter description says Sidechain defaults to Off, using the block input to trigger Threshold; examples of other source levels include Instrument, Mic, Return, USB, and S/PDIF. It is offered for Dynamics models except Feedbacker. The live manual does not publish a complete source list or numeric ranges for this selector. The official [hardware v1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes) list v1.4.2 dated 2026-10-27 and do not call out Sidechain; that does not establish whether behavior changed. Installed device firmware is unknown.

TabletFX now exposes the existing Studio Compressor's project-authored Threshold, Ratio, Knee, Attack, Release, and Makeup controls under **Dynamics**. The new detector selector defaults to **Block Input (Off)**, preserving the prior behavior, or can use **Pre-effect Dry Chain** as a project-only source. It detects the signal at chain input before effect blocks while compressing the current compressor input, and reuses an existing buffer. This is not Stadium's separate Instrument/Mic/Return/USB/S/PDIF routing; no separate input buses, detector filter, or per-model routing are implemented. Numeric compressor ranges/defaults and DSP are project choices, not Stadium settings or sound parity.

make test passes all 123 native CTest cases and 13 fixture-safety checks. make web-test-stadium-dynamics-sidechain verifies the Dynamics picker and HeadlessChrome 151 playback of the local prerecorded Guitar-TECHS stereo DI; changing detector source produces relative output-difference RMS 0.77715. make windows-cross-build compiles the shared core and offline renderer with MinGW. The focused Release benchmark, state, latency, and memory figures are in LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md; no new audio buffer, fixed algorithmic latency, or process() allocation is added. External-bus sidechains, hardware comparison, human listening, and target-device timing remain open.

## XRecer XR1 FX Loop routing gap (2026-11-27)

The live [XRecer FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq), checked 2026-11-27, documents two external effects loops with SEND 1/2 and RETURN 1/2. It says an FX Loop block can adjust send level, return level, and the dry/returned mix. The [XR1 product page](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1) still says “3 Mono/Stereo FX Loops,” so the count and exact loop variants conflict across the manufacturer's pages. The FAQ's physical I/O description and the product-page statement do not establish a third send/return pair. The linked manual portal did not expose readable manual content in this audit.

TabletFX has no usable equivalent yet. The browser worklet and WASM chain process one selected input/output route with up to two channels. The current Windows WASAPI prototype opens default capture and render endpoints and does not provide independent hardware send/return selection. No XR1 FX Loop processor or placeholder is added; routing, device selection, exact controls, and external-hardware behavior remain an open host feature, not a DSP algorithm comparison.

## HeadRush Prime Orange Phaser Stereo control (2026-11-27)

The Prime v5.0.0 User Guide, §4.4.2, printed p. 23 (PDF p. 24), shows Orange Phaser controls for Rate, Stereo On/Off, and Sync On/Off. The pictured 0.6 Hz and Stereo On are screenshot values, not published ranges or defaults. The manual does not define what Stereo changes in the algorithm or how it persists. The official v5.2.0 firmware notes (October 2026) do not call out an Orange Phaser change; this does not establish the v5.0 manual's exact applicability to every later firmware. Installed firmware is unknown. [Prime User Guide v5.0.0](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [Prime v5.2.0 firmware notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf) · [HeadRush downloads](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads)

TabletFX adds a project-authored Stereo toggle to the existing Phaser in Modulation at picker ID 18. It defaults on to preserve the previous processor sound: on uses opposing left/right LFO phases, while off links both channels to the same LFO phase. This implements a useful stereo-spread choice, not a verified recreation of Prime's control behavior. It adds no delay buffer, fixed audio-path latency, or process-time allocation.

`make test` passes 13 fixture-safety tests and 122/122 native CTest cases. `make web-test-fender-phaser-90-tap-division` passes HeadlessChrome 151 using local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI: five Play captures include a Stereo On/Off comparison with output-difference RMS 0.05312; the full browser/native chain comparison passes. `make windows-cross-build` compiles the Windows MinGW core and offline renderer. Callback CPU and state measurements are in `LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`. Listening on hardware, Prime firmware confirmation, and target-device timing remain manual; no Prime algorithm, parameter, DSP, or sound parity is claimed.

## Line 6 Helix Stadium Pitch Ring Mod (2026-11-27)

The current Stadium model catalogue lists Pitch Ring Mod under Modulation as a Stereo Line 6 Original, with initial appearance 1.0. The live Effects manual selector reads v1.4 Rev D / v1.3 Rev B / v1.2; it describes the Modulation family but provides no Pitch Ring Mod-specific parameter table. The hardware v1.4.2 notes dated 2026-10-27 contain no Pitch Ring Mod change. Exact algorithm, controls, ranges/defaults, DSP, and latency are undocumented in these checked sources; installed hardware firmware is unknown. [Stadium model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/) · [Stadium Effects manual](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) · [v1.4.2 hardware release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes)

TabletFX adds a project-authored monophonic pitch-tracked ring modulator in Modulation at picker ID 121, disabled by default. Project controls are Carrier Ratio 0.25–4.0 (default 1.0), Fallback Pitch 55–880 Hz (110 Hz), Tracking Confidence 0.20–0.98 (0.55), Mix 0–1 (0.5), and Level −24…+12 dB (0 dB). It tracks the mono average of the input channels and shares that carrier across stereo output; chord tracking is not reliable. The audio path adds no fixed delay. Initial tracking takes about 42.7 ms, and the measured 220→440 Hz response is 48 ms. These controls and measurements are project choices, not a reconstruction of Line 6's undocumented model.

`make test`, `make web-browser-test`, and `make windows-cross-build` pass; the browser test plays the local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI through the categorized slot. At 48 kHz stereo/128 frames on a Ryzen 9 5950X Release build, 20,000 callbacks measured 1.0065/1.0812/1.2739/2.1676/4.3437% p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max of the 2.667 ms deadline, with zero misses. Native object state is 2,176 B; the pitch estimator uses about 7,180 B of temporary stack scratch; browser effect state reports 2,148 B. Processing allocates nothing. Human listening, physical Stadium comparison, and target-device measurements remain manual; no Line 6 DSP, settings, or sound parity is claimed.

## Fender Tone Master Pro Modulated Large Hall (2026-11-27)

The exact-model Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 55, describes Modulated Large Hall as building on Large Hall with modulation. It lists Level (wet blend), Decay, Dwell (input level), Diffuse (echo density/reflections), Rate, Depth, High Cut, Low Cut, and a Spillover switch. High Cut adjusts post-reverb high-shelf gain; Low Cut adjusts post-reverb low-shelf gain. The guide publishes no numeric ranges/defaults, shelf pivots, modulation topology, DSP, or latency. Fender's firmware history lists v1.8.87 dated 2026-11-23; those notes do not mention this effect. Installed firmware is unknown. [Tone Master Pro Model Guide, printed p. 55](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds a project-authored four-line modulated FDN in the Reverb category at picker ID 120, disabled by default. Project defaults are 28% Level, 6.5 s Decay, 1.00× input Dwell, 74% Diffuse, 0.23 Hz Rate, 20% modulation Depth, −1.5/−0.5 dB High/Low shelf gains, and Spillover off. Its bounds, delay lengths, shelf pivots, and modulation network are project decisions, not Fender settings. It preserves an immediate dry path and makes no Fender DSP or sound-parity claim.

`make test` passes documentation validation, 13 fixture-safety tests, and 121/121 CTests. `make web-build` and `make web-browser-test` pass the local Guitar-TECHS DI playback smoke with two profiles and the full browser/native comparison. `make windows-cross-build` passes. On a Ryzen 9 5950X Release build, 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks, 3,000 warm-up and 20,000 measured callbacks, the project-default isolated cost is p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.618/0.773/0.937/1.188/1.847% of the 2.667 ms callback deadline, with 0 misses. Native state is 118,184 B by object-plus-vector-capacity accounting; WASM reports 117,976 B. `process()` allocates nothing and adds zero fixed dry-path latency. Listening, physical comparison, and target-device performance remain manual; desktop timings do not predict tablet DSP.

## Current Line 6 Helix Stadium TAP modulation reset (2026-11-26)

The live Helix Stadium Effects manual selector lists v1.4 Rev D, v1.3 Rev B, and v1.2. Its common Speed/Rate text says one press of TAP resets modulation for LFO-based effects, with tremolos and rotary speakers as examples. It does not specify an exact phase angle or a numeric setting. Current hardware firmware v1.4.2 notes are dated 2026-10-27; installed firmware is unknown. [Effects manual, Common Effect Parameters](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) · [v1.4.2 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes)

TabletFX adds a global `TAP · reset modulation` action that calls a phase-only hook on the existing categorized Tremolo and Rotary Speaker processors. It preserves their controls, Tremolo smoothing ramps, and Rotary motor/delay history. This does not measure tempo, add a picker row, or reset every existing LFO-based processor; broader model coverage remains open. The action adds no processor state, sample-loop work, audio buffer, or fixed latency. `make test` passes 333 documentation requirements/346 rows, 13 fixture-safety checks, and 117/117 CTests. `make web-test` passes the local prerecorded Guitar-TECHS DI path and browser/native comparison (max absolute error 0.0000016615, RMS error 0.0000003308); its HeadlessChrome 151 self-test verifies the UI command reaches WASM. `make windows-cross-build` passes. The full browser chain reports 80,644,616 B processor state and 10,364 B tuner state in a fixed 100,663,296 B WASM memory. The one-shot TAP handler's CPU time is not separately measured; no effect DSP percentage is added per sample. Human listening and target-device/device-switch checks remain open. No Line 6 phase, settings, DSP, or sound parity is claimed.

**Fractal source revision verified (2026-11-26):** The PDF reached from the current official FM9 Downloads page has a November 2026 cover and 114 pages. Its printed-page map includes Chorus 25–26, Pitch 76–86, Resonator 92, Ring Mod 97, Rotary 98, Synth 102, Tremolo/Panner 106–107, Volume/Pan 109, and Wah 110. A direct fetch may return a stale June 2023 PDF, so use the document linked from Downloads when checking current controls. This is FM9-family evidence; firmware v12.0 is listed for Original and Turbo, not Mark II Turbo by name, and installed firmware is unknown. The exact-model Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 42, describes four Mode buttons selecting distinctive chorus modulations from subtle to extreme. It does not name the modes or publish their curves, numeric settings, stereo behavior, DSP use, or latency. Fender's firmware history is checked through v1.8.87 dated 2026-11-23; its notes do not identify a 3D Chorus change. The guide's applicability to later firmware and the installed firmware on any device remain unknown. [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds a project-authored `3D Chorus` picker entry at Modulation index 116, defaulting to Mode 1. Four UI choices are ordered from subtle to pronounced and configure one existing Chorus processor; they are not Fender mode names or a reconstruction of Fender's curves. The browser/WASM slot exposes the same four choices. Native state is 34,864 B and browser effect state is 34,820 B at 48 kHz stereo. Processing allocates nothing and adds no separate fixed-delay stage. Native tests, the full local-DI browser/WASM suite, Windows MinGW build, and callback benchmark pass. Human listening and device comparison remain open; no Fender settings, DSP, or sound parity is claimed.

## Current Fender Tone Master Pro Chorus Low Cut, High Cut, and Stereo Width (2026-11-26)

The exact-model Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 41, lists Low Cut and High Cut for Chorus Mono and Chorus Stereo; Width is listed only for Chorus Stereo. It explains their control roles but publishes no units, bounds, defaults, filter topology, DSP, or latency. Fender's firmware history was checked through v1.8.87 dated 2026-11-23; its current notes name no specific Chorus Low Cut, High Cut, or Width change. Installed firmware is unknown. The separate Analog Chorus and Stereo Analog Chorus names in older notes do not establish that these controls or behaviors apply to those models. [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds project-authored Low Cut and High Cut controls to the existing Modulation Chorus at picker ID 16, plus Stereo Width for stereo output. Defaults are both cuts off and Width 100%; project bounds are 0–1,200 Hz, 0–20,000 Hz, and 0–200%, respectively. The seven-value legacy Chorus preset array remains unchanged; the new values travel separately. Core/browser tests, full automated checks, local Guitar-TECHS DI Play, browser/native comparison, Windows cross-build, and callback benchmark pass. Native state is 34,848 B and WASM effect state is 34,812 B at 48 kHz stereo; fixed algorithmic latency and processing allocations remain zero. At this checkpoint, the remaining work is human listening and physical-device/target-device verification. Project controls and DSP do not claim Fender settings, DSP, algorithm, or sound parity.

## Current Fender Tone Master Pro Electric Flanger Filter mode (2026-11-26)

The Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 39, lists Electric Flanger controls Rate, Depth, Color, Tap Division, and a Flanger/Filter mode switch. It describes Filter mode as keeping the harmonic flanging characteristic while removing frequency modulation, with Rate changing the resulting tonality. Color adjusts effect intensity. Numeric bounds/defaults, DSP topology, and latency are not published. Firmware history was checked through v1.8.87 dated 2026-11-23; installed firmware remains unknown. [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds a project-authored static short-comb Filter mode to its existing Modulation Flanger at ID 17. Flanger remains the default; Filter Rate position maps 0.05–8 linearly to 0.5–10 ms, and project Color 0–0.9 (default 0.35) controls positive feedback. Tempo division, moving delay depth, feedback polarity, and stereo phase are disabled in Filter mode. It reuses the existing ring; the native instance is 4,840 B, browser state 4,804 B, with no added fixed latency or processing allocation. Native tests, CLI, seven local-guitar browser Play captures, Windows cross-build, and a callback benchmark pass. Device listening and physical Fender comparison remain open. The mapping is not a Fender settings, algorithm, DSP, or sound match.

## Previous verified checkpoint — Line 6 Helix Stadium Delay Trails on bypass (2026-11-26)

The live Helix Stadium Effects manual's common-parameter table describes Trails Off as muting delay repeats or reverb decay after block bypass, and Trails On as allowing the tail to fade after bypass or a snapshot change. It gives no default. Its selector/header says v1.4 Rev D, the cover says Firmware 1.4, and the footer says Rev E / Firmware v1.4.1; the separate Stadium v1.4.2 release notes are dated 2026-10-27. Installed firmware is unknown. Applicability to Simple Delay remains unresolved.

TabletFX adds a project-authored, default-off Trails on bypass control to the existing Delay row at picker ID 21. When enabled, that block preserves dry passthrough and drains stored wet repeats through its existing delay line after bypass; when off, the previous immediate-clear behavior remains. It adds no picker row or delay buffer, introduces no fixed latency, and processing does not allocate. Only block bypass is implemented; snapshot/preset transitions are not. Focused native/offline tests, local Guitar-TECHS DI browser/WASM Play, full `make test` (117/117 CTests), full `make web-test`, Windows MinGW cross-build, benchmark, and `make diff-check` pass. Current native state is 768,824 B in either toggle state. The isolated Ryzen 9 5950X benchmark uses 0.3098% p99 / 0.7028% p99.9 / 1.1097% maximum of a 2.667 ms callback deadline while draining, versus 0.0011% p99 while disabled; full percentiles are in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). These are project behavior and measurements, not Line 6 settings, DSP, or sound parity. [Helix Stadium Effects manual, common parameters](https://manuals.line6.com/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) · [Helix Stadium 1.4 release notes, v1.4.2 section](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes)

## Previous verified checkpoint — Fractal FM9-family Chorus Delay Time/Minimum Delay and Delay Range (2026-11-26)

The June 2023 Fractal Audio Blocks Guide, printed pp. 25–26, documents Chorus Delay Time (labeled Minimum Delay on the Expert page) at 0.1–50 ms and Delay Range at 1–40 ms. The separate voice-count behavior remains up to eight delay lines, no stated numeric voice minimum/default, more than two voices selects SINE, and choosing non-SINE resets the count to two. TabletFX adds project-authored Minimum Delay and Delay Range controls to the existing Modulation Chorus at ID 16: project ranges 0.1–50 ms and 1–40 ms, with defaults 8/14 ms. These defaults map the former 15 ms center / 7 ms depth to the same one-voice sample path. Legacy API/CLI calls and the existing browser parameter array retain their prior center/depth form and translate to the new controls. The fixed ring accommodates a 90 ms maximum tap; native state is 34,752 B at default and both timing endpoints. There is zero fixed algorithmic latency and no processing allocation. Focused native/offline and local guitar-DI browser/WASM Play checks at 0.1/1 and 50/40 ms, full make test (117/117 CTest), make web-test, Windows MinGW cross-build, make diff-check, and the dedicated Chorus benchmark pass. Callback measurements and remaining manual/device checks are in [LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). These are project controls and measurements, not Fractal DSP/settings/sound parity. ## Previous verified checkpoint — HeadRush Prime Reverb Tails (2026-11-26)

The exact-model Prime User Guide v5.0.0, printed p. 11 §4.1.3, documents per-effect Tails: a reverb or delay may continue decaying after its block is bypassed. The p. 22 §4.4.1 Eleven Reverb screen shows Decay, Tone, Pre-Delay, Mix, and Tails; its displayed numeric values are examples, not ranges or defaults. HeadRush lists v5.2.0 as current, but its 11.7 MB guide was not readable in this research pass and its release notes do not mention an Eleven Reverb change. Installed firmware is unknown.

TabletFX adds a project-authored, default-off `Tails on bypass` setting to the existing Room, Hall, Plate, and Spring Reverb controls in the Reverb category. Off preserves current immediate tail clearing. On preserves the dry path and drains stored wet output with zero new excitation; the existing networks are reused with no additional delay buffer or fixed latency. This does not add an Eleven Reverb algorithm or claim HeadRush DSP/settings/sound parity. Native tests, the local guitar-DI browser/WASM Play tail capture, all four UI-to-WASM control serializations, full browser/native comparison, Windows MinGW build, and benchmark checks pass. State, allocation, CPU percentiles, and manual/device limits are recorded in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). [Prime User Guide v5.0.0](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [HeadRush downloads](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads) · [Prime v5.2.0 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf)

## Previous verified checkpoint — Fractal FM9-family Chorus Tempo (2026-11-26)

The June 2023 Fractal Audio Blocks Guide, printed p. 25, says the Chorus `Tempo` control sets `Rate` rhythmically: quarter-note at 120 BPM yields 2 Hz, and `NONE` returns to manual Rate. It separately describes Rate fully counterclockwise as `LFO1 SYNC`; that controller source is not a tempo division and remains unimplemented here. The Blocks Guide is family-level documentation. The official FM9 downloads page lists v12.0 for FM9 Original and FM9 Turbo but does not name Mark II Turbo; exact applicability and installed firmware remain unknown.

TabletFX adds a project-authored Free Hz / quarter-note sync choice to the existing Modulation Chorus at picker ID 16. Free Rate remains 0.8 Hz with the existing 0.05–8 Hz bounds; project tempo is 20–300 BPM, default 120. The UI displays the calculated rate and locks manual Rate only while quarter-note sync is selected. Free mode maps to Fractal's documented `NONE` role, but the project adds no other divisions, phase reset/lock, or `LFO1 SYNC`. Native, offline CLI, full browser/native comparison and local guitar-DI Play, Windows MinGW, and benchmark checks pass. Native effect state is 12,456 B and browser/WASM effect state is 12,424 B; there is no fixed algorithmic latency and processing allocates nothing. On Ryzen 9 5950X Release, 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks, 20,000 measured callbacks after 3,000 warm-up give p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.043/0.066/0.072/0.270/1.030% of the 2.667 ms deadline in Free mode, and 0.043/0.069/0.073/0.264/0.510% in quarter-note/120 BPM mode. These project controls and measurements are not Fractal settings, DSP, phase behavior, or sound parity. Listening, physical-device verification, target-device timing, and exact Mark II Turbo firmware applicability remain open. [June 2023 Blocks Guide, p. 25](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 firmware downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

## Previous verified checkpoint — Fractal FM9-family Diatonic Harmony (2026-11-25)

The November 2026 Blocks Guide, printed pp. 76 and 80, identifies Dual Diatonic (2 voices), Quad Diatonic (4), and Quad Diatonic Delay (4). It documents intelligent **monophonic** scale-degree shifting, Key, Scale, Learn, Harmony 1–4, Glide Time, ±50-cent detune and 0–2,000 ms delay per voice; only the Quad Delay type has feedback after shifting. Custom scales support 4–8 notes with at least one semitone between degrees. The guide does not publish most numeric bounds/defaults, measured latency, DSP cost, or tracking-quality data. TabletFX now has a project-authored Dual/Quad row at Pitch ID 115. Its +2/+4 scale-step defaults use one single-note tracker, not chord tracking; its harmony-step indexing differs from Fractal's Harmony target-degree numbering. At 48 kHz fixed shifter latency is 5.333 ms and estimated first-note response is 21.3 ms at 220 Hz / 29.3 ms at low E, above the provisional ≤20 ms target. Native memory is 728,984 B without delay, 1,496,992 B with two full 2-second delays, and 2,265,000 B with four. Focused browser memory is 724,036 B after enabling the effect with delay off. Ryzen 9 5950X callback-load percentiles for eight profiles are recorded in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md); one Quad low-E/four-delay callback missed its 2.667 ms desktop deadline. Native/browser, full prerecorded-DI browser parity and Windows MinGW checks pass. Real performance, listening, Android device behavior, and exact FM9 Mark II Turbo firmware applicability remain unverified. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 firmware downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

[Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [Firmware](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) · [Mark II Turbo product page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9/) |
| **HeadRush Prime** | The official model page groups Vocal FX, Overdrive, Distortion/Fuzz, EQ, Compressor, Delay, Reverb, Chorus, Phaser/Flanger, Vib/Trem/Rotary, Wah/Filter, Synth, Rhythmic, Pitch, Volume/Dynamics, and Utility. Its Pitch FX includes Up Tune and Drop Tune; v5.0.0 introduced Up Tune and improved Drop Tune, while v5.0.1 added Drop Tune's Legacy/Refined mode. | The official v5.0 video describes Drop Tune's lower limit as −12 semitones and reduced latency, but publishes no exact latency. v5.0.1 says Refined uses the v5.0 algorithm and improves tracking speed/tonal quality. Prime User Guide v5.0.0 also documents generic per-effect Reverb/Delay Tails after bypass (p. 11 §4.1.3); the p. 22 Eleven Reverb screen shows Decay, Tone, Pre-Delay, Mix, and Tails, but its numeric values are examples only. The v5.2.0 guide is listed but was not readable here; its notes do not mention an Eleven Reverb change, and installed firmware is unknown. Up Tune controls/range and exact Drop Tune bounds/defaults/mix/polyphony/DSP remain unknown. TabletFX adds a project-authored Drop Tune workflow to the existing Pitch ID 22 shifter and a default-off bypass-tail control to existing Reverb rows, without an Eleven algorithm. It does not claim Prime algorithm, settings, tracking, or sound parity. Keep Prime-only models separate from Core. [Prime models](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/) · [Downloads](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads) · [v5.0.0 guide](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [v5.0 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [v5.0.1 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/501/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.0.1.pdf) · [v5.2.0 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf) · [official v5.0 video](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uHdfP6vwLb8) · [FAQ](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000834532-headrush-prime-frequently-asked-questions) |
| **XRecer XR1** | The official FAQ lists 111 effect-model entries, four IR loaders, nine FX Loop entries, four chains of up to eight blocks, per-chain CPU display, and a five-band Global EQ for selected outputs. User Manual V1.1.0 (November 2026), Global EQ section, documents per-band Assign, Type (Peak/HP/LP/Hi-Shelf/Lo-Shelf), Gain, Freq, Q, Bypass, plus master Bypass and Reset. | The checked manual section gives no numeric control ranges/defaults, filter topology, or bus-routing table. The project adds ID 113 in Equalizer as a project-authored in-chain approximation: it does not apply across presets/selected outputs and does not reproduce the XR1 dry-DI bypass routing. Other effect-model tables, CPU percentages, and installed OS remain unresolved. [Product page](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1) · [FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq) · [User Manual portal](https://wiki.xrecer.com/docs/global-eq/index.html) |

### Implementation checklist

- [x] Keep the project picker grouped by functional type, with a visible category tag on every row; preserve stable IDs and default-off behavior for optional stages. Pedal Detune is Pitch index 110; Mutant Filter is Filters & wah ID 111; Fractal Chromatic is Pitch ID 112; XR1-inspired in-chain Global EQ is Equalizer ID 113; Stadium-inspired seven-band PEQ is Equalizer ID 114; Fractal Diatonic Harmony is Pitch ID 115 (116 entries). New controls and signal paths are project-authored.
- [~] Broad project coverage exists in Drive & fuzz, Dynamics, Equalizer, Delay, Reverb, Modulation, Filters & wah, Pitch, Synth, IR Loader, Utility, and Loop & performance. Category presence means related function exists; it does not mean every manufacturer algorithm is implemented.
- [ ] Continue exact-source gap work one feature at a time: Axom DeepSynth/Instrument Simulation; Prime Hybrid Synth, Electric Piano, DB-33 Organ, Kill Seq, Splicer, and named Pitch models; Fender Virtual Capo and newer synth/sequencer/reverb families; remaining Stadium block-specific behavior; and Fractal three-oscillator synth behavior. Do not present a broad category as an exact model match.
- [~] Melofty AXOM MAX source audit (2026-11-26): the exact-model User Manual V1.7.4 (2026-08-31, pp. 3, 8, 27, 45, 54) names DeepSynth and `Inst.Sim`, but only documents DeepSynth Quick Preset use, broad module categories, and per-output EQ enablement; it provides no model-level effect catalog, DeepSynth controls, EQ bands/ranges, or algorithm DSP. The official downloads page lists Axom Max firmware V1.9.2 dated 2026-10-27, but provides no readable release notes. Existing looper Auto Record/Reverse/Half-Speed coverage and the in-chain Global EQ at ID 113 do not establish exact Axom behavior or its output-specific EQ. Keep Axom synth/instrument-simulation implementation as an open source gap; do not invent controls or claim parity. [Manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) · [Official downloads](https://www.meloftyaudio.com/support/downloads)
- [~] Fender Tone Master Pro post-guide model audit (2026-11-26): firmware history dates Aethon/Cerberus/Wavemorph Polysynth to v1.7.53 and Pitch Sequencer, Prismatic Delay, Cirrostratus Synthverb, and Spectral Reverb to v1.8.45; currently listed firmware is v1.8.87 (2026-11-23). The official v1.1.100 Model Guide predates these additions and has no parameter tables for them. Fender's launch article describes pitched delay, pitch-shifted reverb, and synth-infused reverb qualitatively; controls, ranges, defaults, DSP, and synth/polyphony behavior remain unknown. Do not transfer controls from the separate generic Chromatic Pitch Shifter to these models or claim functional parity from similar project effects. [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates) · [Feature article](https://www.fender.com/articles/instruments/the-latest-tone-master-pro-update-is-here) · [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf)
- [~] HeadRush Prime source audit (2026-11-26): Prime v5.2.0 cumulative notes retain Drop Tune's v5.0.1 Legacy/Refined selector; Refined is described as improving tracking speed and tonal quality, but no algorithm, latency, polyphony, or default is stated. Prime's v5.0.0 guide also documents a Drum Machine with fill, count-in, auto-tempo/fill, accent, kit, groove, and intensity controls; screen values are examples, not numeric ranges/defaults, and kit/pattern assets are proprietary. A project-authored mode or rhythm feature must not imply Prime DSP/content parity. The linked 11.7 MB v5.2.0 guide is listed by HeadRush but no readable official mirror was verified. [v5.2.0 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf) · [v5.0.0 guide](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [Prime models](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/)
- [~] XR1 User Manual V1.1.0 is now readable for Global EQ: implement only its documented control roles and keep the project stage explicitly in-chain. Exact numeric ranges/defaults, filter topology, selected-output routing, dry-DI bypass integration, and the remaining effect-model parameter tables still need verification.
- [ ] Add appropriate non-guitar fixtures for Prime Vocal FX and other source paths that cannot be validated with guitar-only audio.

Parameter ranges, defaults, firmware applicability, documented CPU/block limits, project CPU percent, memory, and latency are tracked separately. Unknown stays unknown until the exact-model source or device check resolves it.

**Current Fender Tone Master Pro Pedal Detune checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The exact-model Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 68, documents SHALLOW/DEEP and a POSITION blend between dry and detuned signal; it publishes no numeric values or DSP topology. TabletFX adds a project-authored Pitch row at index 110, reusing its existing modulated-delay core with 0.35/1.4 ms project depths. It does not track notes or claim Fender DSP/settings/sound parity. Native tests, focused local-guitar browser/WASM Play, Windows MinGW build, and full `make web-test` pass. Browser/native comparison across 78 optional processor stages reports maximum absolute error 3.40097e-6 and RMS error 5.88049e-7. At 48 kHz stereo/128 frames, benchmark p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max cost is 0.0563/0.0570/0.0836/0.2460/0.6330% of the 2.667 ms deadline; memory is 3,672 B native and 3,632 B in the browser. Human listening and target-device checks remain open. [Tone Master Pro Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf)

**Current Line 6 Helix Stadium XL Poly Detune checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The official model catalogue labels Poly Detune Mono, Line 6 Original, first appearing in Stadium 1.0. The current live Dynamic DSP page groups it with high-DSP Poly effects: at most two such effects per preset and one per primary path. That DSP grouping is not evidence of polyphonic input tracking. The live manual's Effects pages do not publish Poly Detune's individual parameter table; the online manual currently carries a combined revision label, while installed firmware is unknown. TabletFX adds a project-authored Modulation row at picker index 109, disabled by default. It provides an immediate dry path and up to two short modulated-delay taps, with project controls for tap count, rate, depth, width, mix, and level. This is a widener-like modulated delay, not a static pitch shifter, note tracker, or Line 6 algorithm/settings/DSP parity. Full native, browser/WASM, and Windows core checks pass; isolated CPU and memory measurements are in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). **Current Fractal FM9-family pitch-range checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The November 2026 Blocks Guide, printed pp. 76–80, lists Dual Chromatic (two voices), Quad Chromatic (four), and Quad Chromatic Delay (four), each with per-voice Shift of ±24 semitones and Detune of ±50 cents; the algorithms differ in delay and feedback routing. The guide's Pitch-block comparison lists one Pitch block for FM9. Fractal's downloads page lists firmware v12.0 dated 2026-07-07 as compatible with FM9 Original and FM9 Turbo, but does not name Mark II Turbo. Exact Mark II Turbo firmware applicability and installed firmware remain unknown. TabletFX extends the existing single-control Polyphonic Pitch Shifter at Pitch picker index 22 from ±12 to ±24 semitones, neutral by default, in browser/WASM and offline paths. This does not add independent Fractal-style voice routing, per-voice controls, or Fractal DSP/settings/sound parity. Native endpoint tone/chord tests, focused local-guitar browser Play, full-chain browser/native comparison, and Windows core build pass. The full-chain comparison meets tolerance, but earlier combined `make web-test` runs failed later in an existing Stereo Crossover local-guitar smoke; a separate `make web-browser-test` passed. This unrelated broader check remains a recorded intermittent issue, not a pitch-range failure. The existing engine's 48 kHz latency, memory, and +24 CPU load are recorded in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). **Current Fractal FM9-family Diatonic custom-scale checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The November 2026 Blocks Guide, printed p. 81, documents a custom scale with 4–8 notes, tonic-relative semitone offsets, and at least a one-semitone gap between successive degrees. Its Tonic control is a display reference; Key controls the sounding transposition. TabletFX adds project-authored custom scale selection and editing to the existing single-voice Diatonic Harmonizer at Pitch picker index 50, in native code, CLI, and browser/WASM. It keeps the existing monophonic tracker, one generated harmony voice, and detector/shift latency; no extra processor or voice is created. Two-/four-voice intelligent harmony, per-voice detune/delay/pan/level, and Fractal DSP/settings/sound parity remain open. The current project scale is tested with a four-note pattern; it is not a recreation of Fractal's separate Custom Shifter. **Current Fender Tone Master Pro Pedal Shifter checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The exact-model Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 68, documents `PITCH` (one of 10 intervals at full position) and `POSITION` (unison at minimum to the selected interval at maximum). It says the Whammy-inspired effect removes dry signal and can be assigned to an expression pedal or a Parameter Change footswitch. The guide does not name the ten intervals, publish numeric bounds/defaults, or specify the sweep curve. Fender firmware history currently lists v1.8.85 (2026-11-18); its notes describe the separate polyphonic Virtual Capo and do not mention Pedal Shifter changes. Installed firmware is unknown. TabletFX adds a software-only Pedal Shifter mode inside the existing categorized Pitch shifter row at index 22: a continuous project-selected target from −24 to +24 semitones and linear POSITION from heel/unison to toe/target. It reuses the current wet-only polyphonic STFT shifter; it adds no second instance, buffer, state, or fixed latency. Native, focused local-guitar browser/WASM, full browser, and Windows MinGW checks pass. The UI slider is not routed to physical expression hardware; target/position mapping and control range are project choices, not Fender interval/DSP/sound parity. CPU, memory, latency, listening and device evidence are recorded in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). **Current Fender Tone Master Pro Virtual Capo Mix checkpoint (2026-11-25):** Firmware v1.8.85 (2026-11-18) introduced a rebuilt polyphonic Virtual Capo across ±12 semitones; Fender says it maintains unity gain, the same CPU use, a flat frequency response, cleaner attacks, and low latency. Current firmware v1.8.87 (2026-11-23) fixes a public-beta semitone error for settings +1 through +OCT. Fender's feature article says Mix blends shifted and original signals, but neither source publishes Mix bounds/defaults or measured latency/CPU. TabletFX exposes project-authored 0–100% Mix in the existing Pitch row at index 22, defaulting to 100% shifted; 0% is original only. A fixed 256-frame dry-history ring aligns the original path to the existing pitch engine's 256-sample nominal wet latency (5.333 ms at 48 kHz), adding no further fixed delay. The shared ring adds 2,048 B and object alignment brings reported native stereo state from 494,016 B to 496,080 B; no second shifter or separate buffer is allocated per Mix profile. Native, WASM-build, focused browser/WASM local-guitar Play, Windows MinGW, and isolated benchmark checks pass. The measured Fender latency, DSP, and settings/sound parity remain unknown; the installed unit firmware is unknown. [Fender firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates) · [Fender Virtual Capo feature article](https://www.fender.com/articles/parts-and-accessories/unlock-new-effects-with-tone-master-pro-update)

The full `make web-browser-test` also passed after the page deadline was raised from 60 to 180 seconds. Its browser/native comparison across 78 optional processor stages reported 3.4009653e−6 maximum absolute error and 5.8804948e−7 RMS error; the focused browser Play test separately verifies the 0/50/100% Mix blend.

**Current Line 6 Helix Stadium XL 4 OSC Generator checkpoint (2026-11-24):** Line 6's live model catalogue lists 4 OSC Generator as Mono/Stereo, a Line 6 Original first appearing in Stadium 1.0. The catalogue says it produces sound without an instrument and is off by default; it does not publish oscillator controls, note handling, numeric ranges/defaults, DSP use, or latency. TabletFX adds a separately categorized Synth source at picker index 108 (display row 109), disabled by default and placed before the effect chain. Its four static-note voices use project-authored sine/triangle/saw/square waveforms, MIDI note 0–127, octave −2…+2, per-voice level, and master output. Defaults are four sine voices at MIDI 48/55/60/64, level 0.15 each and master 0.35. It replaces the live input when enabled; it does not track guitar pitch or consume MIDI. These controls and DSP are not Line 6 settings, sound, or algorithm parity. [Line 6 Helix Stadium model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/)

It gives no numeric ranges/defaults, ramp or detector behavior, rotor geometry, stereo configuration, latency, DSP cost, or block limit. A, p. 45 names Time, Feedback, Mix, Tap Division, repeat Low/High Cut, Threshold, Attenuation, Release, Mod Rate/Depth/Mode (`SINE`/`TRI`/`RAND`/`TRIG`), and Spillover. TabletFX now adds tempo sync (20–300 BPM; 14 project-selected divisions), retained Free Time, feedback-path Low/High Cut, and project-authored Sine/Triangle/Random/Triggered Ramp modulation to the existing Delay-category Ducking Delay row at picker index 39. Rate is 0.05–10 Hz (0.8 default); depth is off by default (0–50 ms, capped by available delay headroom); the project Triggered Ramp resets on the existing Threshold crossing and rearms below threshold. The browser UI/WASM Play smoke uses local prerecorded Guitar-TECHS DI at 120 BPM/eighth, 150/7,000 Hz cuts, and 1.25 Hz/6.5 ms Sine modulation; it captured a peak of 0.0614053 across 71,430 active samples (difference RMS 0.0138586). `make test` passes 102/102 CTest cases plus 11 fixture checks; `make web-test` passes the 99-row picker and 73-stage browser/native comparison (max absolute error 6.683e-6; RMS 1.218e-6); Windows MinGW core cross-build also passes. At 48 kHz stereo/128 frames, one `make bench` run measured default p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.1331/0.1335/0.1797/1.7003/2.8043% and filtered/modulated 0.1823/0.1838/0.2355/1.6733/1.7640% of the 2.667 ms deadline, with 768,296 B owned stereo state. Browser fixed memory is 96 MiB, with 78,988,648 B processor state plus 10,364 B tuner state. These are desktop processor-call estimates, not live-device latency. Tempo, bounds, defaults, feedback filters, modulation curves, and DSP are project-authored. No Fender settings/DSP/sound parity, listening, or device result is claimed. Sources: [Tone Master Pro Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Fender firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates).

**Current Fractal Synth verification checkpoint (2026-11-25):** The November 2026 FM9-family Blocks Guide (p. 102) documents three oscillators but monophonic input tracking; all voices share the Guitar/Bass range selection. Its per-voice surface is Waveform, Track (Off/Env Only/Pitch+Env/Quantize), Frequency, Shift, Tune ±50 cents, Duty Cycle, Pan, Filter Frequency/Q, Attack and Level. Shared Mix-page controls also include Mix, Level, Balance, Bypass, Bypass Mode and Global Mix. The guide says disabling a voice can conserve CPU, but does not publish most numeric ranges/defaults, DSP cost, latency, or key/scale controls for Quantize. The project-authored Three-Voice Tracked Synth already exists in Synth at picker ID 78; a browser smoke now activates Sine/Triangle/Square together in Bass mode from prerecorded Guitar-TECHS DI. This verifies the UI/worklet/WASM audio path, not real-bass tracking or musical quality. At 48 kHz, Bass detection's ~128 ms analysis window and 32 ms hop produce observed project response about 0.1–0.22 s; that is a control/tracking response, not fixed sample-path latency. The FM9 guide is family-level evidence. The v12.0 download listing names FM9 Original/Turbo, not Mark II Turbo explicitly, and the installed firmware is unknown.

Automated verification: `make test` passed docs validation, 11/11 fixture checks, and 115/115 CTest cases; `make web-test` passed Browser/WASM Play and full browser/native comparison (max absolute error 3.3736e−6, RMS error 5.8412e−7); `make windows-cross-build` passed Windows MinGW core/offline builds. The processor owns 1,031,344 B. On Ryzen 9 5950X Release at 48 kHz stereo/128 frames (2.667 ms callback), Guitar mode p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max was 11.7641/15.1125/17.0858/20.6417/22.3742%; Bass mode was 11.8406/17.3034/20.9259/27.9432/50.2461%. These are isolated desktop CPU estimates, not device guarantees; the Bass maximum is a notable spike. Human listening, actual bass input, hardware behavior and target-device CPU remain open. No Fractal DSP/settings/sound parity is claimed. Sources: [Fractal Audio Blocks Guide, November 2026, p. 102](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/).

**Current Fractal Ring Mod pitch-tracking checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The November 2026 Blocks Guide, p. 97, documents Classic, SSB Upper/Lower, 2–2,000 Hz Frequency, 0.25–4× Frequency Multiplier, optional Pitch Tracking, Guitar/Bass Tracking Mode, High Cut Frequency, and Mix-page controls. Bass shifts tracking down an octave to about F♯0. Project Ring Modulator (picker ID 34) now adds opt-in monophonic tracking with project-selected 55–1,100 Hz Guitar and 23.125–550 Hz Bass ranges; the static Frequency remains the fallback before lock and after signal loss. Its detector window, YIN-style method, ranges and smoothing are project-authored, not Fractal DSP/settings or tracking-quality parity. `make test` passes 102/102 CTest cases and 11 fixture-safety checks, including 110 Hz Guitar and 30 Hz Bass tones. `make web-test` passes the local prerecorded guitar-DI UI/worklet smoke (80,336 changed samples, RMS difference 0.088496 against fixed-carrier mode) and browser/native comparison. That smoke proves the browser path changes output, not note-estimation accuracy. No bass recording, listening, hardware, or isolated CPU evidence exists. FM9 v12.0 download compatibility names Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo explicitly; exact applicability and installed firmware are unknown. Sources: [Fractal Audio Blocks Guide, June 2023, p. 97](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/).

**Published Fractal XVR checkpoint (2026-11-23):** The Blocks Guide snapshot used when this checkpoint was first published documented a two-way stereo fourth-order Linkwitz–Reilly crossover, a Frequency control with ×1/×10 multiplier, separate low/high levels and pans for left/right inputs, and Mix-page Level, Balance, Bypass, and Bypass Mode. TabletFX adds a separate Crossover-category Stereo Crossover at picker index 86, with project-authored 20–2,000 Hz base frequency, −60…+12 dB band/output levels, and −1…+1 pan/balance. Its LR4-style biquad implementation and values are project choices, with no Fractal DSP/settings parity claim. The guide is FM9-family documentation; checked firmware v12.0 names FM9 Original and FM9 Turbo but not Mark II Turbo, so exact Mark II applicability remains qualified. Listening, hardware, and exact device-firmware checks remain open. The current guide revision is recorded below.

**Published Fractal Panner checkpoint (2026-11-23):** The November 2026 Blocks Guide, pp. 106–107, documents FM9-family Panner Rate/Tempo, Width, LFO Type/Duty/Shape/Phase, Pan Center, Ducking, and Mix-page Level/Balance/Bypass/Bypass Mode. Width above 100% is described as psychoacoustic widening; no numeric bounds/defaults are given. TabletFX adds a project-authored Modulation stage at picker index 90 with separate width, phase, center, ducking, level, balance, LFO, tempo-division, and smoothing controls. The ranges/defaults, mapping, stereo math, and DSP are project choices. `make test`, `make web-build`, `make inspect-effect-picker`, and `make web-browser-test` pass; the full stereo browser/native comparison reports max error 0.00000780769 and RMS error 0.00000153992, with a separate local stereo-guitar Panner Play smoke. The browser chain reports 32,959,916 B processor state and 10,364 B tuner state in fixed 64 MiB WASM memory. Fractal DSP/settings/sound parity, per-effect CPU, listening, and hardware behavior remain unchecked. The guide is FM9-family documentation; official v12.0 lists Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo by name, and installed firmware is unknown.

**Current Cloud Reverb checkpoint (2026-11-23):** Fender's Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 names Mix, Tone, Decay, Diffuse, High Damp, Low Damp, Rate, Depth, and Spillover, and describes pitch-modulated ambience. It supplies no numeric ranges/defaults, pitch interval/voice count, modulation waveform, or DSP topology. Fender firmware v1.1.100 introduced the model and v1.2.56 changed High Damp increments from 1 Hz to 100 Hz; the latest checked v1.8.85 release notes do not mention Cloud. TabletFX now has a separate project-authored Reverb-category Cloud stage at picker slot 79. Its ranges, defaults, modulation, and network are project choices, with no Fender DSP or control parity claim. `make test` and `make web-browser-test` pass; the generated local-guitar browser smoke captured output at slot 79. Listening and hardware checks remain open.

**Current Fender Tape Echo checkpoint (2026-11-23):** Fender's Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 pp. 49–50 documents Tape Echo's Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Record Level, Tape Bias, Tape Noise, Wow and Flutter, and Spillover; Stereo Tape Echo adds separate left/right delay times and tap divisions. It publishes no numeric ranges/defaults or DSP and warns that high Feedback may self-oscillate. TabletFX implements separate project-authored left/right free times or tap divisions under a shared time mode/BPM, while preserving the existing shared-time default, in the Delay category at picker index 84. Project ranges/defaults, saturation/noise/modulation, and delay processing are not Fender settings or DSP parity. The v1.8.85 firmware notes dated 2026-11-18 do not mention this model, so exact applicability of the v1.1.100 guide to v1.8.85 is unconfirmed. Listening and hardware checks remain open. Sources: [Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) and [Fender current firmware notes](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-gb/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates).

**Current Rock Box Dist checkpoint (2026-11-23):** Helix Stadium 1.4 documents Drive, Tone, and Level; the notes describe lower Tone as darker and higher Tone as brighter but publish no numeric ranges, defaults, or DSP. TabletFX adds a project-authored Drive & fuzz processor at picker index 88 with those three controls, plus a project-only smoothing control. Its soft clipper, one-pole tone stage, ranges, defaults, and smoothing do not claim Line 6 or Z.Vex parity. Native tests, the 91-row categorized picker, WASM build, and a local-guitar browser Play smoke pass. Listening and device checks remain open. [Helix Stadium 1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes).

**Current Rock Box Boost checkpoint (2026-11-23):** Helix Stadium 1.4 gives two controls: Boost sets the boost-circuit output level, while Level sets the overall block output. It publishes no numeric bounds, defaults, or DSP topology. TabletFX adds a separate project-authored Drive & fuzz entry at index 89 with independently smoothed Boost (0…+24 dB) and Level (−24…+12 dB) controls. The two linear gain stages and their project ranges/defaults do not claim Line 6 or Z.Vex settings, DSP, or sound parity. The effect is separately switchable from Rock Box Dist. Native tests and the 92-row picker pass. Browser/WASM build and Play check pass on a one-second mono excerpt from the existing local prerecorded Guitar-TECHS DI recording; the 71-stage browser/native comparison has maximum absolute error 0.000021462 and RMS error 0.000004163. Listening and device checks remain open. [Helix Stadium 1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes) · [current Helix Stadium model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/).

**Current HeadRush Prime BBD mode checkpoint (2026-11-26):** Prime v5.1 introduced BBD Delay `Mode`; the current v5.2 notes describe CLASSIC as v5.0-and-earlier behavior and MOD as improving engaged/bypassed levels, repeat-to-repeat level, and Feedback linearity/range. No numeric parameters, ranges/defaults, or DSP formula are published. TabletFX exposes project-authored Classic/Mod in the existing Delay row (ID 21); Mod uses a gentler feedback saturation curve plus a 2% repeat-path gain adjustment at a representative 0.5 linear level. It reuses the delay ring and adds no processor, buffer, fixed latency, or processing allocation. `make test` (117 CTests), local guitar-DI browser Play/WASM, full browser/native parity, Windows MinGW build, and separate Classic/Mod benchmarks pass. The current desktop p99 is 0.2449% Classic / 0.2640% Mod stereo; both use 768,824 B. Prime engaged/bypass level matching and Feedback mapping remain unimplemented, so this is partial functional coverage, not HeadRush parity. [Prime v5.2.0 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf).

**Current Line 6 Stadium Deluxe Looper checkpoint (2026-11-24):** Line 6's live Looper page documents one block per preset, mono/stereo variants, volatile loops that are not stored with presets and are cleared at power-off, capacities of 120 s mono / 60 s stereo at full speed (double at half speed), Playback, per-pass Overdub decay, Low Cut, and High Cut, plus Clear, Undo/Redo, Speed, Reverse, Record/Overdub, Play/Stop, and Play x1 actions. TabletFX now provides a project-authored Looper at picker ID 98 in Loop & performance, with the documented transport actions and project-selected playback-level, decay, and filter ranges/defaults; Line 6 does not publish numeric ranges/defaults here. Project DSP and settings do not claim Line 6 parity. The online manual selector identifies Rev E / v1.4.1; hardware release notes separately list v1.4.2 and do not establish an effect change. [Looper manual](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/looper-block) · [firmware notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes)

**Current Pitch checkpoint (2026-11-24):** Fender Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, p. 66 lists Chromatic Pitch Shifter controls Mix, Pitch (−2…+2 octaves), Delay, Tap Division, Feedback, Tone, and Spillover; it says it typically works best with single notes, without establishing input-note polyphony, tracking quality, or DSP. TabletFX now has a project-authored Chromatic Pitch Delay at Pitch index 99, with ±24 semitones, free or tempo-synced delay, Feedback, Tone, Mix, and optional block-bypass Spillover. The project Arpeggiator now also supports ±24 scale degrees, but remains monophonic with ~26.7 ms first-note response and measures 15.8172/25.3743/26.5185/38.7033/63.0741% across those same callback percentiles. Both processors use project-authored DSP and settings; no vendor parity or device performance claim is made. Human listening and tablet tests remain open. The notes publish no model-specific parameter ranges, defaults, or DSP. | **Implemented and verified:** project-authored Step Tremolo appears at picker ID 97 under Modulation. Controls are 0.05–20 Hz rate (4 Hz default), 0–1 depth (0.75 default), 1–16 steps (8 default), 16 editable step levels, and 0–100 ms transition (4 ms default). Full browser/native comparison: max absolute error 6.68e-6 and RMS error 1.22e-6; 32,899,584 bytes processor memory within the 64 MiB WASM limit. These ranges, defaults, step pattern, transition behavior, and DSP are project-authored; no Fender parity is claimed. | **Filter** | Fractal's November 2026 FM9-family Blocks Guide documents 20 Stereo Filter types. Auto-Wah is an automatic LFO sweep; Envelope Filter opens on the pick attack; Touch-Wah follows playing volume and the note's natural fade; Ladder Filter is a resonant low/band-pass, and Touch-Ladder applies the Touch-Wah envelope controls to that Ladder design instead of Frequency/LFO. Touch-Wah controls: Mode (Lowpass/Bandpass/Highpass/Mix), Sensitivity, Attack Time, Release Time, Sweep Shape, Resonance, Start/Stop Freq (100 Hz–10 kHz), Emphasis, Detector Source (Block In or physical input). Touch-Ladder uses those envelope controls and block or physical detector source with the same Ladder Mode, Resonance, Drive, Bass Comp, and Order controls. Ladder controls: Mode (Low-Pass/Band-Pass), Resonance, Drive, Low-Pass-only Bass Comp, Order (2-/4-pole; 4-pole documented default), Low/High Cut, and LFO controls. Most controls have no numeric bounds/defaults in the guide. Common filter controls include Frequency, Order, Q, Gain or comb Feedback, Lowcut/Hicut, stereo Pan/phase, and comb Delay Time/Depth. | **Partial, implemented and verified:** the project-authored Stereo Filter remains at picker ID 96 in Filters & wah and now represents all 20/20 named types. It separates LFO-driven Auto-Wah, Envelope Filter, direct-volume Touch-Wah, Ladder Filter, and Touch-Ladder; Touch-Wah follows the smoothed block-input level through natural note fade in LP/BP/HP modes, while Envelope Filter uses a project-authored attack trigger (65% rise gate) that decays during sustained input. Touch-Ladder applies the direct playing envelope to the Ladder response. Ladder and Touch-Ladder share project-scaled Resonance (0–1, default .25), Drive (0–1, default 0), Bass Compensation (0–1, default 0; LP only), and 2/4-pole selection. Touch-Ladder/Touch-Wah sweep endpoints use the documented 100 Hz–10 kHz span; other scales, threshold, response curves, filter equations, and DSP are project-authored. Block input is supported. Touch-Wah MIX now uses a project-authored 50% dry/band-pass blend, and Block/Physical detector selection is implemented for Touch-Wah and Touch-Ladder. Physical reads the original raw chain-input span; this is not a separate hardware jack or Fractal routing emulation. `make test-stereo-filter` passes 1/1 CTest. `make web-test-stereo-filter` verifies both Touch-Ladder and Touch-Wah 35-value UI/WASM configurations and captures Touch-Wah playback through the existing Filters & wah picker row at ID 96 from local prerecorded stereo guitar DI (48 kHz, peak 0.191801, 95,138 active samples, difference RMS 0.039306). Latest `make test` passes 11 fixture-safety checks and 112 CTest cases; `make web-test` passes the 78-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 3.40097e-6; RMS error 5.88049e-7) in the fixed 96 MiB WASM heap. An earlier full browser run had a transient Stereo Crossover reference mismatch; its immediate rerun passed, as did the latest full run. No Fractal DSP/settings/sound parity is claimed; the guide is FM9-family evidence, firmware v12.0 names Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo explicitly, and installed firmware remains unknown. A separate earlier Touch-Ladder local-DI Play capture produced peak 0.073766, 95,994 active samples, and difference RMS 0.033763. An initial full browser attempt hit the unrelated Stereo Crossover reference check (maximum difference 0.0348242; RMS difference 0.00733673); its immediate rerun and the latest full run passed. |

The separate Phrase Looper remains four seconds and unquantized. The Shuffling Looper now has an XR1-inspired project Beat option, Off by default, using a project quarter-note grid at 40–300 BPM (120 default, tempo latched when armed). XR1's source does not document subdivisions/defaults/tempo changes, so this is beat-alignment function coverage only, not XR1 parity. Prime's named blocks and Axom's long stereo loop/routing graph remain absent. XR1 chain/external-loop workflow remains absent; its FX-loop count is contradictory (FAQ says two, product I/O graphic says three). | **Instrument simulation / IR loader** | Axom V1.7.4 selects Inst.Sim and Synth as separate module categories but supplies no named model/control inventory. XR1 FAQ distinguishes Cabinet IR Loader from Reverb IR Loader, each with single/dual variants, and specifies 24-bit/48 kHz WAV of exactly 2,048 samples. | **Partial:** the project now has a project-authored single-mono Cabinet IR loader at picker index 100 in the 101-entry categorized picker. It accepts the documented WAV shape and applies the mono IR independently to mono/stereo input; channel count and “dual” behavior are unspecified by XR1, so stereo/dual compatibility is not claimed. At 48 kHz it uses 64-frame partitions (1.333 ms fixed latency); this is not XR1 latency or DSP parity. Axom exact instrument-simulation models and controls remain unverified; app notes do not establish firmware controls. [Axom downloads](https://www.meloftyaudio.com/support/downloads) · [Axom V1.7.4 manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) · [XR1 FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq)

**Documentation revisions and limits:** Fender's checked current firmware entry is v1.8.85 (2026-11-18), while several effect names were introduced in v1.8.45; no current installed firmware was inspected. The online Helix Stadium manual selector identifies Rev E / firmware v1.4.1, while separate hardware release notes report v1.4.2; the current model list says Stadium and XL share effect blocks. Prime v5.2.0 release notes do not add parameters for the named v4.0 synth/rhythmic/reverb features, and the v5.2 User Guide was too large to parse in this review. Axom's accessible exact-model manual is V1.7.4 (2026-08-31), while official downloads list firmware, controller, and app V1.9.2 dated 2026-10-27; app-store release text must not be attributed to firmware without confirmation. XR1's official FAQ/product page is readable, but its JavaScript manual portal was not; category and aggregate counts do not reveal exact effect models. This remains a focused comparison of selected, source-checked features rather than a fresh full-catalog audit.

**Current Line 6 Poly Sustain checkpoint (2026-11-23):** Line 6's Stadium model catalogue marks Poly Sustain as mono, polyphonic, DSP-intensive, and first available in Stadium 1.0. The current online Stadium manual identifies Rev E / v1.4.1; its dynamic DSP guidance allows at most two Poly effects per preset and one per primary path. The current hardware v1.4.2 notes list fixes, not Poly Sustain changes. Older Helix 3.0/3.10 notes supply control roles—Interval, Attack, Decay, Mod Freq/Depth, FX Level, RandDpth, RandSpeed, Level, Auto EQ, and Operation—but are not a Stadium control table and publish no verified Stadium ranges/defaults. TabletFX now has a project-authored Delay-category hold at picker index 91. It reuses an 80 ms bounded time-domain capture and the project's polyphonic pitch shifter; its ±12-semitone interval, Attack/Decay, FX Level, Level, and operation controls are project choices. Modulation/random controls, Auto EQ, Line 6's spectral algorithm, DSP, and sound parity are absent. Native tests, the 94-row categorized picker, 71-stage browser/native comparison, and a local prerecorded guitar Play smoke pass; human listening and hardware checks remain open. ## Flagship effect coverage checklist (checked 2026-11-23)

**Fractal guide revision update (2026-11-26):** The current PDF linked from the official FM9 Downloads page is the *Fractal Audio Systems Blocks Guide — Complete Reference for Blocks and Parameters*, with a November 2026 cover and 114 pages. Some direct fetches return a stale June 2023 copy; references below should be checked against the current linked copy. The guide is FM9-family evidence; the FM9 downloads page lists v12.0 for Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo by name, and installed firmware is unknown.

**Fractal Volume/Pan utility audit and implementation (2026-11-25):** The current November 2026 Blocks Guide, printed p. 109, names Type (Volume/Auto-Swell), Volume, Auto-Swell Threshold/Attack/Release/Hysteresis, Balance, seven Taper choices, Input Select (STEREO/LEFT ONLY/RIGHT ONLY), Pan Left, Pan Right, Bypass Mode (pass/mute), and Output Level independently of Volume. It publishes no numeric ranges/defaults or taper/pan laws. TabletFX already has a project-authored Utility row at ID 93: Volume position 0…1 (default 1), configurable min/max endpoints within −96…+12 dB (defaults −96/0), Linear/Log 30A/20A/15A/10A/5A/S-Taper approximations, Balance ±100%, Stereo/Left/Right selection, independent L/R pans ±100%, Output Level −24…+12 dB (default 0), and pass/mute bypass. Auto-Swell remains a separate effect; no physical expression-controller assignment is included. Mono applies shared Volume/Output gain only; for more than two channels, the first pair receives stereo routing and the remainder shared gain. All routing laws and ranges/defaults are project choices, not Fractal parity. The Blocks Guide is FM9-family evidence; firmware v12.0 lists Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo by name, and installed firmware is unknown. Focused native/browser local-guitar Play, Utility ID 93 mapping inspection, and Windows MinGW build pass. At 48 kHz stereo/128 frames on Ryzen 9 5950X, the 80 B effect adds zero latency and uses 0.0180/0.0180/0.0382/0.3428/1.2195% p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max of the 2.667 ms callback. Human listening and hardware checks remain open. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 Downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) · [FM9 Mark II Turbo](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit).

This is a staged comparison of documented model-level gaps, not a fresh audit of every model in every catalog. `[x]` means the project implementation has passed the named local checks; it does not claim manufacturer DSP or sound parity. `[~]` means related project coverage exists but model-specific controls or behavior remain open. `[ ]` is a distinct implementation or source-verification gap.

### Category map and remaining implementation queue

This is a category-oriented roadmap, not a claim that each similarly named project effect reproduces a manufacturer's model. The source boundaries matter: some documents give exact control names, some only list categories, and seller/product pages are not control manuals.

Next exact-model gaps, checked against the cited documents:
- [x] Line 6 Helix Stadium XL — global TAP reset for the existing categorized Tremolo and Rotary Speaker. Native tests confirm phase-only restart while preserving smoothing and rotor history; browser/WASM verifies the button-to-chain path with the local DI test suite; Windows MinGW build passes. This is not tempo entry, does not add an effect row, and does not yet target every project LFO processor. Per-press handler CPU is not separately benchmarked; it adds no processor state or sample-loop processing. Manual listening and target-device checks remain open.
- [x] Line 6 Helix Stadium XL — Modulation / Double Take. Implemented at categorized Modulation index 102 with native tests, a 103-row local-guitar browser Play smoke, full browser/native comparison, and Windows MinGW core build passing. Project defaults are 2 doubles, Slop 0.25, Sensitivity 0.55, Dry 0.85, Wet 0.15, Mono Source; measured stereo state is 9,504 B, the dry path is immediate, and one isolated desktop run used at most 1.6336% of a 2.667 ms callback deadline. Legacy Helix designer notes supply control-role evidence, not a current Stadium parameter table. Project DSP/settings are not Line 6 parity; human listening and device checks remain open.
- [x] Fractal FM9 Mark II Turbo — Pitch / Octave Divider. The November 2026 FM9-family guide (printed p. 84) documents fixed −1/−2 octave voices with independent Pan/Level and recommends single notes, usually before distortion. The project-authored divider is implemented at Pitch index 101 and passes native tests, the local-guitar browser Play smoke, browser/native comparison, and Windows core build. Project ranges/DSP are not Fractal settings or sound parity. Exact Mark II Turbo firmware applicability remains qualified; manual listening and device tests remain open.
- [x] Fender Tone Master Pro — Equalizer / EQ5 Graphic. Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A, printed p. 61, documents five faders centered at 80/240/750/2,200/6,600 Hz plus overall Gain under Dynamics + EQ. TabletFX adds a separately named project-authored five-band peaking stage at Equalizer index 103. Defaults are flat with ±12 dB project bounds and Q=1; the guide publishes no numeric ranges/defaults, bandwidth, topology, latency, or DSP. `make test`, the local prerecorded Guitar-TECHS DI browser Play smoke, browser/native comparison, and Windows MinGW core build pass. One Ryzen 9 5950X Release run at 48 kHz stereo/128 frames measured p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.1054/0.1227/0.3221/0.8700/1.1606% of the 2.667 ms callback deadline and 224 B state. v1.7.53 names a separate EQ-5 Parametric and menu separation but does not confirm current EQ5 Graphic placement; Equalizer is the project taxonomy. These are not Fender DSP/settings/sound parity. Human listening and hardware checks remain open.
- [x] Fractal FM9 family — Modulation / Optical Trem 1, Optical Trem 2, and Neon Trem. The November 2026 family guide, printed pp. 106–107, describes photocell Optical variants (1 more even; 2 choppier) and a separate neon-bulb/LDR Neon Trem with fixed LFO. Shared rate/tempo, depth, ten LFO names, duty/shape/phase, start phase, trigger threshold, and ducking controls are documented; numeric bounds/defaults and exact response curves are not. TabletFX implements the three variants in one Modulation row at picker index 104. Its linear Optical 1 curve, more abrupt Optical 2 curve, fixed-width Neon pulse, project defaults, controls, and DSP are independent interpretations, not Fractal parity. Native/CLI, local prerecorded DI browser Play/parity, and Windows checks pass. The isolated processor uses 216 B, has zero fixed latency/no process allocation, and measured p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.0743/0.0746/0.1204/0.2895/0.3638% of a 2.667 ms callback on a Ryzen 9 5950X. The FM9 firmware page names Original/Turbo, not Mark II Turbo; exact SKU applicability and installed firmware remain unknown. Human listening and hardware checks remain open.

The current November 2026 guide, p. 30, gives Frequency 40–800 Hz (400 Hz–8 kHz with ×10) and no default. | Project-authored Stereo Crossover is integrated at index 86 in a separate Crossover picker category with native/CLI/browser/WASM paths. Its 20–2,000 Hz base frequency, −60…+12 dB levels, −1…+1 pan/balance and LR4-style biquad topology are project choices. Automated checks establish implementation plumbing, not Fractal DSP/settings parity. The current guide covers the FM9 family; firmware v12.0 lists Original and Turbo but does not name Mark II Turbo, so exact applicability remains qualified. Listening and hardware checks remain open. |

November 2026 Blocks Guide, pp. 106–107; width above 100% is described as psychoacoustic widening, with no numeric bounds/defaults. | Project-authored Panner is at Modulation index 90. It implements project-defined LFO wave mappings and tempo divisions, 0–200% width, phase, center, ducking, level/balance and smoothing. Native checks, the 93-row picker, WASM, full stereo browser/native comparison and isolated local-guitar Play smoke pass. Its 64 MiB fixed WASM memory, project DSP, ranges and defaults are not Fractal settings/parity. Classic retains pre-5.1 device behavior; HeadRush describes Mod as improving engaged/bypassed and repeat level consistency and making Feedback more linear with a wider usable range. | The existing project BBD-style Delay now has Classic and Mod choices in the Delay controls.
