No XR1 DSP, timing, or device parity is claimed; the physical send/return path and other model-level controls remain unverified. |

### Source revisions and boundaries

- [Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10146/OM_2274900000_Tone-Master-Pro-Model-Guide-H_EN.pdf) and [current firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates). The guide is a dated control snapshot; current firmware is 1.8.85 (2026-11-18), and the installed firmware is unknown.
- [Helix Stadium model list](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/), [1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes), and [current effects manual](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks). Latest checked hardware firmware is 1.4.2; manuals/releases name control roles but generally do not give model-specific ranges/defaults.
- [Moog MF-105M manual](https://api.moogmusic.com/sites/default/files/2018-06/MF-105M_MIDI_MuRF_Manual.pdf) supports the Asheville Pattrn architecture comparison; it does not publish a Line 6 parameter table.
- [Fractal Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf), current linked PDF has a November 2026 cover date; the entries below cite that guide unless a different dated source is named; [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/); [FM9 Mark II Turbo product page](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit).
- [HeadRush downloads and Prime User Guide 5.2](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads) and [5.1 firmware notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/51/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.1.0.pdf). The separate [HeadRush effect/control list](https://support.inmusicstore.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000801099-headrush-effect-and-control-list) explicitly covers Pedalboard, Gigboard, and MX5, so it is not used as evidence for Prime parameters.
- [Melofty downloads](https://www.meloftyaudio.com/support/downloads), [Axom Max V1.7.4 manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf), and [developer app release notes](https://apps.apple.com/pl/app/melofty/id6762972347?platform=mac).
- [XRecer XR1 product page](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1), [FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq), and [launch article](https://www.xrecer.com/blogs/article/xrecer-unveils-xr1-a-new-era-of-multi-effects). The linked manual portal is [wiki.xrecer.com](https://wiki.xrecer.com/), whose manual contents were unavailable in the checked reader.

Picker audit: `make inspect-effect-picker` lists every category row and verifies contiguous indices and that each row category is represented by the UI filter. The current picker has 93 rows, including Stereo Crossover in the separate Crossover category at index 86, Asheville Pattrn under Filters & wah at index 87, Rock Box Dist and Rock Box Boost under Drive & fuzz at indices 88–89, and Stereo Panner under Modulation at index 90; the effect catalog continues to grow as discrete model gaps are implemented.

**Current Three-Stage Distortion checkpoint (2026-11-23):** Line 6 Helix Stadium firmware 1.4 notes introduced Mercator Dist and document Drive, Drive 2, active Bass/Mid/Treble, Bright 1/2, and Level. TabletFX now has a project-authored Drive-category three-stage soft-clipping processor at index 80. The stage order follows the documented controls, but all ranges/defaults, tone frequencies, clipping curves, bright-boost responses, and smoothing are project-authored. `make test` passes 86/86 CTest cases plus 11 fixture-safety checks; `make web-test` passes the 83-entry categorized picker, 70-active-stage browser/native comparison, and a separate local-guitar Play smoke at index 80 (peak 0.793493 / 47,999 active samples). The general comparison disables this project-only processor because its offline reference has no matching stage. This is not Line 6 DSP/settings/sound parity; listening and hardware checks remain open. [Helix Stadium 1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes).

**Current Axom filtered-delay checkpoint (2026-11-23):** The existing project-authored Filtered Delay remains in the categorized Delay picker and now has free millisecond timing or tempo sync (20–300 BPM, whole/dotted/triplet divisions through 1/64, including 1/32 and 1/64). The 350 ms free-time setting is retained separately when tempo sync is selected. Its one-pole low/high-cut repeat filters and direct dry path are unchanged. `make test` passed 84/84 CTest cases plus fixture checks; `make web-test` passed the 81-entry categorized picker, Filtered Delay control self-check, local guitar Play capture, and 70-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 0.000013969; RMS error 0.000002686). Melofty's official MELOFTY app V1.9.2 notes mention Delay divisions from 1/64 to 1/1 and High/Low Cut but do not identify which delay model receives these features or state numeric ranges. The added controls and behavior are project-authored; no Axom-specific model, settings, or DSP parity, device check, or listening result is claimed.

**Current Quad Tape checkpoint (2026-11-23):** The categorized Delay entry at slot 72 now includes a sixth, project-authored four-head topology. The official Fractal Blocks Guide PDF is dated June 2023; it documents Quad Tape Motor Speed, states 1.000 is normal, and says changing it alters delay time and bends echo pitch while moving, but gives no Quad Tape numeric range. TabletFX uses a project-selected 0.8–1.2× range, linearly interpolated read heads, and a slew capped by the speed change. make test passes 82/82 CTest cases and 11 fixture checks, including timing, proportional pitch-bend, clamping, and allocation tests. make web-test passes all picker/control checks, the 70-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 0.0000144169; RMS error 0.00000276921), and local generated-guitar Play smokes for all five non-parallel topologies; Quad Tape captured peak 0.238366 / 47,999 active samples. Multi-Delay is absent from the offline renderer and remains excluded from numeric parity. No Fractal DSP/settings parity, human listening, prerecorded-user recording regression, hardware route, or isolated CPU result is established.

**Published Fractal Multi-Delay time-LFO checkpoint (2026-11-24):** The [June 2023 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf), pp. 66 and 111, documents two delay-time LFOs with Type, Phase, Rate/Tempo, and Depth, and ten waveform types. It specifies the four-tap phase mapping (Tap 2 follows Phase; Taps 3 and 4 are each 180° from Taps 1 and 2); 90° yields 0°, 90°, 180°, and 270°. The guide does not publish numeric rate/depth ranges or defaults; its waveform reference says phase has no effect on Random. TabletFX adds two project-authored LFO panels to the existing Delay-category Multi-Delay row at slot 72: ten shapes, free rate 0.05–20 Hz or project tempo divisions, phase 0–180° (default 90°), and depth 0–20 ms (default 0). Tempo-sync divisions, project ranges/defaults, random generation, and summing simultaneous LFO contributions are project choices; no Fractal DSP/settings parity is claimed. Native tests pass (101/101 CTest cases and 11 fixture checks); `make web-build` succeeds and `make web-test` passes the 98-row picker, 72-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 6.683e-6; RMS error 1.218e-6), and local prerecorded-guitar playback. The focused browser smoke exercised all five topologies with both LFOs; fixed WASM state was 32,899,836 B within the 64 MiB heap. Human listening, isolated CPU, hardware, and device-firmware checks remain open.

**Prerecorded guitar check (2026-11-23):** `make audio-regression` passed and now includes a separate EGFxSet bridge-pickup render with the project-authored Three-Stage Distortion enabled: 240,000 frames at 48 kHz, peak 0.000592346 and RMS 0.000108918. This verifies bounded finite rendering through the existing offline chain. The output is very quiet and does not establish useful level, musical quality, listening results, or Line 6 behavior.

## Evidence rules

- **Documented model/category** means a manufacturer page or guide names a category or effect. It does not by itself verify every parameter, firmware availability, audio quality, or simultaneous use.
- **Parameter evidence** is listed only where a model-specific guide or manual gives controls. Broad controls such as Mix, Level, and Time are not assumed to exist on every algorithm.
- **Unknown** means the available source did not establish the detail; it does not mean the device lacks it.
- Firmware notes identify model additions or changes. A model guide from an older firmware is not a complete current catalogue.
- Captures, amp/cab/IR assets, external loops, routing utilities, tuners, and MIDI/instrument engines are kept distinct from ordinary audio-effect algorithms.

## Source-backed comparison by model

### Melofty Axom Max

**Evidence:** The Melofty-branded Axom Max User Manual is V1.7.4, dated 2026-08-31. Melofty's official [downloads page](https://www.meloftyaudio.com/support/downloads) now lists Axom Max firmware, Axom Controller, and MELOFTY app V1.9.2, each dated 2026-10-27; this does not establish the firmware installed on a particular unit or revise the manual's effect tables. The current official firmware page offers no readable per-effect changelog. [User Manual V1.7.4](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf), [MELOFTY app release history](https://apps.apple.com/sr/app/melofty/id6762972347), [manufacturer-linked product overview](https://shop.warwick.de/en/Blog/MELOFTY-Axom-Max)

- **Effect browsing / resources:** The V1.7.4 manual describes 19 effect modules, two A/B chains with nine slots each, dynamic CPU limits, serial, parallel, split/merge, A/B-to-Y, and Y-to-A/B routing (pp. 11–12, 17). It advertises 880+ “total effect models,” while distributor product copy also says 130+ effect models; neither count has a sufficiently clear scope to reconcile them. Melofty's official downloads page lists firmware, Axom Controller, and app V1.9.2 dated 2026-10-27; this does not establish a unit's installed version or update the V1.7.4 guide's effect tables. [Manufacturer downloads](https://www.meloftyaudio.com/support/downloads)
- **Category labels / evidence limits:** A readable quick-guide transcription lists 20 labels: `INST.SIM`, `SYNTH`, `OD`, `DYNAMIC`, `FILTER`, `AMP&CAB`, `AMP`, `CAB`, `DUAL CAB`, `LOADER`, `MOD`, `FX`, `EQ`, `PITCH`, `DELAY`, `SPECIAL DELAY`, `REVERB`, `HQ REVERB`, `LEVEL`, and `FX LOOP`. This is secondary evidence, not a verified V1.9.2 on-device menu; it also mixes effect families with amp/cab, loader, and routing labels. The discrepancy between the manual's 19 effect modules and these 20 labels remains unresolved. The accessible manufacturer material does not supply a complete per-algorithm name list or parameter tables/ranges for those blocks. Do not invent Axom-specific model names or controls. [Secondary quick-guide transcription](https://manuals.plus/melofty/sft40-axom-max-hybrid-audio-processor)
- **Other functions:** The manual describes 70+ instrument tones and 50+ DeepSynth sounds as system banks usable with standard guitar input; it does not establish guitar-note polyphony, tracking range/latency, or named algorithms. Delay/modulation support BPM sync. The official MELOFTY app V1.9.2 release notes list Delay divisions from 1/64 to 1/1 with High/Low Cut; reduced Pitch CPU use; MFIM AMP/ENTIRE Sharpness; an added Simplified Noise Gate; improved Wah audio; Synth/Inst Subpatch model switching and dynamic response; long-press module copy/paste; and tuner UI/bass-tuning changes. They give no filter ranges or effect parameter tables, and app notes do not establish the behavior of a particular device's installed firmware. The manual also documents a global input Noise Gate, global output EQ, tuner, FX Send/Return/Loop, and a stereo phrase looper with reverse/half-speed and up to 3,600 seconds. MFIM amp/cab captures and cabinet assets are capture/modeling resources, not ordinary effect algorithms or a complete guitar synth inventory.
- **Checklist result:** broad effect families and selected global/looper behavior are documented, but full effect-model names, per-effect parameters, synth voice configuration/tracking/polyphony, and installed device firmware remain unverified. The Phrase Looper now has a project-authored threshold-triggered Auto Record control based on the manual's standby workflow; its numeric threshold range/default are project choices, not Melofty values. The manual's 19-module count, third-party quick-guide's 20 visible category labels, 880+ total, and separate 130+ product claim remain unresolved scope differences.

### Fender Tone Master Pro

**Evidence and scope:** Fender's official Model Guide is explicitly for firmware **v1.1.100**. The current firmware page lists **v1.8.87 (2026-11-23)**; its note fixes a Virtual Capo pitch issue, while the previous v1.8.85 entry is dated 2026-11-18. The guide was not revised to cover later additions. The guide mostly supplies control names/descriptions, not numeric ranges/defaults; do not invent bounds. Later firmware notes name additions but usually omit their per-model parameters. [Product page](https://www.fender.com/products/tone-master-pro), [Model Guide PDF (v1.1.100)](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf), [firmware and Pro Control release notes](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates), [v1.8.45 effects overview](https://www.fender.com/articles/instruments/the-latest-tone-master-pro-update-is-here).

#### Model Guide v1.1.100 — ordinary effect models and documented controls

- **Stompbox — boost, overdrive, distortion, fuzz:** Boost and Ranger Boost (Level); Blues Maker (Gain, Tone, Volume, Version); Royal Tone (Gain, Tone, Presence, Volume, Mode); Enchanter (Gain, Bass, Treble, Volume, Boost); Greenbox (Level, Overdrive, Tone, Blend); Mythic Drive (Gain, Treble, Output); Nashville Overdrive (Drive, Level, Spectrum, Bass Cut); Tube OD (Drive, Level, High, Low, Bias, Tube switch); Orangebox (Distortion, Tone, Level); Hard-Clip Distortion (Distortion, Output); Maximus Drive (Gain, Resonance, Level, Bass, Treble, Presence, Mid Level/Frequency, Boost Level/Switch); Pugilist Distortion (Gain A/Tone A, Gain B/Tone B, Level, Blend, Mode, Bass Boost); Rodent (Distortion, Filter, Volume); Big Green and Big Horn Fuzz (Sustain, Level, Tone, Blend, Mids); Round Fuzz Germanium/Silicon (Fuzz, Volume); Vari Fuzz (Gain, Level, six-way Filter); Octave Fuzz (Boost, Volume, Octave switch); Octobot (Direct, octave-down, octave-up-fuzz/Sizzle levels).
- **Modulation:** Tube Bias Tremolo (Speed, Tap Division, Intensity, Level); Harmonic Tremolo (Speed, Intensity, Shape, Tap Division, 1960/1963 Mode); Optical Tremolo (Speed, Intensity); Tremolo (Rate, Intensity, Duty Cycle, Tap Division, Shape, LFO Clip); JC Chorus (mono/stereo output mode); JC Vibrato (Rate, Tap Division, Depth, mono/stereo output mode); Vibratone (Drive, Tone, Level, Fast/Slow, Speed switch, Ramp, Sensitivity, Dynamics); Uni-Verse Vibe (Speed, Intensity, Volume, Tap Division, Vintage/Modern and Chorus/Vibrato modes); Rotary Speaker 122/147 (Drive, Tone, Level, Fast/Slow, Speed switch, Ramp, Sensitivity, Dynamics); Flanger Mono (Rate, Depth, Resonance, Tap Division, Manual); Flanger Stereo adds L/R Phase; ’70s Flanger (Speed, Width, Regen, Tap Division, Manual); Electric Flanger (Rate, Depth, Color, Tap Division, Flanger/Filter mode); Phaser 90 (Speed, Tap Division); Phaser (Rate, Depth, Resonance, Tap Division, four Modes, Stages); Chorus Mono (Rate, Depth, Mix, Tap Division, Low/High Cut); Chorus Stereo adds Width; 3D Chorus (four-mode selector); Triple-Double Chorus (Preset/Manual switches, Rate, L/C/R Depth, Feedback, Sparkle, Stereo); Orbit Stereo Panner (Rate, Depth, Tap Division, Square/Sine/Triangle type).
- **Delay:** Auto-Swell Delay (Time, Feedback, Mix, Tap Division, Swell, Mod, Tone, Sense, three Taper choices, Dry switch, Spillover); Digital Delay Mono (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Tone, Spillover); Dynamic Delay (Time, Feedback, Mix, Tap Division, Low/High Cut, Threshold, Attenuation, Release, Mod Rate/Depth/Mode); Echo Filter Mono (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Filter Speed/Depth, Q, Filter Mix, Spillover), with Echo Filter Stereo adding Offset/Spread; Memory Delay Mono (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Depth, Gain, Grit, Chorus/Vibrato Mode, Spillover), with Stereo Memory Delay adding separate left/right time and tap divisions; Ping Pong Delay (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Offset, Spread, High Cut, Spillover); Reverse Delay (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, High/Low Cut, Forward Level, Spillover); Space Delay (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Record Level, Delay Mode, Bass/Treble Level, Tape Noise, Wow and Flutter, Spillover), with Stereo Space Delay adding three head-pan controls; Tape Echo (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Record Level, Tape Bias, Tape Noise, Wow and Flutter, Spillover), with Stereo Tape Echo adding separate left/right time/tap controls; Studio Delay (Time, Tap Division, Feedback, Mix, Low/High Cut, Mod Rate/Depth/Mode, Pan Rate/Depth/Mode, Spillover). Dynamic Delay modulation modes are Sine, Triangle, Random and Triggered; the Studio Delay guide describes Sine/Triangle/Random/Triggered modulation and Sine/Triangle/Envelope panning. Firmware v1.6.34 extended certain delay ranges: Digital, Echo Filter, Stereo Echo Filter and Ping Pong to 1–2,000 ms; Dynamic and Studio to 30–2,000 ms.
- **Reverb:** ’63 Spring and ’63 Spring Convolution (Mixer, Dwell, Tone, Spillover); ’65 Spring (Reverb, Tone, Spillover) and ’65 Spring Convolution (Reverb, Spillover); Large/Small Hall (Mix, Predelay, Decay, Damping, Diffuse, Hall Size, High/Low EQ, Mod Depth, Dispersion, Spillover); Large Room (Mix, Predelay, Decay, Damping, Diffuse, Room Size, High/Low EQ, Dispersion, Spillover); Large Plate (Mix, Decay, Diffuse, High/Low Damp, High/Low Tone, Balance, Spillover); Modulated Large/Small Hall (Level, Decay, Dwell, Diffuse, Rate, Depth, High/Low Cut, Spillover); Cloud (Mix, Tone, Decay, Diffuse, High/Low Damp, Rate, Depth, Spillover); Celestial (Mix, Decay, Pitch 1/2, Pitch Decay, High Cut, Blend, Spillover); Nebula (Mix, Size, Diffuse, Pitch, Pitch Decay, Mod, High/Low Cut, Spillover); Shimmer (Mix, Decay, Damping, Diffuse, Feedback, High Pass Filter, Spillover). The guide says only one convolution reverb, including convolution reverb built into an amp, can be used at once.
- **Celestial Reverb evidence boundary:** Model Guide v1.1.100 lists Mix, Decay, Pitch 1, Pitch 2, Pitch Decay, High Cut, Blend, and Spillover, but no numeric ranges, defaults, or DSP topology. The current firmware page lists v1.8.87 (2026-11-23); those notes do not identify Celestial changes. The feature list is documentation evidence, not evidence of an individual unit's installed firmware.
- **Dynamics + EQ:** Dynamic Compressor (Output, Sensitivity); Pedal Comp (Level, Tone, Attack, Comp); Simple Compressor (Type); Studio Compressor (Gain, Threshold, Ratio, Attack, Release, Knee, Blend); Sustain (Sensitivity, Output); Metal Gate and Noise Gate (Threshold, Attenuation); Simple Gate (Type); Volume Pedal (Volume); Auto Swell (Time, Sense, fast/slow Taper); EQ3 Parametric (Bass, Mid, Mid Freq, Mid Q, Treble, Gain); EQ5 Graphic (80/240/750/2.2k/6.6k Hz bands, Gain); EQ7 Graphic (100/200/400/800/1.6k/3.2k/6.4k Hz bands, Gain).
- **Filters + Pitch:** Custom Wah (Position, five Range choices, Q, five Inductor choices); Teardrop Wah and Vocal Wah (Position); Chromatic Pitch Shifter (Mix, Pitch, Delay, Tap Division, Feedback, Tone, Spillover; guide states −2 to +2 octaves in cents); Diatonic Harmonizer (Mix, Pitch, Key, Tone, Scale, Delay, Feedback, Tap Division, Spillover); Feedback Generator (Attack, Level, Type); Pedal Detune (Shallow/Deep Mode, Position); Pedal Shifter (10 Pitch intervals, Position). Fender says the Chromatic Pitch Shifter and Diatonic Harmonizer typically work best with single notes; this is not evidence of reliable chord tracking.

#### Later firmware — names, category evidence, and limits

Fender's rolling notes identify the following additions; for entries not documented in the v1.1.100 guide, detailed controls/ranges remain **unknown**. Where notes give only an undivided “Effect Models” list, grouping below follows the model names and is not a claim about the on-device menu taxonomy.

- **v1.3.74 (2024-06-03):** drive/fuzz/phase/filter names Super Drive, Rocket Man, Big Apple Fuzz, Little Rock Phaser, Filtron and Enigma Filter; delay names Analog Delay and EchoTank; reverb name Reverse Nebula Reverb.
- **v1.4.71 (2024-11-20):** Greenbox 10, Mythic Drive II, Bass Overdrive, Stereo Harmonic Tremolo, Glooper (Glitch Looper), EQ-7B, EQ-7W, EQ-10 Stereo and Virtual Capo. Glooper is a named effect; do not conflate it with the separate global looper/footswitch functions.
- **v1.6.34 (2025-05-14):** Comic Sans Drive; Analog Chorus, Stereo Analog Chorus, Analog Bass Chorus, Analog Vibrato, Stereo Doubler; Aurora Delay, Stereo Aurora Delay, Polyhedron Pitch Delay; Arctic/Antarctic Sustainer; Small Room/Small Plate and Ascension/Imperium Convolution Hall, Kinetic Convolution Plate, Atmosphere Convolution Room reverbs; Slow Attack; Micro Shifter, Polygon Octave Shifter, PolyVoice Pitch Shifter and Granular Arpeggiator. Fender limits convolution reverb use to one per preset, including amp convolution reverbs.
- **v1.7.53 (2025-12-03):** Stompbox additions SDD-Boost, XO Boost, Sapphire Drive, Namaste Drive; Stereo Digital Delay, Digital Hold Delay/Stereo Digital Hold Delay, Echelon Delay/Stereo Echelon Delay and Prime Delay/Stereo Prime Delay; Vast Convolution Chamber, Ethereal/Retrograde Convolution Hall and Alkaline Convolution Plate; EQ-5 Parametric EQ, Low/High Cut, High Cut, Low Cut and Notch Filter; Aethon, Cerberus and Wavemorph Polysynths. Fender states it separated Dynamics, EQ, Filter and Pitch menus and added a Synth menu.
- **v1.8.45 (2026-06-03):** Fender explicitly categorizes Lightyear Overdrive, Pinions Overdrive, Runes Bass Overdrive, Integrator Boost and Grunt Boost, Rockbox 100; Step Tremolo; Step Filter Delay and Prismatic Delay; Cirrostratus Reverb, Cirrostratus Synthverb and Spectral Reverb; Seventy Sixer Compressor; Step Filter; Pitch Sequencer. Fender describes Grunt as a single-knob boost up to +20 dB; controls/ranges for the other new effects remain unknown. [Update article](https://www.fender.com/articles/instruments/the-latest-tone-master-pro-update-is-here). The article describes Step Filter as rhythmic sweeps/pulses/morphs and Pitch Sequencer as pitch-bending sequences, glides, glitches and freeze-like behavior, but gives no model-specific parameter tables or ranges.
- **v1.8.85 (2026-11-18):** Lightyear Overdrive is renamed Greer Amps Lightspeed. Virtual Capo has a rebuilt polyphonic pitch-shifting algorithm across ±12 semitones; Fender says it removed Resolution and Tracking controls. These release notes do not publish other control ranges. Installed firmware on a particular unit is not established by the public release page.

#### Project comparison

The earlier **66-entry** picker snapshot is categorized as Utility, Dynamics, Filters & wah, Drive & fuzz, Modulation, Equalizer, Delay, Pitch, Synth, Resonator, Loop & performance, Stereo & image, and Reverb. Its Equalizer category contains the existing three-band peaking EQ, a project-authored five-band peaking EQ, graphic EQs, and shelf/tilt EQ. The five-band stage adds adjustable per-band frequency, gain, and Q; its supported filter type is peaking only. The rest of the picker has broad function-family coverage for drive/fuzz, compression/gating/swell, filters/wah, modulation including project-authored Harmonic Tremolo, delays, pitch shifting, a project-authored monophonic Diatonic Harmonizer and Pitch Arpeggiator, a project-authored monophonic guitar-tracked oscillator synth, a separate project-authored free-running Note Generator, and the bounded Feedback Generator, room/hall/plate/spring reverbs, a local user-IR convolver, project-authored pitch-fed Shimmer Reverb, and a project-authored one-layer phrase looper, and a project-authored Stereo Enhancer with Modern, Classic, and Stereoizer modes. The looper is capped at 192,000 frames (4 s at 48 kHz), with no quantization, multi-layer recording, or loop-file/preset saving; this is not parity with the longer model-specific loopers below. Grain Delay, Pitch Feedback Delay, and Resonant Delay are available in Delay with project-authored controls; Resonant Delay does not claim Prime RESO DELAY topology or parameter parity; neither claims HeadRush parameter or DSP parity. Pitch Feedback Delay cumulatively shifts repeats and uses a polyphonic pitch shifter internally; the latest 48 kHz stereo/128-frame benchmark measures 11.5218% p50 / 14.4177% p95 (20.5892% maximum) of a 2.667 ms callback deadline and owns 2,798,168 B at its 6 s capacity. Feedback Generator is a separate, limited feature: default Unison mode measures 11.5271% p50 / 15.0623% p95 (37.0735% maximum) and owns 1,522,968 B; octave modes invoke a pitch shifter and have not been separately benchmarked. The 66-entry picker passes its categorized browser checks, including the Stereo & image category and Enhancer mode control. The sample-aligned browser/native comparison bypasses Enhancer; native and offline CLI tests exercise its processing. The 63-active-processor sample-aligned browser/native Play comparison leaves the free-running Note Generator disabled, then a separate browser check measures its A4 output at 440 Hz (RMS 0.4525); its free-running phase is checked by frequency and level rather than cross-build sample phase. The local IR remains loaded during browser validation; the latest 63-processor prerecorded regression passed across five pickup positions and stereo/mono DI using a deterministic locally generated IR because the offline renderer has no looper integration. Browser/native error is within the 0.0002 maximum-error and 0.00002 RMS-error tolerances, but output remains extremely quiet. The harmonizer exposes project-authored Mix, scale-degree interval, key, scale, tone, voice delay, feedback, tap division, and tempo controls. Its STFT shifter has about 5.33 ms nominal stream latency at 48 kHz; the 1,024-sample note-analysis frame makes first-note control response about 26.7 ms before host/browser buffering, above the current 20 ms response target. It tracks one note at a time and does not implement Tone Master Pro's Spillover bypass switch. The project-authored arpeggiator adds a 16-step sequencer, but currently supports only ±7 scale degrees because it shares the tracker interval clamp; Fractal's ±24 range remains open. The synth offers sine, triangle, saw, and fixed square waveforms plus Mix, Level, Gate Threshold, Attack, Release, and Portamento controls. Its tracker is limited to approximately 55–1,100 Hz with a 1,024-sample analysis frame (about 21.3 ms at 48 kHz); it has no pulse-width control and does not establish polyphonic input tracking. The latest 48 kHz stereo/128-frame synthetic benchmark measured synth CPU at 11.4150% p50 / 12.1140% p95 (17.4219% maximum) and 1,024,760 B owned memory. These processor measurements do not establish live device performance or sound quality. None of these project-authored processors claims manufacturer tracking or sound parity. The six-model scope is a focused comparison, not a fresh audit of every catalogue entry; the feature gaps are checked in the comparison table above.

Modulation includes a project-authored Dimension Chorus using Line 6 Stadium’s documented Version C/D and ten Mode labels; its delay/phase profiles and DSP are not a Dimension W/DC-2W emulation. It also includes a project-authored Triple-Double Chorus with Preset Switch, Manual Switch, Rate, L/C/R Depth, Feedback, Sparkle and Stereo Switch controls named in Fender’s Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100. The guide gives no numeric ranges or DSP details, so the implementation’s ranges, profiles, routing, and sound are project-authored and do not establish Fender parity. Drive & fuzz includes a project-authored Bit Crusher with signed 2…24-bit quantization and per-channel sample hold; its sample-rate reduction intentionally aliases and has no selectable anti-alias filter. The Formant Filter retains its generic A/E/I/O/U sweep and adds project-authored Start/Middle/End selections from Fractal’s ten documented vowel labels plus manual Control morphing; frequency profiles/ranges are not published by Fractal, automated modifier routing remains absent, and DSP/settings parity is not claimed. The existing Harmonic Resonator is an eight-mode resonant filter bank; the four-band Resonator is a project-authored feedback-delay bank with Manual/Chord modes and separate per-band filters. These processors provide category coverage only: none establishes named-model DSP/settings parity, and neither resonator is a synth, pitch-formant processor, or automatic humanizer. A project-authored mono-tracked Pitch Arpeggiator is in the Pitch category with Trigger Threshold and 16 steps; its audible range remains ±7 degrees pending a separate wider-range tracker. The Synth category now contains a project-authored monophonic guitar-tracked oscillator with fixed sine/triangle/saw/square choices, not the flagship models’ broader synth or instrument engines. A project-authored one-layer phrase looper is in Loop & performance, capped at four seconds at 48 kHz, and now offers optional project-authored peak-threshold Auto Record (−90…0 dBFS; −36 default) based on Axom Max's documented standby behavior; the manual publishes no numeric threshold values and no Melofty settings/DSP parity is claimed. Quantization, multi-layer recording, loop-file/preset saving, and configurable looper routing remain unimplemented. Dedicated multioscillator and polyphonic guitar-synth engines, audio-driven synth voices, sampled instrument tones, instrument simulations, pedal-shifter/detune, and commercial convolution-reverb models remain unimplemented. Step Filter, Rhythmic Slicer, Auto Swell, generic pitch shifter and Sweep Delay are related tools, not verified equivalents to Fender Step Filter/Tremolo/Pitch Sequencer, Auto-Swell Delay, Virtual Capo, or the newer delay/reverb models. Do not infer their tracking, latency, texture, parameter ranges, or simultaneous-use limits from manufacturer documentation.

**Earlier implementation snapshot (2026-11-22):** The categorized picker now has 75 entries. Project-authored Multi-Delay appears in Delay at slot 72 with four lines, millisecond/tempo timing, self/cyclic cross-feedback, per-line level/pan, and a 512 KiB storage ceiling. This is partial function coverage; the other FM9 Multi-Delay variants, Plex, Rhythm Tap, and Fractal DSP/settings parity remain unimplemented. This six-model comparison remains a focused audit, not a fresh audit of every catalogue entry.
**Later checkpoint (2026-11-22):** The browser picker now has 77 entries. Project-authored Pitch Sequence is slot 74 under Pitch. It applies programmed semitone steps, with separate Pitch, Glitch, and sample-loop Freeze modes. Fender's firmware history introduces Pitch Sequencer in v1.8.45, records a potential-crash fix for the step-effects group in v1.8.58, and lists v1.8.85 as the current checked firmware. The sources describe the broad behavior but provide no Pitch Sequencer control table, ranges, or DSP. TabletFX values and algorithms remain project-authored; the feature is not Fender parity. This remains a six-model focused audit, not a fresh audit of every catalogue entry.

**Current Rhythm Tap checkpoint (2026-11-22):** The picker now has 78 entries. Rhythm Tap Delay is slot 75 in Delay. `make test` passes 81/81 CTest cases plus 11 fixture-safety checks, and `make web-test` passes the category checks, 70-stage browser/native comparison, and local generated guitar-like fixture Play smoke through Rhythm Tap (peak 0.7162358; 46,079 active samples). It is a project-authored control/DSP path; the offline CLI has no matching stage, so the parity comparison disables it. Direct Fractal sound/control parity, listening, isolated CPU, and device behavior remain open.

**Current Plex Matrix Delay checkpoint (2026-11-22):** The picker now has 79 entries, with project-authored Plex Matrix Delay at slot 76 in Delay. `make test` passes 82/82 CTest cases plus 11 fixture-safety checks; `make web-browser-test` passes the 79-entry category/control checks and 70-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 0.0000147457; RMS error 0.00000286683). A separate local guitar fixture Play smoke captured peak 0.4904613 / 46,079 active samples. The official Fractal Blocks Guide PDF is dated June 2023; its Plex Delay section documents matrix size, decay, diffusion, cuts, predelay, Stack/Hold, master controls, up to eight time/tempo/level/pan controls, wet filter and LFO, envelope follower, ducker, Mix-page controls and spillover. TabletFX implements a project-authored matrix core and a subset of those controls. Stereo Spread, wet filter/LFO, envelope, ducker, Mix-page Bypass/Bypass Mode/Input Select/Kill Dry/Global Mix, and spillover remain gaps. Plex Delay itself has no pitch effects; Plex Detune and Plex Shift are separate types. The FM9 downloads page lists v12.0 for Original/Turbo, not separately for Mark II Turbo, so applicability remains a family inference. No Fractal DSP/settings/preset/tail parity, isolated CPU, human listening, or hardware result is claimed.

**Current Plex advanced-controls checkpoint (2026-11-23):** The 90-entry picker keeps Plex Matrix Delay categorized under Delay at effect index 76 (displayed row 77). Native/browser validation covers a selected subset of documented FM9-family controls: wet Filter Type/Frequency/Q/Gain and LFO Type/Rate/Mod Frequency; input envelope Threshold/Attack/Release controlling send; Ducker Attenuation/Threshold/Release; Stereo Spread; Input Select (LEFT, RIGHT, or L+R default); and Kill Dry. The current manufacturer-linked Blocks Guide is dated June 2023; p. 90 describes separate input `Diffusion Mix` and `Diffusion Time` controls that smear transients before the Plex matrix. TabletFX includes a project-authored three-stage input diffuser with Mix 0–100% (default 0%) and Time 0.1–50 ms (default 10 ms). It is independent of matrix cross-feedback `Diffusion`; its topology, ranges, and defaults are not vendor values. The guide's p. 4 describes Kill Dry as removing the direct signal while leaving the effect settings active; TabletFX's project switch does this while retaining the Mix-scaled wet output. TabletFX exposes all three Input Select choices in the existing Delay controls: L+R keeps the earlier stereo average, Left/Right choose the wet source, envelope/ducker detection follows the selected source, and the dry path remains stereo when Kill Dry is off; these routing details are project-authored. The wet filter, detector mapping, ducker attack/timing, stereo math, Kill Dry behavior, numeric ranges, and defaults are project-authored. `make test` passes 93/93 CTest plus 11 fixture checks. `make web-build` and `make web-browser-test` pass; the latter reports a 71-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 0.0000134999; RMS error 0.0000027541), Plex browser smoke with Kill Dry enabled, stereo Right-input Plex native/WASM comparison (zero max/RMS error), and local generated-guitar Play capture. It confirms browser integration only; Plex is omitted from full-chain numeric parity because the offline reference has no matching stage. The 24 MiB WASM heap uses 21,964,340 B processor state plus 10,364 B tuner state. The matrix/pre-delay rings remain capped at 1 MiB; the input diffuser has a separate fixed 256 KiB cap. Six other documented Filter-block types, delay-line modulation, Plex Detune/Shift/Verb and Shimmer Verb, Mix-page Bypass/Bypass Mode/Global Mix, Plex spillover, vendor DSP/settings/preset/tail parity, isolated CPU, and hardware listening remain open. FM9 guide firmware applicability to Mark II Turbo remains an inference; installed firmware is unknown.

**Current Quad-Tap topology checkpoint (2026-11-22):** The existing project-authored Multi-Delay now also offers independent Quad-Tap-like feedback lines, per-line Level/Pan, cross-feedback disabled, and Master Time/Feedback scaling. The official June 2023 Fractal Blocks Guide describes Quad-Tap as four independent taps and documents shared master controls, but gives few numeric bounds. `make test` passed 82/82 CTest cases plus 11 fixture-safety checks. `make web-test` passed the 79-entry categorized picker, 70-stage browser/native comparison (maximum absolute error 0.0000147457; RMS error 0.00000286683), and local generated guitar fixture Play smokes: Quad Series peak 0.3384107 / 48,000 active samples, Diffusor 0.2539146 / 47,999, Quad-Tap 0.4415284 / 47,999, and Quad Tap Band 0.4435770 / 47,997. Numerical parity excludes Multi-Delay because the offline renderer does not implement it. This is functional topology coverage only; no Fractal DSP/settings parity, isolated CPU, listening, or device result is claimed. Quad Tape, the documented two-second Diffusor, and remaining processing pages are still open.

**Current Quad Tap Band checkpoint (2026-11-22):** Added a fifth project-authored Multi-Delay topology that retains independent tap Time/Tempo/Feedback/Level/Pan and applies the project shared Low/High Cut filters after the feedback loop, reflecting the documented Quad Tap Band placement distinction. It does not implement Fractal’s per-tap BPFs or claim its DSP. Native coverage verifies the external-filter feedback path; the browser smoke captures output from local generated guitar audio. `make test` and `make web-test` pass. Quad Tape and the other Multi-Delay processing pages remain open.

#### Octobot implementation checkpoint (2026-11-23)

Fender's Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 describes Octobot as a combination of octave-down and octave-up fuzz. Its controls are Direct (dry level), Down (octave-down level), and Sizzle (octave-up fuzz level); the guide does not publish numeric ranges, defaults, or DSP topology. The guide lists the separate Octave Fuzz model with Boost, Volume, and an Octave switch, so it should not be conflated with Octobot. The latest checked Fender firmware release is v1.8.85 (2026-11-18); its notes do not mention Octobot. The installed firmware on the user's device is unknown. [Tone Master Pro Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf), [Fender firmware updates](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-gb/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates).

TabletFX now has a project-authored `octobot` entry under Drive & fuzz at index 82 in the 85-entry picker. Direct, Down, and Sizzle use project-selected defaults 0.65/0.20/0.25 and normalized 0–1 bounds; Down is a −12-semitone pitch-shift branch, while Sizzle is a +12-semitone branch followed by a fixed tanh shaper. The unshifted branch is delayed to align with the project's 256-sample pitch-shifter latency (5.333 ms at 48 kHz). These values, shaper, topology, and latency behavior describe this implementation, not Fender's undocumented DSP or settings. Native tests cover branch separation and alignment; browser/WASM controls and local-guitar Play capture pass. The new stage is disabled in browser/native numerical parity because the offline reference has no matching processor. No human listening, hardware test, or Fender sound/control parity is claimed.

### Cross-model implementation checklist

**Earlier browser picker checkpoint (2026-11-22):** 74 categorized entries, including project-authored Megatap Delay at slot 68 under Delay, De-Esser at slot 69 under Vocal FX, Dual-Engine Distortion at slot 70 under Drive & fuzz, and Vibe at slot 71 under Modulation. `make test` passed 78/78 CTest cases and `make web-test` passed the 74-entry picker, control self-check, browser/native comparison, and local-guitar Play smoke for Vibe (peak 0.4336884; 47,998 active samples). The Vibe stage is excluded from native/browser parity because the offline renderer has no matching processor. Prime's official product page confirms De-Esser's Vocal FX listing and HeadRush Original attribution but gives no model controls/ranges. The project has a project-authored De-Esser, so the Prime comparison is partial coverage rather than an unimplemented category; its controls and DSP are not Prime parity. Fender's Model Guide v1.1.100 p. 27 documents Pugilist Distortion control concepts; project controls, ranges and processing remain independent because numeric bounds/defaults and DSP details are absent and current firmware applicability is unverified. Line 6 Helix Stadium v1.4 release notes list Essex Vibe controls Rate, Depth, Trem, Stages, stereo Spread, and Level, with no numeric ranges/defaults/topology. TabletFX now has a project-authored Vibe under Modulation using those control names and independent ranges/topology. `DLY-14` remains a project-authored onset-sensitive delay with Time, Feedback, Mix, Tap Division, Swell, Mod, Tone, Sense, Slow/Normal/Fast Taper, Dry, and Spillover controls. These names follow Fender’s Model Guide v1.1.100; numeric ranges and signal behavior remain project choices, with no Fender parity claim.

**Pitch-sequence verification:** The updated 77-entry picker test passes category/control checks; HeadlessChrome 151 captured Pitch, Glitch, and Freeze output from prerecorded local guitar audio at slot 74. The 70-stage browser/native comparison leaves this new stage disabled because the offline renderer has no matching processor. This verifies the UI-to-WASM path, not pitch quality, Fender sound, physical hardware, or end-to-end latency.

This checklist summarizes the six source-backed model sections above; it does not claim a fresh audit of every model or every TabletFX processor.

It operates on polyphonic audio without note detection or scale quantization; the freeze method, controls, ranges, and DSP are project choices. Category/function coverage only; firmware-specific behavior and Fender parity remain unknown. |

The two swept all-pass stages, amplitude modulation, defaults, and ranges are project choices. It does not claim Line 6 DSP, settings, or sound parity; listening and hardware checks remain open. |

See the model-specific EQ comparison below; do not count global EQ as a chain effect. |

Modern modifies side energy, Classic delays the right channel, and Stereoizer uses a compact all-pass-derived side signal. The browser host now duplicates mono input and runs Modern in stereo; local guitar playback confirms distinct L/R output. The Pedal Shifter mode reuses the wet-only global shifter at Pitch picker index 22; its slider does not receive physical controller input and its continuous ±24-semitone target is not Fender's undocumented ten-interval list. The tracker offers ±7 scale degrees; Fractal documents ±24. The harmonizer/arpeggiator first-note response is ~26.7 ms at 48 kHz. The Arpeggiator costs 15.4107% p50 / 17.4398% p95 of one Linux 128-frame callback in isolation, so low-latency/live suitability needs optimization and device testing. Fine detune, faster tracking, expression hardware routing, and Virtual Capo parity remain open; Dual/Quad Harmony adds generated voices from one monophonic tracker, not chord tracking; do not treat polyphonic transposition as polyphonic tracking. |
| Synth and instrument simulation | Axom Max labels Synth/Inst.Sim and DeepSynth system tones; FM9 and Stadium name oscillator synths; Tone Master Pro firmware adds Aethon, Cerberus, and Wavemorph Polysynth; Prime lists Hybrid Synth and instrument tones; XR1's announced synth belongs to planned firmware, not the checked OS 1.0 list. | **[~] Partial.** The Synth picker contains a monophonic tracked oscillator, a free-running Note Generator, and a three-voice tracked synth. The latter has per-voice waveform, tracking, frequency/shift/tune, filter, attack, level, and pan controls; its ranges and DSP are project-authored. All three tracked voices share a monophonic detector limited here to about 55–1,100 Hz, not polyphonic input-note tracking. Sampled instrument-tone engines and Prime/Stadium named sources remain absent. Tests and local-guitar browser playback are automated evidence, not listening, hardware, or flagship parity. |
| Resonator and formant | FM9 documents a four-band Manual/Chord Resonator and a Formant block with Start/Middle/End vowel selections, Resonance, and Control morph. | **[x] Related project coverage.** The project has manual generic and three-vowel Formant modes, an eight-mode harmonic resonator, and a four-band feedback-delay resonator. Formant spectra/ranges remain project-authored; automatic modifier/LFO assignment, humanizer/formant-on-pitch behavior, and exact Fractal DSP/settings parity remain absent. |
| Feedback generation | Helix Stadium lists a mono Feedbacker; its Line 6 software-family description documents pitch/harmonic modes, Attack/Release, dry handling, reference, thresholds, Retrigger, and Trails, but no numeric ranges. | **[~] Partial, project-authored only.** `feedback-generator` is a single-note-tracked sine sustain with −12, 0, +12, +19, +24, +28, +31, and +35 semitone choices; these generate one oscillator voice and do not claim stacked-voice or chord-tracking behavior. Attack, Release, Generated Level, Dry Kill Off/On, Dry Level, and Silence Threshold remain project-authored controls. It omits Mid to Low, High to Low, Random Onset/Trigger, Reference, Onset/Offset thresholds, Dry Kill Always, Retrigger, and Trails. The fixed chain places this stage late, unlike Line 6's early-placement recommendation. The measured monophonic tracker response bound is 21.333 ms at 48 kHz, and desktop isolated CPU ranges from 10.677% p50 to 57.810% worst maximum of a 2.667 ms callback across modes; no Stadium DSP, tracking, settings, or sound match is claimed. |
| Delay and special/time effects | Prime lists Grain Delay and other pitch/rhythmic delay families; Fender documents model-specific delays and later step/pitch effects; Axom Max documents a stereo Phrase Looper with Reverse and Half-Speed; FM9-family documentation includes Megatap, Multi-Delay, Plex, and Ten-Tap. | **[~] Partial.** Project-authored Digital, Reverse, Ducking, Modulated, Filtered, Swept, Grain, Pitch Feedback, Resonant, Auto-Swell, Ten-Tap, Rhythm Tap, and Megatap Delay processors are present. Auto-Swell Delay uses Fender control names with project bounds/topology; Ten-Tap and Megatap implement related documented control concepts with project-authored behavior, not Fractal parity. Grain Delay and Pitch Feedback Delay remain unverified Prime algorithms/control matches. Four-line Multi-Delay now offers limited Quad-Parallel-like cross-feedback, Quad-Series-like cumulative taps, a four-stage cascaded all-pass Diffusor-like topology, independent Quad-Tap/Quad Tap Band-like lines, and a four-head Quad-Tape-like mode with shared Motor Speed. In the tape mode, a project-selected 0.8–1.2× control scales delay spacing and slews interpolated read heads to bend stored echoes; Fractal documents 1.000 as normal speed but gives no numeric Quad Tape range. Per-tap tape coloration, advanced BPF/comb/ring-mod/chorus pages, Diffusor stereo spread/filters/LFO/envelope, and Fractal behavior/settings parity remain gaps; Plex is tracked separately. Phrase-Looper quantization, multi-layer recording, and persistent loop files remain gaps; the separate Shuffling Looper now has a project-authored XR1-inspired Beat option, Off by default, but this is not XR1 settings or parity. The project Phrase Looper also offers project-authored Auto Record based on Axom's documented threshold standby, but this is not Reverse Delay or vendor parity. |
| Reverb | Fender, Stadium, FM9, Prime and Axom Max document several room/hall/plate/spring/ambient or convolution families, with per-model behavior differing. | **[~] Partial.** Generic project-authored room, hall, plate, spring, and user-IR convolution algorithms exist. The convolver exposes project-authored wet filters, Wet Level, Mix, and per-block Tails; it accepts local mono/stereo WAV IRs up to 10 seconds at 48 kHz. Its 21.33 ms partition latency, measured callback outliers, unsupported AIF/OPUS formats, arbitrary multichannel import, vendor DSP, and rig-switch behavior remain distinct gaps. |
| Instrument simulation, looper and routing | Axom Max documents Inst.Sim, phrase looper and split/merge/A-B/Y routes; Stadium, Tone Master Pro, Prime and XR1 list loop/routing functions separately from ordinary effects. | **[~] Partial.** A one-layer, four-second-at-48-kHz Phrase Looper and a separate capacity-selectable Shuffling Looper are in Loop & performance. The Shuffling Looper has project-authored Off/Beat recording alignment; Phrase Looper quantization, multi-layer capture, saved loop files, configurable pre/post placement, and vendor-specific looper behavior remain absent. Instrument simulations, captures/IRs, hardware routing, and amp/cab models are not counted as ordinary effect algorithms. |

Status: **[x]** category coverage verified in this project, **[~]** partial/related/experimental coverage, **[ ]** not implemented, and **Unknown** where the reviewed primary source does not settle availability or controls. The model-specific details and links remain in each section below.

### Priority implementation checklist

This is the working checklist for the six-model comparison, not a claim that every model feature has been freshly audited or that similarly named project processors match commercial algorithms.

| Status | Picker category | Manufacturer feature and source scope | TabletFX state and next step |
