### Looper and performance comparison (checked 2026-11-22)

Loopers are documented as either dedicated blocks or global performance utilities. Recording time, overdub model, persistence, and switching behavior are product-specific; those values are not inherited by TabletFX.

| Exact model and source scope | Documented looper behavior | Parameter/control evidence and boundary |
|---|---|---|
| **Melofty Axom Max** — User Manual V1.7.4 (2026-08-31) | Stereo phrase looper up to 3,600 seconds, with Undo/Redo, Reverse, Half-Speed, and Auto Record. Page 32 says enable Auto Record, adjust a trigger threshold, enter standby with FS 6, and recording starts when the playing signal reaches the threshold; with Auto Record disabled, FS 6 starts recording immediately. | The reviewed manual does not establish a per-layer count or threshold range/default. [Manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) |
| **Fender Tone Master Pro** — firmware history; latest checked v1.8.85 (2026-11-18) | The v1.7 update article says it extended the looper from 60 seconds to 3 minutes. The v1.4.71 notes list EZ Looper, Play/Stop, Record/Overdub, Reverse, 1/2 Speed, and Undo footswitch assignments and explicitly identify them as Global Looper controls, not a new effect block. | Fender's current product page still advertises 60-second stereo recording, so current installed firmware and effective duration remain source-qualified. The firmware notes do not publish a parameter table or numeric ranges. [v1.7 article](https://www.fender.com/articles/parts-and-accessories/tone-master-pros-latest-firmware-update-is-one) · [firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates) · [product page](https://www.fender.com/products/tone-master-pro) |
| **Line 6 Helix Stadium XL** — manual selector identifies Rev. E / firmware v1.4.1; linked looper/effect subpages still show Rev D headers | Deluxe, 1 Switch, and Shuffling Loopers are mono/stereo options; one looper block per preset. Deluxe/1 Switch allow 120 s mono or 60 s stereo at full speed (double at half speed); Shuffling allows 60 s mono or 30 s stereo and has no half-speed mode. Switching presets stops the loop unless the next preset has the same type/channel configuration at the same path. Loops are not saved with presets and clear on power-off. The model list identifies loopers as hardware-only, unavailable in Stadium Native. | Deluxe lists Playback, relative Overdub level, Low Cut, and High Cut; footswitch functions include Clear, Undo/Redo, Speed, Reverse, Record/Overdub, and Play/Stop. Shuffling exposes Slices, SeqLength, Shuffle, Pitch, Reverse, Repeat, Smoothing, Seq Drift, Playback, Low/High Cut, and Interval 1/2. Shuffle, Pitch, Reverse, Repeat, and Seq Drift are likelihood controls; most numeric bounds are not published. [Manual selector](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live) · [Looper Block](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/looper-block) · [Effect blocks](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) · [Model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/) |
| **Fractal FM9 Mark II Turbo** — June 2023 family Blocks Guide; exact Mark II Turbo applicability not separately stated | The FM9 comparison lists one stereo Looper block, up to 120 seconds, with undo. | The guide documents Playback/Dry/Overdub levels, Overdub Decay, Crossfade, Loop Quantize, Record Threshold, Record 2nd Press, speed, Low/High Cut, Balance, and bypass behavior. This family guide is not proof of a particular unit's installed firmware. [Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) |
| **HeadRush Prime** — current official FAQ and User Guide | Up to 50 layers, 5 minutes per layer, and 20 minutes total; one loop at a time. Peel removes layers in reverse order; Insert overwrites a selected part. | The reviewed FAQ does not specify its exact firmware applicability or a complete looper parameter table. [FAQ](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000834532-headrush-prime-frequently-asked-questions) · [User Guide](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/HeadRush/prime/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v1.0.pdf) |
| **XRecer XR1** — current official FAQ | One Looper or Simple Looper block per preset; up to four minutes per loop, overdub, quantize, and up to eight saved Loop Files. | The FAQ gives no exact firmware revision for this looper behavior and no numeric range for its Quantize control. [FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq) |
| **TabletFX Phrase Looper** — project-authored core and browser/WASM picker | Mono/stereo, one layer, 192,000-frame cap (4 s at 48 kHz), one undo/redo snapshot, reverse and half-speed playback. | Record/Play/Overdub/Stop/Undo/Redo/Clear, Reverse, Half-Speed, Overdub Input (0–100%), and optional Auto Record with project-authored −90…0 dBFS peak threshold (−36 dBFS default). While stopped, a rising per-frame input peak crossing starts a replacement and includes the crossing frame; the previous loop remains undoable. This does not claim Axom behavior/settings parity. No quantization, multi-layer overdubs, loop-file/preset saving, or configurable placement. CTest and browser smoke verify threshold capture from the local guitar fixture; replacement start swaps buffers in constant time, while existing overdub/clear snapshots remain unprofiled for glitch-free operation. No device/listening evidence is claimed. |
| **TabletFX Shuffling Looper** — project-authored Helix Stadium-inspired category entry | Mono/stereo, replaces its previous loop, defaults to 4 s at 48 kHz, with selectable capacity up to 60 s mono / 30 s stereo at 48 kHz. Old plus replacement sample buffers remain within 16 MiB during capacity changes; current state reports 11,521,440 B at either maximum, and transition peaks are 12,288,000 B mono / 13,056,000 B stereo. No undo snapshot or persistence. | Explicit Record, Stop recording + play, Play, Stop, Randomize, and Clear buttons; controls include slices, sequence length, shuffle/pitch/reverse/repeat probabilities, smoothing, sequence drift, playback level, low/high cuts, and two pitch intervals. Native, local-guitar browser/WASM, and Windows checks pass. On Ryzen 9 5950X Release at 48 kHz / 128 frames, maximum-capacity playback p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max is 0.0881/0.1444/0.1519/0.3559/0.4920% mono and 0.1245/0.1256/0.2070/0.3836/0.4436% stereo, with 0/5000 callback deadline misses. Timings are desktop-only. Capacity and status behavior are project-authored; this does not reproduce Stadium's single-switch gestures, exact bounds/DSP, or save behavior. |

This is a focused comparison of documented looper behavior for the six selected units, not a fresh audit of the complete effect catalogues. `PRC-05` through `PRC-07` in [`IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md`](IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md) track the remaining project looper work.

### Parametric and global EQ comparison

| Model | Manufacturer-documented EQ path and controls | Evidence boundary / TabletFX gap |
|---|---|---|
| MELOFTY Axom Max | Manual V1.7.4 (2026-08-31) describes separately enabled global EQ for unbalanced, balanced, and headphone outputs. Its 10-band EQ appears in the MFIM tone-capture fitting workflow. | The manual does not expose the global EQ band controls/ranges or establish a 10-band chain effect. TabletFX has no per-output global EQ. [Manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) |
| Fender Tone Master Pro | Firmware v1.7.53 (2025-12-03) added EQ-5 Parametric EQ; v1.8.85 (2026-11-18) is the latest listed firmware and fixes the EQ-5 Band 5 label. | Firmware notes establish the model and category, not EQ-5 control names/ranges. The v1.1.100 Model Guide predates EQ-5. [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates) · [Model Guide v1.1.100](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) |
| Line 6 Helix Stadium XL | The current Stadium manual says the legacy Parametric [5-band] was replaced by a new seven-band Parametric EQ. The model catalogue lists the mono/stereo Parametric family as first appearing in firmware 1.0; the manual describes improved peak Qs and more precise low/high-cut slopes. | The reviewed manual/catalogue do not provide the seven bands' individual numeric limits or full control table. Do not transfer old Helix values. [Manual compatibility note](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/rev-d-v1.3/presets) · [Model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/) |
| Fractal FM9 Mk II Turbo | The June 2023 family Blocks Guide documents a five-band Parametric EQ. Frequency ranges: band 1 20–2,000 Hz; bands 2–4 100–10,000 Hz; band 5 200–20,000 Hz. Q is 0.1–10 and gain is −20…+20 dB. Bands 1/5 allow Shelving, Shelving 2, Peaking, Peaking 2, or Blocking; bands 2/4 allow Peaking and the two Shelf/Peaking 2 variants; band 3 allows Peaking or Peaking 2. Blocking slope is 6–36 dB/octave. | The guide covers the FM9 family and is not an FM9 Mk II Turbo firmware release manual. Its PEQ also documents band solo and block Level, Balance, Bypass, and Bypass Mode, which TabletFX does not implement. [Blocks Guide, June 2023](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 firmware page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) |
| HeadRush Prime | Prime's product page lists PARA EQ and identifies it as inspired by Avid Custom Parametric EQ. Its Prime v5.0.0 User Guide separately documents a four-band Global EQ with EQ On, output Level, Low/High shelf-or-cut choices, and Low, Low Mid, High Mid, and High bands with frequency, gain, and quality/Q controls. | The reviewed Prime-specific sources do not provide the Para EQ block's control table/ranges or the Global EQ numeric limits. Do not substitute Global EQ settings for the effect block or use the detailed Pedalboard/Gigboard/MX5 effect list. [Prime product page](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/) · [Prime User Guide v5.0.0](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) |
| XRecer XR1 | The FAQ says its effects library has EQ blocks and documents a five-band Global EQ for selected outputs; dry DI channels bypass that global EQ. | The FAQ does not give its five bands' controls/ranges or establish that the Global EQ is a chain PEQ. [XR1 FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq) |

TabletFX's five-band EQ is a project-authored processor with per-band Frequency, Gain, Q, and a constrained shape selector. Bands 1/5 have Peaking plus their corresponding shelf and outer cut; bands 2/4 have Peaking plus their corresponding shelf; band 3 is Peaking-only. Project cut slopes are 6–36 dB/octave; the shelf/cut implementations, bounds, defaults, and DSP are not Fractal settings or parity. The June 2023 Fractal Audio Blocks Guide (checked 2026-11-23) is the family reference and documents Shelving, Shelving 2, Peaking, Peaking 2, and Blocking in the bands described above, plus band solo and Level/Balance/Bypass/Bypass Mode. Exact FM9 Mark II Turbo installed firmware remains unknown: the FM9 v12.0 download page names Original/Turbo. TabletFX does not implement Peaking 2/Shelving 2, band solo, those Mix-page controls, or output-specific global EQ. The vendor comparison documents controls and does not copy its settings. See [`EFFECTS_MATRIX.md`](EFFECTS_MATRIX.md) and [`IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md`](IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md).

### Line 6 Helix Stadium XL

**Evidence:** The current online owner manual is Rev. E for firmware v1.4.1; separate release notes say Stadium hardware v1.4.2 was released 2026-10-27. The model catalogue was checked 2026-11-22 and identifies mono/stereo/legacy entries and their initial-appearance firmware. Stadium XL and Stadium share effect blocks, while XL adds Instrument 1+2 input choices. [Manual selector](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium), [current effect-block reference](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks), [v1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes), [current model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/).

- **Categories:** Distortion, Delay, Reverb, Modulation, Dynamics, EQ, Pitch/Synth, Wah/Filter, and Volume/Pan; Looper is separate. Amp/preamp, cab/IR, clones, FX loop, split/merge, and input/output blocks are separately identifiable and should not be counted as stompbox effects.
- **Drive / dynamics / EQ / modulation:** The current catalogue includes boost, overdrive, distortion, and fuzz models, plus compressors, gates, autoswell, Feedbacker, modulation, EQ, and wah/filter categories. Specific model availability must follow each catalogue entry's initial-appearance version.
- **Feedbacker:** Stadium's current model catalogue lists Feedbacker as a mono Line 6 Original feedback generator. The Stadium manual's shared effects page does not provide a Feedbacker-specific parameter table. The official Line 6 software model page describes Fdbk Gain and Fdbk Type; types are −Octave, Unison, +Octave, Oct +5th, +2 Octaves, 2 Oct+3rd, 2 Oct+5th, 2 Oct+7th, Mid to Low, High to Low, Rndm Onset, and Rndm Trigger. It also lists Attack, Release, Dry Kill (Off/On/Always), Dry Level, Reference (Lowest/Loudest), Silence Thr, Onset Thr, Offset Thr, momentary Retrigger, and Trails. Numeric ranges are not published there. That page documents the Helix software model family; exact controls/ranges and firmware applicability to current Stadium XL remain unverified. It says the effect loads bypassed, works best early in the chain, and may overwhelm the input at high gain.
- **Delay / reverb:** The current model catalogue names Dynamic Hall, Dynamic Plate, Dynamic Room, Dynamic Ambience, Dynamic Bloom, Shimmer, Hot Springs, Nonlinear, Glitz, Ganymede, Searchlights, Plateaux, Double Tank, and legacy Plate/Room/Chamber/Hall/Echo/Tile/Cave/Ducking/Octo/Spring/Particle Verb. Current manual revisions present Reverb as a category spanning room, plate, hall, chamber, spring, shimmer, and nonlinear effects; the catalogue specifically lists Hot Springs, ’63 Spring, and Spring, but the reviewed Stadium material has no per-model spring parameter sheet. the shared effect-parameter table includes Decay, Predelay, Mix, Level, Low Cut, High Cut, and Trails where an individual block supports them. That shared table is not a per-model Dynamic Hall/Plate parameter list, so exact model controls remain block-specific. Helix Stadium's [official catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/) lists Dynamic Hall in mono/stereo; the [current effect-block documentation](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium) gives common controls rather than Dynamic Hall internals.
- **Pitch / synth distinction:** The catalogue separates 3 OSC Synth, Poly Pitch/Wham/Capo, Pitch Wham and other tracked pitch effects, 3 Note Generator, and 4 OSC Generator. Do not infer input-note polyphony from oscillator or generated-voice counts. The manual lists common parameter families such as Drive/Gain, Bass/Mid/Treble, Rate/Speed, Depth, Spread, Headroom, Low/High Cut, and Sidechain; they are not controls on every algorithm.
- **New-model parameter evidence:** v1.4 release notes give controls (but no numeric ranges) for six additions: Mercator (Drive, Drive 2, Bass, Mid, Treble, Bright 1/2, Level); Rock Box Dist (Drive, Tone, Level); Rock Box Boost (Boost, Level); PortaBox 424 (Gain 1/2, Bass, Treble, Level); Essex Vibe (Rate, Depth, Trem, Stages, stereo Spread, Level); Dimension W (Version, Mode, Mix, Level). Stadium's public manuals generally rely on in-device parameter descriptions for other algorithms.
- **TabletFX implementation:** `Dimension Chorus` is available under Modulation with Version C/D and all documented single/pair Mode choices. Mix is 0–100%; output Level is −24…+12 dB as a project-authored bound because the release notes do not publish its numerical range. Internal modulation profiles and DSP are project-authored, with no Line 6/BOSS parity claim.
- **TabletFX Note Generator (2026-11-22):** A separate Synth-category, project-authored source provides one to three fixed oscillators with selectable waveform, static MIDI note, octave shift, per-voice level, and output level. It does not track guitar or accept MIDI input, and oscillator count does not establish polyphonic input tracking. The [Line 6 Stadium drone tutorial](https://blog.line6.com/2026/08/17/perfect-practice-with-helix-stadium-part-2-creating-drones-and-harmony-beds/) shows the 3 Note Generator with input set to None and controls for Osc Shape, Note, Octave, and Level; it gives no numeric bounds. TabletFX ranges and DSP are project choices, not Line 6 settings or algorithm parity. Native tests and a browser A4 frequency/level check pass; cross-build sample-phase parity is not claimed.
- **Stepped filtering:** The current model catalogue lists Asheville Pattrn, Seeker, and Obi Wah among filter models, but no effect named Step Filter or model-specific control tables for those entries. Similar rhythmic/filter behavior must not be treated as the same algorithm without documentation.
- **Shuffling Looper:** The current Stadium online block manual (firmware v1.4.1 / Rev. E) documents recording, sliced playback, sequence randomization, double-press stop, and press-and-hold clear. Its controls are Slices, SeqLength, Shuffle, Pitch, Reverse, Repeat, Smoothing, Seq Drift, Playback, Low Cut, High Cut, and Interval 1/2. The guide explicitly gives 0–100% for Shuffle, Reverse, Repeat, and Seq Drift and describes interval values from one octave down to one octave up; most other numeric bounds/defaults are not published. Firmware v1.4.2 release notes were also checked, but do not publish changes to these controls. [Looper Block](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/looper-block) · [v1.4 release notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes)
- **DSP caveat:** Rev D v1.3 allows only one effect from the combined Feedbacker / Poly Sustain / Poly Detune / Poly Pitch / Poly Wham / Poly Capo / 12 String group on a given path. This is a per-path limit, not a claim that only one may appear in a whole preset, and DSP still constrains combinations.
- **Checklist result:** strong category and model-name coverage; use the model catalogue's version column for current applicability and the block-specific inspector for exact controls.

### Fractal FM9 Mark II Turbo

**Guide source-date note (checked 2026-11-26):** The current PDF linked from FM9 Downloads has a November 2026 cover and 114 pages. A direct fetch can surface an older June 2023 copy; the official download-page link is the current revision. The guide provides family-level FM9 evidence; exact Mark II Turbo firmware applicability and installed firmware remain unknown.

**Evidence and scope:** Fractal's downloads page lists firmware **v12.0**, posted **July 7, 2026**, as compatible with “FM9 Original and FM9 Turbo.” That wording does not identify the **FM9 Mark II Turbo** separately. The Mark II Turbo product page describes the exact model and its FM9 effects library, but this audit preserves the firmware qualification instead of treating “Turbo” as explicit Mark II firmware confirmation. The current official Blocks Guide PDF is dated **June 2023** and covers family-level FM9 blocks; it is not v12 release documentation or a device-specific Mark II guide. The installed firmware on any particular unit is unknown. Sources: [FM9 Mark II Turbo product page](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit), [FM9 downloads and firmware compatibility](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/), [Fractal Audio Blocks Guide (June 2023)](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf), and [FM9 Owner's Manual, version 3.x (August 2022)](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/FM9/FM9-Owners-Manual.pdf).

This is a focused comparison to TabletFX's current **92-entry picker**, not a new audit of the whole TabletFX catalogue. “Related” below means that a stage shares a broad function. A shared label or effect family does not establish Fractal algorithm, model, or control parity. TabletFX has project-authored Asheville Pattrn, three-voice tracked synth, and four-band resonator stages informed by documented function/topology concepts; none claims manufacturer DSP or exact settings parity.

#### FM9 block inventory

**Guide source note (checked 2026-11-26):** The current Fractal Audio Blocks Guide linked from FM9 Downloads is the November 2026 PDF. The older June 2023 copy may be returned by direct fetches; confirm page references against the current linked source. Manual Thru-Zero Flanger types and Dry Delay controls still need a source-by-source check; numeric bounds remain unknown.

The guide's “Types” column counts variations inside a block. The instance column is the maximum number of that block in an FM9 preset; “not counted” means the table does not give a type total. Amp, Cab, and IR processing are excluded here. Mixer/routing/looper utilities are listed in the official guide but omitted from the audio-effect comparison.

| Block | Guide types | FM9 instances | TabletFX picker comparison |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Chorus | 27 | 2 | Chorus and Vibrato are related, not equivalent |
| Compressor | 19 | 2 | Pedal, studio, optical-style, and FET-style compressors are related, not equivalent |
| Crossover | 1 | 2 | Project-authored Stereo Crossover at picker index 86; documented control-role coverage only, not Fractal DSP/settings parity |
| Delay | 29 | 2 | Digital, Reverse, Ducking, Modulated, Filtered, and Sweep Delay are related, not equivalent |
| Drive | 87 | 3 | Boost, overdrive, distortion, and fuzz stages are related, not equivalent |
| Enhancer | 3 | 2 | Project-authored Stereo Enhancer provides related category coverage; it does not claim Fractal behavior or control parity |
| Filter | 20 | 4 | High-pass, Low-pass, Notch, Envelope Wah, and Step Filter are related, not equivalent |
| Flanger | 31 | 2 | Generic Flanger plus project-authored Through-Zero Flanger are categorized under Modulation; Off/Auto/Manual and the basic Auto LFO are implemented, while remaining Fractal types and controls (LFO filtering/quantize/reset, Auto Depth, VCO response, Tempo, and tone controls) stay open |
| Formant | 1 | 2 | No dedicated formant filter |
| Gate/Expander | 4 | 4 | Noise Gate is related, not equivalent |
| Graphic EQ | 18 | 4 | Guitar/Bass Graphic EQ are related, not equivalent |
| Megatap | 22 | 2 | No dedicated Megatap stage |
| Multi-Delay | 39 | 2 | Project-authored four-line Quad-Parallel-like, Quad-Series-like, four-stage Diffusor-like, and Quad-Tap/Quad Tap Band-like independent-delay subsets; Quad Tape, the guide's two-second diffuser DSP, advanced processing, and Fractal DSP/settings parity remain absent |
| Multiband Compressor | not counted | 2 | Project-authored three-band Peak/RMS compressor is related, not equivalent; it offers the documented crossover ranges and slope choices but does not implement Fractal's Auto Attack/Release or claim filter-topology/control parity. |
| Parametric EQ | five-band block; band types include peaking, shelving, and blocking | 4 | Project-authored three-band and five-band peaking EQs provide related coverage. The five-band block has project ranges, not Fractal band-type, solo, mix-page, parameter, or DSP parity. |
| Phaser | 19 | 2 | Phaser is related, not equivalent |
| Pitch Shift | 16 | 1 | Polyphonic Pitch Shifter is related, not equivalent |
| Plex Delay | 45 | 1 | No dedicated Plex matrix-delay stage |
| Resonator | not counted | 2 | Project-authored four-band Resonator exposes related Manual/Chord modes and band controls; its DSP/settings are not Fractal parity |
| Reverb | 79 | 2 | Room, Hall, Plate, and Spring are related, not equivalent |
| Ring Mod | not counted | 1 | Ring Modulator is related, not equivalent |
| Rotary | not counted | 2 | Rotary Speaker is related, not equivalent |
| Synth | not counted | 1 | Project-authored three-voice tracked synth is integrated under Synth with related oscillator/filter/track controls; one shared monophonic tracker means it is not polyphonic input tracking or Fractal DSP/settings parity. |
| Ten-Tap Delay | 2 | 2 | Project Ten-Tap covers common/tap controls, and a separate project-authored Rhythm Tap stage provides custom timing, Quantize, Learn, and feedback. Neither claims Fractal DSP/control parity. |
| Tremolo/Panner | 7 | 2 | Tremolo is related, not equivalent; no dedicated panner |
| Volume/Pan | 2 | 4 | Volume Pedal is related; no dedicated pan stage |
| Wah | 9 | 2 | Pedal Wah is related, not equivalent |

The FM9 column gives one **Pitch Shift** block and one **Synth** block. Its pitch-block table lists an Arpeggiator with one voice, 16 scale-aware steps, a ±24 scale-degree range, and no voice delay; the control page names Key, Scale, Run, Trigger Thresh, Steps, Repeats, Tempo, amplitude/pan Shape and Alpha, and each step's Shift. TabletFX has a project-authored Pitch Arpeggiator in its Pitch category with 16 steps and trigger/tempo/shape controls, but only ±7 scale degrees and an estimated 26.7 ms first-note response; it does not claim Fractal tracking or DSP parity. The June 2023 family guide does not publish a numeric Trigger Thresh range. It does not have the Axe-Fx III-only Dynamic Distortion or RTA blocks; the guide's product comparison shows zero FM9 instances for those, and also zero for IR Player. Vocoder is not listed as available on FM9. These are not counted as FM9 effect capabilities.

#### Important documented algorithms and controls

- **Drive and dynamics:** The Drive block has 87 types across boosts, overdrives, distortions, and fuzzes. Named examples include 77 Custom OD, Face Fuzz, Klone Chiron, Rat Distortion, T808 OD, Octave Distortion, and Bit Crusher. The guide documents model-dependent Drive/Gain/Distortion, Tone and Level controls; low/high cuts; EQ; and advanced clip type/shape, diode, bass-response, and dry-level controls. Compressor examples include VCA modern/classic, VCA FF/FB Sustainer, VCA Bus, Analog Compressor/Sustainer, Compander, Dynami-Comp Classic/Modern/Soft/Econo, Dynamics Processor, JFET Studio/Pedal, Optical, Vari-Mu Tube, Rockguy, and Citrus Juicer. Common controls include Threshold, Ratio/Compression, Attack/Release, Detector Type, Knee, Mix, and sidechain source/filtering. The four Gate/Expander types are Classic Expander/Gate and Modern Expander/Gate. Multiband Compressor is documented as a three-band compressor. The Drive `Bit Crusher` separately names `Bit Reduce` (bits subtracted from 24-bit full scale) and Sample Rate reduction, which intentionally aliases; the checked guide section supplies no numeric ranges for either control.
- **Filter and wah:** The 20 Filter types are All-Pass, Auto-Wah, Band-Pass, Envelope Filter, Feedback Comb, Feedforward Comb, High-Pass, High-Shelf, High-Shelf 2, Ladder Filter, Low-Pass, Low-Shelf, Low-Shelf 2, Notch, Null, Peaking, Peaking 2, Tilt EQ, Touch-Ladder, and Touch-Wah. General controls include Frequency, Order, Q, Gain (for shelf/peaking), Low/High Cut, pan and phase. Order is 2nd or 4th (12 or 24 dB/octave); All-Pass uses 1–12 stages; the comb types document 0–40 ms delay. Filter LFO controls include Enable, Type, Rate, Duty, Tempo, Mod Freq, and Quantize; Quantize can create stepped modulation, but the guide does not describe an editable per-step sequence. Envelope/Touch-Wah controls include LOWPASS/BANDPASS/HIGHPASS/MIX mode, Sensitivity, Attack/Release, Sweep Shape, Resonance and Start/Stop Freq (100 Hz–10 kHz). The nine Wah types are FAS Wah, Clyde, Cry Babe, VX846, Color-Tone, Funk, Mortal, VX845, and Paragon; controls include Wah Control, Control Taper, Resonance Q, Q Tracking, low cut, minimum/maximum frequency, Drive, Inductor Bias, and Fat.
- **Modulation:** Chorus types include Digital/Analog Mono/Stereo, Japan CE-2, Warm Stereo, 80's Style, Triangle, 8-Voice Stereo, Tape Chorus, Dimension 1/2/3, 4-/8-Voice Analog, Stereo Tri-Chorus, Dual Chorus, Tape Flanger, Japan CE-1 Chorus/Vibrato, Japan CH-1, MX234, Small Copy, Japan CE-2 Bass, Vibrato, Rockguy, and MX134 Stereo. Documented controls include voices, Rate/Tempo, Depth and Delay Time; minimum delay is 0.1–50 ms and Delay Range 1–40 ms. The 31 Flanger types include Analog/Digital Mono/Stereo, BBF-2, Binary Flange, Cancel Flange, D/AD 185, FAS Flanger, Manual Thru-Zero, Step Flanger, Stereo Jet, Thru-Zero, Vowel Flanger, Zero Flanger, and 80's Rack Flanger. Controls include Rate/Tempo, Depth, Feedback (−100 to +100), Manual (0–10), minimum/maximum time (0.340–20 ms), Thru-Zero, Dry Delay, Phase Reverse, LFO Type/Phase/Reset/High Cut, and LFO Quantize. The 19 Phaser types include Digital Mono/Stereo, Script 45/90, Block 90, Classic Vibe, Stereo 8-Stage, Barber Pole, FAS Vibe, Treadle Phaser, Virtuoso Phaser, Borg Phaser, and other Vibe/Phaser variants; controls include 2–12 stages, Rate/Tempo, Depth, Feedback, Manual, and sweep frequency limits. Tremolo/Panner types are Tremolo, Bias Trem, Harmonic Trem, Optical Trem 1/2, Neon Trem, and Panner; controls include Rate/Tempo, Depth/Width, waveform/duty/shape, phase, start phase, trigger threshold, and ducking. Rotary documents separate drum/rotor behavior, Rate/Tempo, Drive, Hi/Low Depth, rotor length, low-rate multiplier, acceleration time constants, mic spacing/distance, and stereo spread. The November 2026 guide lists Manual Thru-Zero Flanger as a type but does not publish that type's seeded values. Its Thru-Zero selector offers Off/Auto/Manual; Dry Delay shifts the cancellation point, and in Manual mode minimum Manual position marks zero. Exact Dry Delay bounds and type defaults are Unknown.
- **Pitch, synth, formant and resonator:** The 16 Pitch types are Dual Detune, Dual Detune Delay, Quad Detune, Dual Chromatic, Quad Chromatic, Quad Chromatic Delay, Dual/Quad Diatonic, Quad Diatonic Delay, Custom Shifter, Arpeggiator, Classic/Advanced Whammy, Octave Divider, Crystal Echoes, and Virtual Capo. The guide distinguishes fixed mono/polyphonic Chromatic shifting from **monophonic** intelligent Diatonic tracking. Documented examples include ±50-cent detune, ±24-semitone shifts, 0–250 ms or 0–2000 ms voice delays depending on type, a 16-step Arpeggiator, and formant correction/shift; the Pitch block normally sums its input to mono, while documented Detune and Shift types can use stereo input. Synth is a three-voice **monophonic** guitar-tracking synth; its three internal voices do not establish three-note input polyphony. Per-voice parameters include waveform (sine, triangle, square, sawtooth, random, white/pink noise, off), tracking mode, Frequency, Shift, Tune, Duty Cycle, Pan, filter frequency/Q, Attack, and voice Level. Formant selects Start/Middle/End vowels and Resonance, with Control to morph among them. Resonator offers Manual or Chordal mode, four resonant bands, and per-band Frequency, Feedback, Filter Q, Level, and Pan.
- **Delay and reverb:** The 29 ordinary Delay types include Digital/Analog Mono/Stereo, Mono/Stereo Tape, Ping-Pong, Dual, Reverse, Sweep, Ducking, Vintage Digital, 2290 w/ Modulation, Ambient Stereo, Deluxe/Stereo Mind Guy, Mono/Stereo BBD, Lo-Fi Tape, Diffused Delay, Graphite Copy, Worn Tape, Zephyr, DM-Two, Stereo Trem, Wandering, Pan, Dual Head Tape, and Surround Delay. FM9 delay time is documented up to 16 seconds. Reverse Delay documents Time, Run, Trigger Restart, and Crossfade Time. Ducking uses Ducker Atten, Threshold and Release. Sweep Delay is an LFO-controlled stereo band-pass output sweep. The other dedicated blocks are Megatap (22 types; 64 taps on FM9), Multi-Delay (39 types; core algorithms Quad-Tap, Quad Parallel, Quad Series, Quad Diffusor, Quad Tape, Quad Tap Band), Plex Delay (45 types; core Plex Delay, Plex Detune, Plex Shift, Plex Verb, Shimmer Verb, using up to eight delay lines), and Ten-Tap/Rhythm Tap (two types, 1–10 taps). Ten-Tap documents Delay Time/Tempo, Number of Taps, Decay, Shuffle, Spread, Ratio, Pan Shape/Alpha, Low/High Cut, and Tap 1–10 Level; tap level is −80 to +20 dB and tap count is 1–10. Rhythm Tap allows millisecond, quantized-division, or Learn-entered intervals. The guide gives no universal delay-time, tempo, decay, spacing, pan-alpha, cutoff, or rhythm-interval bounds/defaults, and its general Delay maximum must not be transferred to Ten-Tap. Reverb has 79 types grouped as Chamber, Cloud, Echo, Hall, Huge, Plate, Room, SFX, and Spring; controls include Time, Predelay, Size, frequency-dependent decay, diffusion, early/late levels, and modulation. Pitch shifting is available in some FM9 reverb types; it is explicitly absent from FM3 because of CPU limits.
- **EQ, crossover and stereo:** Graphic EQ has 18 types, with 10/8/7/5-band, passive, console, JMP-1, GE-7 and bass GE-7B variants; band gain/cut is up to ±12 dB. The five-band Parametric EQ supports Shelving, Shelving 2, Peaking, Peaking 2 and Blocking band types; documented ranges include Q 0.1–10, gain −20 to +20 dB, and slope 6–36 dB/octave for Blocking filters. Crossover is a two-way stereo fourth-order Linkwitz-Riley split, with frequency 40–800 Hz or 400 Hz–8 kHz using ×10; two blocks can form a three-way split. The Enhancer block has three types and lists two FM9 instances. The November 2026 guide says Level and Bypass are on Config for all types and the block has no Mix page. Modern uses frequency-based L/R separation with Width, Depth, Low Cut, and High Cut; the guide describes it as less prone to mono-summing phase issues and says it can convert mono to stereo. Classic delays the right channel with Width 0–20 ms, plus Phase Invert, Pan Left/Right, and Balance. Stereoizer uses multiple high-order filters; Depth sets band count and Width sets spread. Numeric ranges other than Classic delay are not published in the checked section. TabletFX has a project-authored Enhancer in Stereo & image: its Modern path is an M/S side-band processor, Classic delays the right side, and Stereoizer uses an all-pass-derived side signal. The standalone in-place mono processor passes mono unchanged; the browser host duplicates mono input and runs Modern in stereo, verified with local prerecorded guitar. It exposes project-defined controls and bounds and a Mix control absent from the documented Fractal block; no Fractal DSP or parameter parity is claimed. The November 2026 guide covers FM9-family products; firmware v12.0 is listed for FM9 Original/Turbo but the Mark II Turbo is not named separately, and installed firmware is unknown.

#### Resource, latency, and parity limits

The guide's FM9 instance counts are per preset, not a guarantee that every block can run at once. The FM9 uses a flexible grid, but available DSP and block-specific costs constrain a preset. The older v3.x Owner's Manual recommends about 80% CPU load; above the allowable limit, audio processing is muted. It notes that parameter choices also matter: Phaser stages, Filter Order/Q, Multi-Delay type, Plex delay count, and active Synth voices affect CPU; reverb quality settings trade CPU for smoother tails. Treat the 80% guidance as the 2022 manual's recommendation, not a verified v12-specific threshold. The current guide documents no general end-to-end latency figure; Compressor Look-Ahead Time is 0–2 ms and explicitly adds actual latency to both wet and dry output.

**TabletFX match status:** The picker has generic Dynamics; Filters & Wah; Drive & Fuzz; Modulation; Equalizer; Delay; Pitch; Resonator; Reverb; and Utility stages. Shared broad functions—such as Chorus, Flanger, Phaser, Tremolo, Rotary, Ring Modulator, Wah, EQ, pitch shift, Reverse/Ducking/Sweep Delay, and the four basic reverb families—are **related, not equivalent** to Fractal's named types and controls. A project-authored monophonic Diatonic Harmonizer is categorized under Pitch; its ~5.33 ms nominal stream latency and ~26.7 ms first-note control response at 48 kHz, single-note tracker, and project-authored ranges do not establish Fractal Diatonic tracking or device parity. A project-authored 16-step Pitch Arpeggiator is now present in the same category with Trigger Threshold; it is mono-tracked and supports only ±7 scale degrees because of the existing interval clamp, leaving Fractal's documented ±24 range open. The Formant Filter offers the documented ten Start/Middle/End vowel names and manual Control between them, alongside its older generic sweep. A default-off project-authored sine/triangle/square LFO now drives Control in three-vowel mode (0.05–8 Hz, default 0.5 Hz; project Control range 0–100%, default endpoints 0/100%; 32-frame coefficient update plus existing 10 ms smoothing). Expression assignment remains absent. The November 2026 Blocks Guide permits LFO/expression/modifier assignment to Control, while its general LFO controls are separately described in the FM9 Owner’s Manual v3.x; the project’s control behavior, profiles, bounds, and defaults are not Fractal settings or DSP parity. The eight-mode Harmonic Resonator remains a distinct integer-harmonic filter bank. The four-band Resonator uses four project-authored feedback delays and band-pass filters, with Manual or Chord mode, per-band Frequency/Feedback/Filter Q/Level/Pan, PRE/POST filter positions, and MONO/STEREO input routing. It uses the guide’s published frequency spans (Manual 100–10,000 Hz; Chord root 50–5,000 Hz); its delay/filter DSP, master-frequency range, feedback/Q ranges, defaults, and bounds are project-authored. Input Gain is now available as a project-authored −24…+12 dB control on the existing Resonator row, default 0 dB; the separate Mix-page Level, Balance, Bypass Mode, and Global Mix switch remain unimplemented, and the app’s stage toggle only provides ordinary enable/bypass. This is a documented feature-family match, not Fractal sound or setting parity. A complete Fractal-matching Enhancer remains unimplemented; the project-authored stereo image stage is only related coverage. Crossover, FM9 Synth, Megatap, Multi-Delay, and Plex Delay remain missing; the project Ten-Tap stage is partial and does not implement Rhythm Tap; a generic three-band compressor exists, but Fractal MBC parity is unverified. TabletFX Sweep Delay is project-authored: it is a wet-output band-pass biquad sweep with an immediate dry path and lacks Fractal Filter Type and Sweep Tempo controls. Whether TabletFX's combined Digital Delay stage internally covers subsets of Fractal's other delay blocks is **unknown from the picker**.

### HeadRush Prime

**Prime source clarification (2026-11-23):** Firmware v5.2.0 (October 2026) lists no effect additions/changes. Its release notes repeat the v5.1.0 (August 2026) BBD Delay Mode `CLASSIC`/`MOD` description: Classic retains v5.0-and-earlier behavior; Mod is described qualitatively as improving engaged/bypassed and repeat-level consistency and making Feedback more linear over a wider usable range. The notes do not publish model-specific control bounds or an algorithm formula. TabletFX now exposes Classic/Mod in the existing Delay character control. The project Mod mode uses gentler feedback-path saturation to retain more late-repeat level; it does not implement HeadRush bypass-level matching or its feedback mapping. This is partial, project-authored behavior, not Prime DSP parity. The separate HeadRush effect/control article is scoped to Pedalboard, Gigboard, and MX5 and is not used for Prime settings. C-Verb's documented 10 s maximum is now accepted by the project convolver as 480,000 target-rate frames at 48 kHz. Project coverage remains WAV-only, uses project-authored controls/DSP, and lacks arbitrary multichannel import, Prime IR assets, rig-switch spillover, and Prime processor parity. The native 10-second stereo benchmark on Ryzen 9 5950X measured 79.4286% p95 / 108.1668% p99.9 / 130.4420% maximum of a 2.667 ms callback; therefore real-time playback without glitches remains unverified. [Prime v5.1.0 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/51/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.1.0.pdf) · [Prime v5.2.0 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf) · [Prime v5.0 User Guide](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf).

**Evidence:** The official Downloads page lists Prime firmware/User Guide v5.2.0; the update notes are dated October 2026 and describe NAM performance/bug fixes, with no effect additions/removals. The 11.7 MB manual exceeds the available document-fetch limit, so its per-effect parameter tables and ranges could not be inspected. [Prime product page](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/), [official downloads](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads), [Prime User Guide v5.2 PDF](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.2.0.pdf), [v5.2 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf).

- **Categories and names on the Prime page:** The current product page lists 16 categories and the following named models: **Vocal FX** — Auto-Tune, De-Esser, Vocal Distortion, Vocal Doubler, Vocal Harmony, Vocal Stutter, Vocoder; **Overdrive** — Jimmy OD, Palace OD, Anxiety OD, Anxiety OD V2, B2 Drive, Budda OM OD, Budda ZenMan, D250 Drive, Green JRC-OD, Greener, Greener Lite, Grinder Bass OD, K Drive, S1 Drive, Sher'ff, Treble Booster, White Boost, Glorious Drive; **Distortion/Fuzz** — 8-BIT CRUSH, B DIST 7000, Black Death, BLACK OP, D1 Distortion, DC DISTORT, MX Dist, OCT FUZZ, Round Fuzz, Slab O Meat, TRI FUZZ; **EQ** — Acoustic Sim, Acoustic Pre, Auto Q 8 Band EQ, BASS EQ, G EQ, GRAPHIC EQ, PARA EQ, Promise EQ, 10 Freq EQ; **Compressor** — Budda Comp, DynIII Comp, Gray Comp, Pressor, Side Comp, Slammer; **Delay** — Again Delay, AIR DELAY, BBD DELAY, Budda Samsara Delay, DYN DELAY, M104 Analog Delay, PITCH DELAY, RESO DELAY, REVERSE DELAY, STEREO DOUBLER, TAPE ECHO, Time Warp, Multi Delay, Grain Delay; **Reverb** — AIR REVERB, ELEVEN REVERB, Party Verb, SHIMMER, SPRING REVERB, Spring Guru, C Verb; **Chorus** — Budda Karma Chorus, C2 Bass Chorus, C2 Chorus, Chorus, Detune, Dim Chorus, Multi Chorus; **Phaser/Flanger** — Air Flanger, Analog Flanger, Digital Flanger, Flanger, Orange Phaser, Ring Mod, Square-Phase, Stone Phaser, Tron Phaser, Vibe Phaser; **Vib/Trem/Rotary** — AIR Vibrato, Panner, Rotary, Tremolo, Tube Tremolo, Vibrato; **Wah/Filter** — Air Filter, Black Wah, Budda BudWah, Env Filter, More Wah, Shine Wah, Tron Filter, White Bass Wah; **Synth** — Ambi-Verb, Hybrid Synth, Electric Piano, DB-33 Organ; **Rhythmic** — Kill Seq, Splicer; **Pitch** — Up Tune, Chord Wham, Drop Tune, Harm, Octaves, Octaves Up, Smart Harm, Wham; **Volume/Dynamics** — Autoswell, Feedback, Hold, Volume, Sustain; **Utility** — Gate, Kaften Gate, Noise Filter. These are product-page inventory names, not a verified per-model parameter table or proof of input-note polyphony/guitar-tracked synthesis. [Prime product page](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/)
- **Inventory qualifications:** The page labels Overdrive “16” but names 18 models. Its Pitch list header says “7” and repeats `Octaves`; the detailed model cards below name eight distinct entries (listed above). The page has no complete firmware-tagged changelog for every model, and its list omits the 5.1 Graphic Bass EQ addition. Treat the displayed names/counts as the manufacturer's current web inventory with those internal inconsistencies, not a frozen or independently audited v5.2.0 effect list. The official v5.2.0 guide is listed but could not be inspected here; its model-specific controls and ranges remain unverified.
- **Grain Delay:** Prime firmware v4.1.0 (July 2025) introduced HeadRush's original Granular Delay; the current Prime page lists Grain Delay under Delay. The v5.2.0 guide is linked but could not be text-inspected here, so its Grain-specific controls, bounds, grain scheduling, pitch range, channel behavior, and bypass/tail behavior remain unknown. Prime's general delay/reverb tail setting does not establish Grain-specific parameter or preset-change behavior. [v4.1.0 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v4.1.0.pdf), [Prime product page](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/)
- **Firmware additions:** v3.2 adds Hybrid Synth, Electric Piano, and DB-33 Organ; v4.0 adds Arpeggiator controls to those blocks, Kill Seq/Splicer, and Spring Guru. v5.0 adds Up Tune; v5.0.1 adds Drop Tune's Legacy and Refined modes (the notes describe Refined as faster/better tracking); v5.1 adds Graphic Bass EQ and BBD Delay's Classic/Mod mode. v5.2 does not list new or removed effects. The product page has count/list mismatches (for example Overdrive says 16 but lists 18, and Pitch says 7 but lists 8 with a duplicate Octaves label); do not treat those counts as an audited inventory.
- **Rig resources:** Prime supports up to 14 blocks in a rig; official signal paths include 2 vocal + 12 guitar blocks, 4 vocal + 10 guitar blocks, and a dual-guitar layout with 7 FX blocks per input. Delay and reverb tails can spill across rig changes. See the [Prime FAQ](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000834532-headrush-prime-frequently-asked-questions) and [signal-path reference](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000834956-headrush-prime-what-are-the-new-signal-paths).
- **Spring:** The Prime product page calls its Spring Reverb Fender-inspired; it does not publish the exact controls. Do not transfer Spring controls from the separate list for Pedalboard, Gigboard, and MX5.
- **Parameter evidence:** Prime-specific Vocal Harmony support articles document Lead Level, Harmony Level, Humanize, Auto-Src, Scale Key/Type, Octave Split, delay sync, Stereo, EQ, plus per-voice on/off, Mode, Pitch, Level, Pan, Gender, Delay, and Smooth. Guitar can drive the harmony source, but this is vocal harmony following guitar chords, not proof that guitar pitch effects track chords. The readable official v5.0 guide shows Orange Phaser Rate/Stereo/Sync and Eleven Reverb Decay/Tone/Pre-Delay/Mix/Tails as editor examples; these are not numeric-range tables. C-Verb controls are High Pass, Low Pass, Wet, and Tails, with one C-Verb per rig and IR-length/DSP constraints. The full v5.2 manual is listed officially but was not parseable here; do not borrow Pedalboard/Gigboard/MX5 effect tables.
- **Specific uncovered model families:** The official Prime product page lists **De-Esser** under Vocal FX and **Acoustic Sim / Acoustic Pre** under EQ. The readable official material does not expose those models' parameters or numeric ranges. The project now includes a project-authored split-band De-Esser in Vocal FX; that is related category coverage, not proof of Prime's detector, controls, or sound. Acoustic simulation is distinct from an acoustic-body IR. Prime also lists Auto-Tune, Vocal Distortion, Vocal Doubler, Vocal Harmony, Vocal Stutter, and Vocoder; the page names do not prove the detailed v5.2 controls or tracking behavior.
- **Checklist result:** current Prime effect families and firmware additions are documented, and some vocal/editor controls are verified. Dedicated electric-to-acoustic simulation and the other six named vocal models remain implementation gaps; De-Esser has independent project coverage, while its Prime-specific settings and DSP remain unknown. Numeric parameter ranges and the complete per-model v5.2 parameter inventory remain unverified. [Vocal Harmony setup](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000855449-headrush-setup-and-troubleshooting-the-vocal-harmony-effect), [guitar as Vocal Harmony source](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000839530-headrush-prime-controlling-vocal-harmonies-with-your-guitar)

### XRecer XR1

**Evidence:** XRecer's official product page, FAQ, November 2026 founder letter, and download center were checked 2026-11-23. The exact-model User Manual link resolves to a wiki root that provides no readable effect tables in this review; the Download Center exposes a Windows ASIO driver v6.16.0 (released 2026-09-01), not a readable FX manual. The FAQ's OS 1.0.0 mention applies specifically to NAM/CapX training, not proof of firmware installed on all units. The product page lists first-batch pre-orders with estimated shipping on 2026-12-09; the founder letter says firmware 1.1 will ship with full vocal processing, while more amps/effects and a guitar synth are future plans. Do not count these as verified current-device features. [XR1 product page](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1), [Nov 19 launch article](https://www.xrecer.com/blogs/article/xrecer-unveils-xr1-a-new-era-of-multi-effects), [founder's letter](https://www.xrecer.com/blogs/article/founder-letter), [official FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq), [download center](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/download), [manual wiki](https://wiki.xrecer.com/).

- **Listed categories and inventory scope:** The FAQ/product page list Overdrive, EQ, Dynamics, Modulation, Reverb, Pitch, Filters, Delay, Wah, Volume, Vocal, Loop, IR Loader, FX Loop, CapX, NAM, and NAM Lite; amp/cab are also separate. The launch article assigns firmware 1.0 counts of 50 amps, 31 drives, 13 cabinets, 21 modulation effects, 16 delays, 14 reverbs, and 11 dynamics, with other categories included in its 187 built-in amps/cabs/effects total. Separately, the manufacturer FAQ says the manual's Effects List contains 111 effect-model entries, plus four IR Loader and nine FX Loop entries. The accessible official pages do not expose the 111 individual names or their per-effect controls/ranges; the manual link resolves to a wiki root without readable tables. These counts have different tally boundaries and do not reveal algorithm names or parameter tables. [FAQ inventory answer](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq)
- **Resources, routing, and documented system controls:** The FAQ describes up to four signal chains, up to eight blocks per chain and 32 per preset, subject to CPU; each chain has an input noise gate and the unit has a five-band Global EQ. Delay/reverb tails have a Tail setting; tempo is 40–300 BPM with global/preset/scene scope. A preset can use one Looper or Simple Looper block, with overdub, quantize, up to four minutes, and eight Loop Files. FX Loop exposes Send Level, Return Level, and Mix; EXP1/2 accepts TRS pedals/switches, global calibration, and parameter assignment. Numeric ranges for these system controls are not published in the reviewed FAQ.
- **Effect vs assets/routing:** IR Loader, CapX, NAM/NAM Lite, FX Loop, global EQ, input gates, and loopers are distinct asset/capture/routing/utility functions; do not count each as a built-in audio-effect algorithm without model-level manual evidence. The available sources do not clarify whether the `Loop` category and the one-per-preset looper entries refer to the same block.
- **Firmware boundary and checklist result:** As of 2026-11-23, the product page still lists pre-orders and estimates first-batch shipping for 2026-12-09; the first batch had not shipped. The founder letter says firmware 1.1 will add full vocal processing at shipment; additional amps/effects will follow during the next two months, and a guitar synth is in development. Those are announced plans, not present OS 1.0 availability or published model/parameter tables. XR1 has verified effect categories and system/routing behavior, but factory algorithm names, individual controls/ranges, current installed firmware, and shipped v1.1 availability remain Unknown. TabletFX has related generic categories, not XR1 factory model parity; a project-authored monophonic guitar-tracked oscillator and one-layer/shuffled sliced-loop processors provide related function coverage, but XR1 vocal processing, synth model, quantized recording, longer loop storage, and saved Loop Files remain gaps.

## Shimmer and convolution reverb comparison (checked 2026-11-23)

This focused check separates pitch-fed shimmer algorithms from convolution reverb IR loaders. A product listing establishes that an effect is named or offered; it does not establish undocumented controls, pitch intervals, quality, or tails. The six-model results below are not a fresh audit of every algorithm in the catalogue.

| Exact model and source revision | Shimmer evidence and documented controls | Convolution / IR evidence and remaining unknowns |
|---|---|---|
| **Fender Tone Master Pro** — Model Guide v1.1.100; current checked firmware v1.8.85 (2026-11-18) | The guide names **Shimmer Reverb** and describes a reverb plus two-octave pitch shift. Controls: Mix, Decay, Damping, Diffuse, Feedback, High Pass Filter, and Spillover Switch. No numeric ranges are published. It separately lists **Celestial** with Mix, Decay, Pitch 1, Pitch 2, Pitch Decay, High Cut, Blend, and Spillover; no bounds, defaults, or topology are published. Firmware v1.6.48 fixed a Shimmer Damping issue; the v1.8.85 notes contain no newer Shimmer or Celestial parameter change. | The guide also names spring convolution reverbs; later notes add convolution hall, plate, chamber, and room models. The guide says only one convolution reverb, including one built into an amp, can be used at a time. Per-model IR controls and numeric ranges for the later models are not in the reviewed guide. [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates) |
| **Line 6 Helix Stadium XL** — current v1.4 effect-block reference and model catalogue; latest checked Stadium hardware firmware v1.4.2 (2026-10-27) | The catalogue lists mono and stereo **Shimmer**, first appearing in 1.0. The manual describes shared Reverb parameters Decay, Predelay, Mix, Level, Low Cut, High Cut, and Trails where supported; it explicitly does not guarantee that every model exposes every common control. No Shimmer-specific table or numeric bounds were found. | The checked materials identify broad Reverb families but no per-model convolution reverb IR loader or settings in this review. [Effects reference](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) · [Model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/) · [Firmware notes](https://kb.line6.com/helix-stadium-1-4-release-notes) |
| **Fractal FM9 Mark II Turbo** — June 2023 family Blocks Guide; downloads page lists v12.0 for FM9 Original/Turbo, without separate Mark II Turbo confirmation | **Shimmer Verb** is a type in the Plex Delay block, not the standard Reverb block. The guide documents Reverb Size and Decay Time for Plex Verb and adds Shimmer Intensity (0–10) and Direction (Forward/Reverse) for Shimmer Verb. Pitch Shift 1–8 at ±24 semitones is explicitly described under the separate Plex Shift type; do not transfer that interval setting to Shimmer Verb. The guide is family-level and does not establish a separate Mark II Turbo firmware inventory. | The reviewed guide does not identify a user IR convolution reverb in the FM9 effect comparison. [Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 firmware downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) · [Mark II Turbo product page](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit) |
| **HeadRush Prime** — C-Verb controls verified in official v5.0.0 User Guide; latest checked firmware v5.2.0 (October 2026). Official downloads list a v5.2 User Guide, but that 11.7 MB PDF was not text-inspected. | The model page lists **SHIMMER**, identified as a HeadRush Original. The examined v5.0.0 guide does not provide a Shimmer-specific parameter table; controls and ranges remain unknown. | **C-Verb** is the convolution reverb. The v5.0.0 guide names Preset, Reverb File, High Pass, Low Pass, Wet, and Tails (On/Off); WAV/AIF/OPUS file formats; a 10 s maximum IR length; use of only the first two channels of a multichannel IR; one instance per rig; and CPU use dependent on IR length. Numeric slider ranges are not stated. Per-effect Tails lets an active reverb continue decaying after bypass; Prime also has rig-wide tails, which do not work with FX Loop or USB Audio enabled. The v5.2.0 release notes focus on NAM additions/improvements and do not name a C-Verb change. [Prime model list](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/) · [Prime User Guide v5.0.0](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [v5.2.0 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf) · [official downloads](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads.html) |
| **MELOFTY Axom Max** — User Manual V1.7.4 (2026-08-31) | The manual documents reverb as an effect family but does not identify a Shimmer algorithm or publish a per-model reverb parameter table. | Manual support for third-party IR files is specifically described as cabinet simulation; that is not evidence of convolution reverb. Exact reverb algorithms, parameters, and ranges remain unknown. [Manual V1.7.4](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) |
| **XRecer XR1** — official FAQ and product page checked 2026-11-22; still listed for pre-order, with first shipment estimated 2026-12-09 | No named Shimmer effect or Shimmer control table is established by the reviewed public materials. | The FAQ documents single and dual Reverb IR Loader entries and third-party 24-bit/48 kHz WAV IRs at 2,048 samples. It does not publish IR-loader parameter controls. Pre-order material is not evidence of a shipped device or installed firmware. [FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq) · [Product page](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1) |

**TabletFX implementation:** `Shimmer Reverb` is an independent item in the categorized Reverb picker (stage 57). It is project-authored: a fully wet plate tail is pitch-shifted, high-pass filtered, and returned through a bounded feedback path. Controls are Decay 0.2–12 s (3 s), Damping 200–min(18 kHz, 0.45×sample rate) (6,500 Hz), Diffusion 0–1 (0.85), Pitch return 0–0.65 (0.25), Return high-pass 20–min(1,500 Hz, 0.2×sample rate) (80 Hz), Pitch +1…+12 semitones (+12), Shimmer intensity 0–1 (1), Mix 0–1 (0.25), and wet Level −24…+12 dB (0 dB). A 128-frame feedback ring adds 2.67 ms at 48 kHz before the pitch-shifter's own wet latency; the direct dry signal is immediate. The 10 ms control ramps, bounds, and DSP are project decisions, not vendor settings. On a Ryzen 9 5950X Release build, its isolated CPU was 11.7097% p50 / 18.5503% p95 / 20.0691% p99 / 23.5008% p99.9 / 27.3648% maximum of a 2.667 ms callback. Bypass clears the tail; spillover is not implemented. Native/CLI and browser/WASM checks pass. No manufacturer algorithm or sound parity is claimed.

**TabletFX implementation — Celestial Reverb:** The Reverb picker now has a separate project-authored dual-pitch feedback reverb at stage 77. Its control labels follow the Fender Model Guide, while values, ranges, defaults, plate network, pitch paths, and 128-frame returns are authored for this project: Mix 0.25 (0–1), Decay 3 s (0.2–12), Pitch 1 +12 and Pitch 2 +7 semitones (each −12…+12), Pitch Decay 2 s (0.2–12), High Cut 9 kHz (200…min(20 kHz, 0.45×sample rate)), Blend 0.5 (0–1), and Spillover Off/On. The direct path has no added fixed latency; the wet pitch shifters have their own processing latency. Spillover preserves this block's wet tail on bypass only, not across preset/rig changes. This is not Fender's Celestial DSP or control-range parity.

**TabletFX implementation — Cloud Reverb:** The Reverb picker has a project-authored mono/stereo Cloud entry at effect index 79 (display row 80). It exposes Mix 0.25 (0–1), Tone 0.50 (0–1), Decay 7 s (0.2–20), Diffuse 0.80 (0–1), High Damp 9 kHz (1,000–min(20 kHz, 0.45×sample rate)), Low Damp 80 Hz (20–1,000), Rate 0.30 Hz (0.05–8), Depth 15% (0–1), and Spillover Off/On. The Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 names these controls but provides no bounds, defaults, modulation waveform, pitch interval/voice count, or internal topology; firmware v1.1.100 introduced Cloud, v1.2.56 changed High Damp increments to 100 Hz, and the latest checked firmware release v1.8.85 (2026-11-18) names no Cloud change. Project defaults, bounds, modulation, and DSP are not Fender settings or algorithm parity. Spillover preserves the effect's tail on block bypass only, not across preset/rig changes.


**TabletFX implementation — Convolution Reverb.** The Reverb picker has a project-authored uniform 1,024-frame overlap-add convolver for a user-selected local mono/stereo WAV. It accepts up to 480,000 target-rate frames (10 s at 48 kHz), linearly resamples at load time, and aligns dry and wet paths with a fixed 21.33 ms partition latency at 48 kHz. Controls are Mix, Wet Level, wet High Pass (0–10,000 Hz), wet Low Pass (0–20,000 Hz), and per-block Tails (Off by default); these bounds/defaults and the one-pole wet-filter DSP are project-authored because Prime publishes labels but no numeric ranges. When Tails is On, a bypassed block passes the chain input through and adds its stored wet tail; when Off, pending state is cleared. Mono IRs feed both output channels; stereo IRs process left and right independently, without cross-channel matrix convolution. No IR files are bundled. Prime's 10-second duration ceiling is covered, while arbitrary multichannel import, AIF/OPUS support, Prime IR assets, global rig switching, FX Loop/USB spillover rules, and vendor DSP remain unimplemented. This is duration/control-role coverage, not HeadRush C-Verb emulation. The 2026-11-23 native test passes the 10-second IR, impulse timing, filter, tail, and allocation checks; the browser self-test verifies the categorized Reverb row, loads and replaces a 10-second stereo WAV in the fixed 64 MiB heap, checks separate channel output and 1,024-frame latency, and confirms the 10-second worklet limit. The native 10-second stereo benchmark is 79.4286% p95 and 108.1668% p99.9 of a 2.667 ms callback; timing outliers mean glitch-free real-time behavior remains open. Manual listening and real-route performance remain unverified.

Manufacturer sources: [Fender Tone Master Pro Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) and [firmware history](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates); [HeadRush Prime v5.0.0 User Guide](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf), [v5.2.0 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf), [official downloads](https://www.headrushfx.com/downloads.html), and [C-Verb IR support note](https://support.headrushfx.com/en/support/solutions/articles/69000878423-prime-core-and-flex-prime-c-verb-reverb-file-missing-or-corrupt); [XRecer XR1 FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq).

## Reverse-delay comparison (checked 2026-11-22)

This is a focused reverse-delay check of the six requested processors, not a claim that their algorithms or controls are interchangeable. Four source sets name a Reverse Delay. Axom Max and XR1 public documentation does not establish an algorithm-level Reverse Delay entry. TabletFX now exposes the shared documented controls with project-authored signal paths and bounds; native, browser/WASM, prerecorded pickup/DI, and Windows cross-build checks pass. Manufacturer-specific numeric behavior and hardware listening remain unverified.

| Processor and source scope | Documented reverse-delay behavior and controls | Ranges and limits not established by the reviewed source |
|---|---|---|
| **Fractal FM9 Mark II Turbo** — the June 2023 [Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) covers the FM9 family; Fractal's [FM9 downloads page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) labels firmware v12.0 (2026-07-07) compatible with FM9 Original and FM9 Turbo. The [Mark II Turbo product page](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit?sort=newest) identifies the hardware, while firmware documentation does not give a separate Mark II effect inventory. | **Reverse Delay** is a Mono Delay-based effect that continuously records timed snippets and plays each one backward while recording the next; it reverses successive snippets, not a whole phrase or take. `Time` sets recording-window length and can sync to `Tempo`; `Run` enables reverse playback, while OFF mutes playback but recording continues; `Trigger Restart` restarts recording when `Run` turns on; `Crossfade Time` extends the recording interval so neighboring snippets overlap and crossfade. Set `Mix` to 100% for reverse-only output. | The guide does not publish reverse-specific numeric ranges for `Time` or `Crossfade Time`, nor buffer memory, measured latency, or reverse-specific tail/bypass behavior. Its generic Delay-block summary says up to 16 seconds on FM9, but does not explicitly assign that as the Reverse `Time` range; do not use it as a confirmed setting limit. |
| **Line 6 Helix Stadium XL** — the current [manual selector](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium) offers the v1.4 Rev E / v1.4.1 documentation set; the latest checked hardware release notes are v1.4.2 (2026-10-27). Line 6's [model catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/) lists current models. | The catalogue lists **Reverse Delay** as a Line 6 Original in Mono and Stereo, first appearing in firmware 1.0. It separately lists a **Reverse** model in the Legacy subcategory; these are distinct entries. The current [Effects manual](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks) confirms the broad Delay category, including reverse effects. | The reviewed Stadium manual does not provide a Reverse Delay model-specific parameter table. Exact controls, ranges, buffering/latency, phrase/window behavior, and model-specific bypass/tail rules remain unknown. Do not import parameters from older Helix manuals. |
| **Fender Tone Master Pro** — the linked [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) is firmware v1.1.100; Fender's [rolling firmware notes](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates) currently list v1.8.85 (latest checked here). | **Reverse Delay** is described as a digital delay whose repeats play backward, with a forward-delay contribution as an extra tone component. The guide names `TIME` (time before first repeat), `TAP DIVISION`, `FEEDBACK` (number of repeats), `MIX`, `HIGH CUT`, `LOW CUT` (repeat EQ), `FORWARD LEVEL`, and `SPILLOVER SWITCH` (continues repeats after bypass). Fender's v1.1.111 notes (2023-11-20) record a fix for “Reverse delay volume drop.” | The v1.1.100 guide supplies no numeric ranges for these Reverse Delay controls and does not explain its reverse-buffer/window construction or measured latency. The spillover switch is documented; per-model behavior during preset changes is not specified by this entry. |
| **HeadRush Prime** — the current [Prime product/model page](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/) lists the effect and links the **v5.2.0 User Guide**; official [v5.2.0 update instructions](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf) date that firmware to October 2026. | The model list names **REVERSE DELAY** and identifies it as inspired by the BOSS DD-5. The product page separately states that Prime can configure reverb/delay tails to continue when changing presets or bypassing those effects in a rig; this is general Prime delay behavior, not a Reverse-specific control. | Reviewed official Prime material does not publish Reverse Delay control names/ranges or buffer/window semantics. The v5.2.0 User Guide is linked by HeadRush but its large PDF could not be text-inspected in this review; no DD-5 controls are substituted for missing Prime evidence. |
| **Melofty Axom Max** — Melofty's [V1.7.4 manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) (2026-08-31) documents Delay and Looper categories. | The manual does not enumerate delay algorithms or expose a Reverse Delay parameter table. It describes reverse/half-speed playback as a phrase-looper function; that is looper playback, not evidence of a Reverse Delay block. | Whether Axom Max has a separate Reverse Delay algorithm, its parameters/ranges, and current device firmware are unverified. Do not infer it from the Delay category or reverse looper function. |
| **XRecer XR1** — XRecer's [FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq), [product page](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1), and [launch article](https://www.xrecer.com/blogs/article/xrecer-unveils-xr1-a-new-era-of-multi-effects) were checked on 2026-11-22; the FAQ's OS 1.0.0 mention is specifically about NAM/CapX training and does not establish current installed firmware. | Public sources list a Delay category and effect-count summaries but do not expose algorithm-level names or control tables. The published manual/wiki content was not readable in this review. | A Reverse Delay entry, controls/ranges, and its behavior are unverified; this is unknown rather than No. Published delay/effect counts have different tally boundaries and are not directly comparable. Do not infer a model from the Delay category or planned firmware additions. |

**TabletFX implementation (2026-11-22):** The project-authored rolling-snippet delay now has free or tempo-synced Time (20–300 BPM, twelve divisions), Feedback, Mix, Crossfade, Run, Trigger Restart, repeat Low/High Cut, Forward Level, and block-bypass Spillover. The project bounds are Time 1–2,000 ms, Feedback 0–0.95, Mix 0–1, Crossfade 0–50 ms subject to less than half of the effective Time, Low Cut 0–1,000 Hz, High Cut 0–20,000 Hz (sample-rate constrained; zero disables each cut), and Forward Level 0–1. The forward level is a parallel forward tap; spillover covers only bypass and does not establish preset/scene tails. Those are project design choices, not copied Fender numeric ranges or claimed vendor topology. Native unit tests, CLI checks (including a tempo-synced capture), 45-stage browser/native parity, prerecorded pickup/stereo-DI regression, Linux CPU/memory benchmark, and MinGW Windows cross-build pass. Device listening, actual commercial latency, and tail comparisons remain open.

The evidence supports different design questions: Fractal explicitly documents continuously rolling reverse snippets and restart/crossfade controls; Fender documents reverse repeats plus an adjustable forward component and a bypass spillover switch; Stadium and Prime establish model availability but not accessible per-effect control tables; Axom Max and XR1 algorithm-level Reverse Delay status remains unknown. No reviewed source provides comparable measured latency or memory data. Keep reverse delay separate from reverse reverb, reverse looper playback, and a phrase sampler.

## Ducking and dynamic delay comparison (checked 2026-11-21)

This is a focused documentation comparison of ducked/dynamic delay behavior across the six named processors. “Ducking Delay” here means the repeated signal is reduced while the current input is strong and recovers after it falls; it does not equate a model name with identical DSP. The project implementation uses its own linked peak detector and does not claim any vendor's modulation, tone, tempo, spillover, or model voicing.

| Processor and reviewed documentation | Documented model behavior and controls | Applicability and unresolved detail |
|---|---|---|
| **Fractal FM9 Mark II Turbo** — the official [Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf), current checked cover dated **June 2023**, covers FM9 at family level. The [FM9 download page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) lists firmware **v12.0 (2026-07-07)** for FM9 Original and Turbo; the [Mark II Turbo product page](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit?sort=newest) identifies the hardware. | The stereo Delay block's **Ducking Delay** lowers echo level as the player plays harder. Controls include `Ducker Atten`, `Ducker Threshold`, and `Ducker Release`. Attenuation 0 disables ducking; 20 dB is a guide example. | The guide does not state that Mark II Turbo has a separate effect inventory, nor publish complete numeric bounds for the three ducker controls. The generic FM9 delay maximum of 16 seconds is not established as the Ducking Delay-specific range. |
| **Fender Tone Master Pro** — the official [Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) is firmware **v1.1.100, Rev. A**. ## Modulated delay (DLY-11)

The comparison below checks manufacturer documentation for delay-time modulation. Similar names do not imply identical modulation paths: an LFO-modulated delay, an analog/BBD voicing switch, and a delay category with no accessible effect list are different evidence.

| Exact model | Documented model and controls | Revision and limits |
|---|---|---|
| **Fender Tone Master Pro** | The Model Guide names **Dynamic Delay** and **Studio Delay** with Mod Rate, Mod Depth, and Mod Mode (`SINE`, `TRI`, `RAND`, `TRIG`); Studio Delay also lists Pan Rate, Pan Depth, and Pan Mode (`SINE`, `TRI`, `ENV`). The guide says ENV follows input strength and gives wider panning at lower input. In Dynamic Delay, `TRIG` synchronizes a ramp to input crossing its threshold; Threshold, Attenuation, and Release are the ducking controls. Memory Delay documentation also separates Chorus/Vibrato mode and modulation depth. TabletFX now maps the documented Envelope pan role to a project-authored mode in the existing Stereo Panner (Modulation index 90), using its input-level envelope to reduce LFO excursion on louder passages. This is a control-role mapping, not a Studio Delay block or wet-repeat panner. | Guide v1.1.100, Rev. A; installed firmware remains unknown. Fender v1.6.34 notes extend Dynamic Delay Time to 30–2,000 ms. Other modulation ranges, current firmware applicability, and parameters on any individual unit are not established. App v1.9.2 notes mention generic delay divisions and High/Low Cut, but are app notes, not firmware controls or proof of modulation on a named delay. | Manual dated 2026-08-31; app release notes dated 2026-10-26. Model, controls, and ranges remain Unknown. [Product](https://www.xrecer.com/products/xr1) · [FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq) · [Downloads](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/download) |

**TabletFX implementation:** `modulated-delay` is a project-authored Delay-category processor. It provides Time, Feedback, Mix, LFO Rate/Depth, Sine/Triangle/Random/Triggered Ramp, stereo spread, and a trigger threshold. Its bounds/defaults are project choices; the four waveform options are not attributed to any one device. The direct path remains immediate, settings ramp over 10 ms, and the delay ring is allocated at construction. The existing Stereo Panner at Modulation index 90 also exposes a project-authored Envelope mode using its level-following ducking path; this is not an integrated Studio Delay or wet-only repeat panner. Native tests, local prerecorded-guitar browser Play, Linux mode benchmark, and Windows cross-build pass. Manual listening and device verification remain open. See `EFFECTS_MATRIX.md`, `IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md`, and `LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md` for exact project bounds, metrics, and limits.

**Project match:** `DLY-10` is a project-authored Time/Feedback/Mix delay with Threshold, Attenuation, Release, tempo-synced Tap Division, feedback-path Low/High Cut, optional block-bypass Spillover, and Rate/Depth/Mode modulation. A stereo-linked current-input peak detector reduces only delayed wet signal above threshold; the dry signal and internal feedback remain un-ducked. The project Sine/Triangle/Random/Triggered Ramp modes, 0.05–10 Hz rate, 0–50 ms headroom-capped depth, and threshold-triggered ramp behavior are not vendor settings or DSP parity. With Spillover on, bypass preserves unity dry input and lets the existing wet tail decay without capturing new input; with it off (default), bypass clears delay memory. This covers block bypass only, not preset/scene transitions. It has no additional fixed dry-path latency. Numeric project bounds and defaults are recorded in `EFFECTS_MATRIX.md`. Fractal, Fender, Stadium, and Prime document an algorithm-level match at the stated scopes; Axom Max and XR1 remain Unknown. No reviewed model source provides comparable measured DSP latency, resource use, or preset-switch tails.

**DLY-10 manufacturer references:** [Fractal Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf), [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/), [FM9 Mark II Turbo product page](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit?sort=newest), [Fender Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf), [Fender firmware notes](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates), [Helix Stadium catalogue](https://line6.com/helix-stadium-models/), [Stadium effects manual](https://manuals.line6.com/en/helix-stadium/live/effect-blocks), [HeadRush Prime product page](https://www.headrushfx.com/products/prime/), [Prime v5.2.0 update notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf), [Axom Max V1.7.4 manual](https://cdn.rockboard.de/nos/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf), [Axom Max app notes](https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.melofty.axommax), and [XR1 FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq).
