| **MELOFTY Axom Max** | The exact-model V1.7.4 manual documents Delay and Special Delay categories but does not enumerate algorithms. The official app v1.9.2 release notes report generic delay subdivisions from 1/64 to 1/1 and added High/Low Cut. | The app note is dated 2026-10-26 and is not proof of device firmware applicability. Cutoff ranges, algorithm identity, and whether those cuts process feedback repeats are unknown. Preserve the discrepancy between the manual's 880+ model claim and the distributor's 130+ figure. [V1.7.4 manual](https://dealer.warwick.de/warwick/data/erp/Artikell_PDF/Manual_PDF/Melofty/EN/MEL_AXOM_MAX_Manual_EN.pdf) · [App release history](https://apps.apple.com/il/app/melofty/id6762972347?platform=mac) |
| **XRecer XR1** | The official FAQ lists effect categories including Delay and Filter and states an Effects List of 111 entries. | The FAQ does not name a filtered-delay model or provide effect-specific parameters. The linked wiki manual was not readable in this review; exact availability and controls are **Unknown**, not No. [XR1 FAQ](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/faq) · [Download center](https://www.xrecer.com/pages/download) · [Manual wiki](https://wiki.xrecer.com/) |

**TabletFX implementation status (2026-11-23):** DLY-12 is a project-authored static filtered feedback delay; DLY-20 is a separate swept-filter delay. Its project-authored per-channel biquad can sweep low-pass, band-pass (default), or high-pass filtering across delayed wet output, with an immediate dry path. Controls/defaults include Time 350 ms (1–2,000 ms), Feedback 0.25 (0–0.95), Mix 0.25 (0–1), Start/Stop 250/6,000 Hz (each 20–min(18,000 Hz, 0.45×sample rate)), Resonance Q 1.2 (0.5–12), free Rate 0.5 Hz (0.05–10 Hz) or tempo-synchronized LFO cycles using project divisions from whole through 1/64 at selected 20–300 BPM (one cycle per division, project-derived 0.083–80 Hz), Sine/Triangle/Square sweep shapes, and Phase 90° (0–360°); parameter changes ramp over 10 ms. The Fractal guide documents filter type and sweep tempo, but gives no numeric ranges, defaults or block-specific sweep-shape choices. TabletFX timing, ranges, defaults and DSP are project-authored, not vendor emulation or settings parity. `make web-test` passes 93/93 CTest cases and browser/native parity (maximum absolute error 0.0000134999, RMS 0.0000027541); isolated browser Play with only Sweep Delay enabled changed 47,999/48,000 local-guitar samples (peak 0.540929, RMS difference 0.205560). `make audio-regression` passes the five pickup positions and 72.5 s stereo DI through the 70-stage chain. `make bench` measured 768,968 B object-inclusive memory and 0.4046% isolated p95 CPU at 48 kHz stereo / a 2.667 ms deadline. DLY-13 remains a separate project-authored Pitch Feedback Delay; DLY-20 does not satisfy DLY-13, and DLY-19 controlled self-oscillation remains open. Hardware listening remains open. See `IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md` and `RESEARCH.md` for evidence and source notes.

Rhythmic Slicer (`MOD-16`) and Step Filter (`EQF-14`) are project-authored 16-step amplitude and filter effects. Their controls are implemented from the project's checklist, not cloned from the proprietary models above. More sequenced modulation families, such as sample-and-hold filters, step tremolo/delay, and pitch sequencing, remain open.

For the complete per-requirement status and evidence, use the linked checklist IDs rather than duplicating every project requirement here. They should only be marked complete after their implementation and automated-test boxes pass; device/listening evidence remains a separate column.

## Recommended implementation order

This order favors categories documented across the named units, while limiting DSP cost and keeping distinct algorithms separate. It is a work queue, not a claim that all processors share identical models or controls.

1. **Room reverb (`REV-01`) — implemented foundation.** Native C++, offline CLI, browser/WASM picker, prerecorded audio regression, and Windows cross-build are covered; automated tests and CPU/memory measurement pass. The algorithm is project-authored and does not claim a commercial room model. Active-tail response when changing room size and human listening remain open.
2. **Hall (`REV-02`) — implemented and validated.** The project-authored algorithm has separate native and browser stages, controls, tests, recorded-guitar regression, Windows cross-build, and CPU/memory figures. It is not represented as a Helix Stadium Dynamic Hall or Fender convolution-Hall clone; exact commercial model internals remain unpublished.
3. **Plate (`REV-03`) — implemented and validated.** The project-authored plate-style FDN is not a Fender Plate model or Helix Dynamic Plate clone. **Spring (`REV-04…05`)** is next; keep convolution assets and hardware amp spring models separate.
4. **Auto Swell (`DYN-13`) — implemented and validated.** Fractal's guide documents threshold, attack, release, hysteresis, taper, and volume controls but omits numeric limits and taper curves. This project uses its own detector and curve approximations.
5. **Rotary Speaker (`MOD-15`) — implemented and validated.** The November 2026 FM9-family guide (printed p. 98) establishes separate mic-spacing and motor-time-constant controls and says zero Hi Depth can simulate drum-only operation; it gives no numeric limits or mappings. TabletFX uses its own crossover, bounded delay, motor model, and normalized controls, and now parks the horn/Doppler path at zero Hi Depth regardless of Rotor Length. `make test` and the current native benchmark pass; the desktop p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max load is 0.4819/0.4917/0.6994/1.0369/1.5627% of a 2.667 ms callback with 1,208 B state. FM9-family firmware applicability is qualified because the current v12.0 page names Original/Turbo but not Mark II Turbo explicitly; installed firmware is unknown. Listening and hardware comparisons remain open.
6. **Ring modulation (`MOD-17`) — Classic and SSB Up/Down implemented with project-authored DSP; pitch tracking remains open.** The Fractal guide names Classic, two SSB types, Frequency Multiplier, and Pitch Tracking; the Helix Stadium catalogue separately lists AM Ring Mod, Pitch Ring Mod, and a legacy Ring Modulator. No shared proprietary controls or sound are assumed.
7. **Rhythmic slicer (`MOD-16`), sequenced filter (`EQF-14`), and static notch (`EQF-06`) — implemented and automated.** Their patterns, controls, timing, browser parity, prerecorded input, CPU, and memory are recorded in `EFFECTS_MATRIX.md` and `IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md`; manual listening remains open. The reverse-delay manufacturer comparison is recorded above; the partial prototype and its remaining controls/validation are tracked in `DLY-09`. Static filtered delay is DLY-12; the additional swept-filter delay is DLY-20. A tracked monophonic synth remains a separate research lead.
8. **Delay variants (`DLY-09…20`)** — Reverse Delay (`DLY-09`) now has tempo division, repeat cuts, forward tap, and block-bypass spillover with automated native/browser/CLI, recorded-audio, benchmark, and Windows cross-build evidence; live device listening remains open. Ducking Delay (`DLY-10`), Modulated Delay (`DLY-11`), static Filtered Delay (`DLY-12`), and swept-filter delay (`DLY-20`, approved addition) have automated evidence. DLY-13 is integrated as a project-authored pitch-feedback delay, without flagship algorithm parity. Resonant Delay (DLY-15) is integrated and automated, but is not a HeadRush Prime RESO DELAY match; device listening remains open.  swell (DLY-14), hold/freeze (DLY-17), lo-fi (DLY-18), and controlled self-oscillation (DLY-19) remain open. Grain Delay covers DLY-16. Keep each manufacturer comparison scoped to its exact algorithm, controls, and evidence level; preserve safe feedback/memory and document latency/tail behavior.
9. **Pitch/synth and vocal families (`PIT-*`, `SYN-*`, `DYN-16`)** — higher tracking, latency, polyphony, and resource risk. Require chord tests, latency reporting, and a strict distinction among mono tracking, polyphonic audio transposition, sampled tones, oscillators, and MIDI triggering.
10. **Four-band Resonator (`SYN-10`) — first implementation integrated.** The picker exposes Manual/Chord mode, four per-band frequencies/feedback/Q/level/pan controls, per-band pre/post filters, mono/stereo routing, and master controls. Native, CLI, browser/WASM, and recorded-audio validation are tracked before this row is marked fully verified. Fractal’s June 2023 family guide provides the comparison vocabulary and the two frequency spans, but project-authored DSP is not an FM9 emulation; separate Mix-page controls and device listening remain open.
11. **Porta preamp (`AMP-01`) — project-authored version integrated.** Line 6 Helix Stadium v1.4 notes name the PortaBox 424 and its Gain 1/2, Bass, Treble, and Level controls, without publishing numeric ranges/defaults or DSP. TabletFX provides independent project values and simplified saturation/tone processing in a dedicated Amp / preamp picker category; it does not model the full bass amp or claim Line 6 parity. Native and local-guitar browser checks pass; listening, device behavior, isolated CPU, and manufacturer parity remain open.

## Fractal FM9 five-band PEQ — project-authored extension (2026-11-24)

The June 2023 Fractal Audio Blocks Guide is family-level documentation naming the FM9, and describes a five-band PEQ. Bands 1 and 5 support Peaking, Peaking 2, Shelving, Shelving 2, and Blocking; bands 2 and 4 support Peaking, Peaking 2, and either shelf type; band 3 supports the two peaking types. Frequency spans are 20–2,000 Hz for band 1, 100–10,000 Hz for bands 2–4, and 200–20,000 Hz for band 5. Q is 0.1–10, Gain is −20…+20 dB, and outer-band Blocking slope is 6–36 dB/oct. Fractal describes Shelving 2 as a passive-style response with overshoot, Peaking 2 as different at low Q, and Solo as isolating a selected band. Its Mix page documents Level, Balance, Bypass, and Bypass Mode.

The current project retains its existing Five-band Parametric EQ in **Equalizer** at picker ID 59. It now offers project-authored Peaking 2, Low/High Shelf 2, and selectable band-pass solo; labels explicitly identify the shape variants as project variants. The project uses Q 0.25–10 and gain −18…+18 dB. Its Peaking 2 low-Q map, shelf-plus-peak overshoot recipe, solo filter, frequency/control mapping, and DSP are independent approximations, not Fractal settings or sound parity. Fractal's full Mix-page controls are not implemented as part of this EQ stage.

`make test` passes 102/102 CTest cases and 11 fixture-safety checks. `make web-test` passes the 99-row picker and 73-active-slot browser/native comparison (48 kHz stereo; max absolute error 6.6833456e-6; RMS error 1.2175505e-6). Those browser measurements cover the existing five-band EQ path, not focused UI selection of each newly added shape or solo value; that focused browser/WASM check remains open. The picker category is verified by `make inspect-effect-picker`. No human listening or Fractal hardware test was performed. Fractal's FM9 downloads page lists firmware v12.0 for FM9 Original and Turbo but not Mark II Turbo by name; exact Mark II applicability and firmware installed on any owned device are unknown. [June 2023 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

For each implementation, update the relevant row in `IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md` only after native tests and browser/Windows integration checks pass. Record Linux CPU as a percentage of the callback deadline, fixed state/memory, algorithmic latency, and manual listening status in the project's existing benchmark/evidence documents.

## Fender Tone Master Pro Flanger Tap Division — project-authored extension (2026-11-26)

The Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A (p. 38) lists Mono/Stereo Flanger parameters Rate, Depth, Resonance, Tap Division, and Manual, with L/R Phase added for Stereo. Tap Division chooses a tempo subdivision for effect speed. The guide does not list the subdivisions or publish bounds, defaults, latency, or DSP. Fender's firmware history has been checked through v1.8.87 dated 2026-11-23; those notes do not mention Flanger, and the firmware installed on any device is unknown. [Tone Master Pro Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware updates](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds a project-authored Free Hz / Tap Division selector to the existing Modulation Flanger at ID 17, without changing the picker or adding a processor. Free mode remains the 0.25 Hz default. The project offers 14 divisions and a 20–300 BPM range (120 BPM default); quarter note maps to 2 Hz at 120 BPM and 1 Hz at 60 BPM. These values and mappings are independent project choices, not Fender ranges or sound/DSP parity. Native tests verify the mapping and invalid-value fallback; the offline CLI verifies free 2 Hz equals quarter-note sync at 120 BPM and changes at 60 BPM. HeadlessChrome 151 played three local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI captures through the categorized Flanger. The full browser/native comparison and Windows MinGW core/offline-renderer build pass. The 48 kHz stereo/128-frame desktop benchmark reports 4,832 B native state and sync p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.0533/0.0795/0.0829/0.4763/2.3255% of the 2.667 ms callback deadline; no fixed dry-path latency or process-time allocation is added.

Manual guitar listening, a physical Tone Master Pro comparison, and target-device timing remain open. No Fender algorithm, control-range, firmware, DSP, or sound parity is claimed.

The pictured 0.6 Hz and Stereo On are examples; the guide does not define Stereo behavior, ranges/defaults, DSP, or latency. Current v5.2.0 notes do not mention Orange Phaser, which does not prove that the controls are unchanged. [Prime User Guide v5.0.0](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/5/HeadRush%20Prime%20-%20User%20Guide%20-%20v5.0.0.pdf) · [Prime v5.2.0 notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf)

Prime's project toggle defaults On for the existing opposite left/right LFO phase; Off links the phases. The maximum-stage fixed arrays report 360 B native / 344 B browser/WASM, 64 B more than the prior eight-stage capacity. There is no added audio buffer, fixed latency, or processing allocation. Tests, local Guitar-TECHS DI browser Play, and the MinGW Windows cross-build pass. On a Ryzen 9 5950X Release build with 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks, p50 cost is 0.072% at 4 stages and 0.192% at 12 stages; detailed percentiles and the stereo On/Off profiles are in `LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`. These desktop timings do not predict tablet load. Human listening, device checks, and target-device timing remain manual; no Fender or HeadRush algorithm/settings/sound parity is claimed.

## Fender Tone Master Pro Tremolo LFO Clip — project-authored extension (2026-11-26)

The Tone Master Pro Model Guide v1.1.100 / Rev. A (p. 34) lists Rate, Intensity, Duty Cycle, Tap Division, Shape, and LFO Clip. It describes LFO Clip as adjusting tremolo signal clipping percentage, but does not specify a range, default, clipping curve, DSP, or latency. Firmware history currently lists v1.8.87 dated 2026-11-23; its noted change is Virtual Capo, and v1.8.58 separately fixes a possible Step Tremolo crash. Installed firmware is unknown. [Tone Master Pro Model Guide](https://www.fmicassets.com/Damroot/Original/10018/OM_2274900000_TONE-MASTER-PRO-Model-Guide_English.pdf) · [Firmware updates](https://support.fender.com/hc/en-us/articles/46768129660315-Fender-Tone-Master-Pro-Firmware-and-Pro-Control-App-Updates)

TabletFX adds LFO Clip to the existing Modulation Tremolo at ID 15. The 0% default is bit-for-bit equivalent to the existing waveform path. Project-authored 0–100% controls symmetrically hard-clip and renormalize the bipolar LFO; this chosen curve and range are not Fender settings or parity. Native tests cover bounds, positive/negative peak flattening, default identity, allocation behavior, and finite output. The offline renderer checks output changes. HeadlessChrome 151 played three local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI captures and verified the categorized picker and control range/default. Full browser/native parity and the Windows MinGW core/offline-renderer cross-build pass. Native state is 96 B, browser/WASM state 84 B, and no fixed audio latency or process-time allocation is added. At 48 kHz stereo/128 frames on Ryzen 9 5950X Release, CPU use at full clip is p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.0743/0.0750/0.0765/0.5374/2.0974% of the 2.667 ms callback deadline.

Human listening, physical Tone Master Pro comparison, and target-device timing remain open. No Fender algorithm, settings, control range, DSP, or sound parity is claimed.

## Current Fractal FM9-family Plex Shift checkpoint (2026-11-26)

The current Fractal Audio Blocks Guide linked from FM9 Downloads has a November 2026 cover and 114 pages. Its Plex Shift section (printed pp. 87–90) describes per-line Shift and Detune, Master Pitch and Master Detune controls, and Forward/Reverse direction. The guide distinguishes Plex Shift from Plex Delay and Plex Detune. FM9 firmware v12.0 is listed for Original and Turbo, not Mark II Turbo separately; installed firmware is unknown, so applicability to that exact SKU remains a family-level qualification. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 downloads and firmware applicability](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) · [FM9 Mark II Turbo](https://shop.fractalaudio.com/products/fm9-mark-ii-turbo-amp-modeler-multi-fx-floor-unit)

The existing Delay-category Plex Matrix Delay at picker ID 76 now offers an optional project-authored granular pitch-shift subset, disabled by default and with no new picker row. Each active line has a project range of −24…+24 semitones and −50…+50 cents; Master Pitch and Master Detune are 0–200%, default 100%; Forward is the default direction. The shift remains a delay-tap processor, not a tracked guitar synth or general standalone pitch shifter. At 48 kHz stereo the full Plex processor state is 1,132,152 B, including preallocated pitch history capped at 256 KiB. `process()` allocates nothing. Dry signal stays immediate; the wet shifter uses a project-selected 5.333 ms granule target and shift/direction-dependent history lookback, so no single fixed wet latency is claimed.

`make test`, `make web-build`, `make web-browser-test`, `make windows-cross-build`, and `make bench` pass. HeadlessChrome 151 confirms the Delay category/default-off state and captures local prerecorded Guitar-TECHS DI through both unshifted and one-line +12-semitone Plex Shift. At 48 kHz stereo/128 frames on Ryzen 9 5950X Release, one isolated processor callback uses p50/p95/p99/max 0.2145/0.3124/0.3675/1.9463% of a 2.667 ms deadline with one shifted line; four lines use 0.4688/0.6945/0.8340/14.9479%; eight Forward lines use 0.7069/0.9803/1.1625/10.3679%; eight Reverse lines use 0.6529/0.9514/1.2019/7.8759%. The no-shift matrix baseline is 0.1324/0.2025/0.2434/4.7803%. All profiles recorded zero deadline misses in 20,000 callbacks. These are isolated host processor timings, not target-device guarantees or Fractal DSP measurements. Human listening, physical-device comparison, installed firmware, and actual tablet timing remain open.

## Fractal FM9-family Through-Zero Flanger VCO Response comparison (2026-11-27)

The November 2026 Blocks Guide (printed p. 50) names Linear, Exponential, and Square Law VCO Response, with an Exponent control; its text says an Exponent of 1.0 gives a linear sweep. It describes how VCO Response changes sweep shape/dwell, but does not publish the transfer law, Exponent bounds/default, CPU cost, or latency. The guide is family-level and the current FM9 downloads page does not list Mark II Turbo separately. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 firmware/download page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

TabletFX adds project-authored curve selection to its existing Through-Zero Flanger at Modulation ID 81; it does not add a picker row or delay buffer. Project default is Linear; Exponential applies `x^Exponent` over normalized LFO sweep position, Square Law applies `x²`, and Exponent defaults to 1.0 over project bounds 0.25–4.0. These are explicit local mappings, not claimed Fractal equations or parameter values. Automated curve, browser/WASM local-guitar, Windows compilation, and CPU/memory results are recorded in `IMPLEMENTATION_CHECKLIST.md` and `LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`. Exact hardware curves, model/SKU firmware applicability, listening, and target-device timing remain open.

## HeadRush Prime per-block Phase Invert control (2026-11-27)

The official Prime v5.1.0 firmware notes (August 2026) say Phase Invert was added to Advanced Block Edit to address phase cancellation when using Block Mix and in other scenarios. The v5.2.0 notes (October 2026) reproduce the v5.1.0 change entry; their v5.2-specific section does not describe this control, and does not explicitly state that its behavior was retained unchanged. These are release notes, not a complete parameter table. They do not establish polarity processing point, stereo/split-path behavior, bypass behavior, numeric bounds, defaults, DSP cost, or latency. The installed Prime firmware is unknown. [Prime v5.1.0 firmware notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/51/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.1.0.pdf) · [Prime v5.2.0 firmware notes](https://cdn.inmusicbrands.com/Software/SGEE/52/READ%20ME%20-%20HeadRush%20Prime%20Firmware%20Update%20Instructions%20v5.2.0.pdf)

TabletFX adds a default-off polarity toggle in Advanced Block Edit. Its target selector groups the existing processors by their current effect categories (122 targets in 17 groups); this is a per-block control, not another effect row. The project applies a sign flip to each channel after the selected enabled stage and leaves the option dormant when that stage is bypassed. This signal position and bypass rule are project choices, not Prime behavior verified by the release notes. Focused native and HeadlessChrome Play checks with local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI verify neutral default, sample-wise inversion while enabled, no change while bypassed, and category grouping. The shared portable core compiles for the Windows MinGW target; the broader native Windows UI/audio path is not yet integrated or runtime-verified. No HeadRush algorithm, parameter, DSP, or sound parity is claimed.

## Fractal FM9-family Global Mix — initial Chorus coverage (2026-11-27)

The November 2026 Fractal Blocks Guide describes Global Mix as a per-block opt-in to the global Effects Mix offset (up to ±50%) and lists Chorus, Delay, Flanger, Megatap Delay, Multitap Delay, Phaser, Pitch, Plex Delay, Resonator, Reverb, Synth, and Ten-Tap Delay. It does not define an exact formula for combining the offset with each block's Mix or publish the full per-block Mix laws. The August 2022 FM9 Owner Manual (firmware 3.x / Original and Turbo) lists a different set, including Formant, Ring Mod, and Rotary but not Synth or Megatap; treat this as a version/source discrepancy rather than merging the lists. FM9 downloads currently name v12.0 for FM9 Original and Turbo, not “FM9 Mark II Turbo” separately; applicability to that exact SKU and installed firmware remain unverified. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 Owner Manual](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/FM9/FM9-Owners-Manual.pdf) · [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

TabletFX adds an opt-in project Global Effects Mix adjustment to the existing Chorus at Modulation picker ID 16. The UI defaults to disabled and 0 pp; its slider is bounded to −50…+50 percentage points. For this project-only first implementation, the effective Chorus mix is `clamp(baseMix + offset, 0, 1)` when opted in, while the stored base Mix remains unchanged. This additive percentage-point rule is a project interpretation, not a published Fractal equation, setting, or sound match. No new effect row is added. The other guide-listed block families remain queued for separate compatibility checks and implementations.

Native, browser/WASM Play on local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI, and Windows MinGW checks pass. Browser RMS difference is 0 when disabled with a +20 pp setting, 0.041153 when enabled, and 0 for both endpoint-clamp pairs. Native memory is 34,856 B; browser effect state is 34,820 B. On Ryzen 9 5950X Release at 48 kHz stereo/128 frames, enabled/disabled p50 and p95 are 0.076/0.101% and 0.076/0.096% of the 2.667 ms callback budget; the highest measured maximum is 2.111% with no deadline misses. Full percentiles and test scope are in [`LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md`](LATENCY_AND_QUALITY.md). Manual listening and exact-SKU firmware applicability remain unresolved.

## Fractal FM9-family Global Mix — Delay subset (2026-11-27)

The November 2026 [Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf), printed p. 4, lists Delay for the per-block Global Mix opt-in and shared Effects Mix offset up to ±50%. Printed p. 39 says Delay uses a different Mix law than other blocks, but does not give the Global Mix combination equation. The current [FM9 downloads page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) lists v12.0 for FM9 Original/Turbo, not Mark II Turbo separately; exact SKU applicability and installed firmware remain unknown.

TabletFX adds a per-block project opt-in to the existing Delay at category Delay / picker ID 21. It defaults Off with 0 pp; the offset range is −50…+50 pp. When enabled, TabletFX uses `clamp(baseMix + offset, 0, 1)` and leaves the stored base Mix unchanged. This is a project-authored percentage-point interpretation; it does not reproduce the unpublished Fractal Delay Mix law or settings. The effect's existing parameter ramp is reused. There is no added picker row, buffer, fixed path latency, or process allocation.

`make test-fractal-global-mix-delay` passes focused native Delay tests and offline CLI checks. `make web-test-fractal-global-mix-delay` passes HeadlessChrome 151 Play with the local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI. The test checks Delay category/ID 21, default Off/0 pp, bounds, base-Mix preservation, disabled-offset RMS below 1e-7, enabled +20 pp RMS difference 0.0130543164, upper/lower clamp RMS 0, and browser effect state of 768,648 B. `make windows-cross-build` compiles the Windows MinGW shared core/offline renderer and the Delay benchmark.

The Ryzen 9 5950X Release benchmark used 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks, 3,000 warm-up and 20,000 measured calls, with a 2.6667 ms callback budget. Processor-call p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max were 0.225/0.263/0.318/1.049/9.368% with the offset stored at +20 pp but disabled, and 0.111/0.252/0.290/0.582/10.501% with it enabled. Both profiles used 768,832 B native state, made zero process allocations, added zero fixed latency, and missed 0/20,000 deadlines. These desktop results do not predict tablet or full-chain load. Human listening, physical FM9 Mark II Turbo comparison, installed firmware, exact SKU applicability, and target-device timing remain manual.

## Fractal FM9-family Global Mix — Flanger subset (2026-11-27)

The November 2026 [Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) lists Flanger as a Global Mix eligible block and describes the shared Effects Mix offset as ±50%; the Flanger Mix page is printed p. 50. Neither section specifies the exact equation. The current [FM9 downloads page](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/) lists firmware v12.0 for FM9 Original/Turbo, not Mark II Turbo separately; exact SKU applicability and installed firmware remain unknown.

TabletFX adds a per-block opt-in to the existing Flanger at Modulation picker ID 17. Its project offset defaults to 0 pp with the switch off, and ranges from −50 to +50 pp. When enabled in Flanger mode, the project computes `clamp(baseMix + offset, 0, 1)` while retaining the stored base Mix. The existing project Filter mode ignores this control. This additive percentage-point rule is a project interpretation, not Fractal's undocumented equation, setting, DSP, or sound parity.

`make test-fractal-global-mix-flanger` passes focused native Flanger tests and offline CLI checks. `make web-test-fractal-global-mix-flanger` passes HeadlessChrome 151 Play on local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI: picker category Modulation/ID 17, default off/0 pp, disabled-offset RMS 0, enabled +20 pp RMS 0.018654, upper-clamp RMS 0, lower-clamp RMS below 1e−15, and 4,808 B browser effect state. `make windows-cross-build` passes the Windows MinGW portable core/offline-renderer build.

The Ryzen 9 5950X Release benchmark at 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks measured the full Flanger processor at p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max 0.059/0.060/0.140/0.415/1.177% of a 2.667 ms budget with Global Mix disabled, and 0.059/0.073/0.092/0.413/1.059% enabled at +20 pp. Both profiles used 4,840 B native state, allocated nothing in `process()`, added no fixed latency, and missed 0/20,000 deadlines. The maximums are desktop scheduling-sensitive and these are not tablet or Fractal DSP estimates. Human listening, physical-device comparison, installed firmware and target-device timing remain manual.

## Fractal FM9-family Global Mix — Surround Delay subset (2026-11-27)

The November 2026 Blocks Guide (printed pp. 4 and 39) lists Delay for the Global Mix offset up to ±50% and notes that Delay uses a different Mix law. It does not state the Surround Delay offset equation. The current FM9 downloads page lists v12.0 for Original/Turbo, not Mark II Turbo separately; exact SKU applicability and installed firmware remain unknown. TabletFX implements a project-only opt-in on the existing categorized Delay ID 117. It is off at default, stores base Mix unchanged, and applies `clamp(baseMix + offset, 0, 1)` only when enabled. This is not a Fractal setting or parity claim.

`make web-test-fractal-surround-delay` passes HeadlessChrome 151 Play on local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI. It verifies Delay category/ID 117, default Off/0 pp, bounds, disabled-offset RMS 0, enabled +20 pp RMS 0.0225445485, both endpoint-clamp RMS 0, rotation response, and 144,132 B browser state. `make test-fractal-surround-delay` passes the focused native test; `make windows-cross-build` compiles the MinGW shared core and benchmark.

The Ryzen 9 5950X Release benchmark used 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks, a 2.6667 ms budget, 3,000 warm-up and 20,000 measured callbacks. Percentages are isolated processor-call time relative to one callback deadline:

| Profile | p50 | p95 | p99 | p99.9 | Max | Native state | Deadline misses |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Default stereo | 0.0713% | 0.0761% | 0.0803% | 0.3034% | 0.9825% | 144,176 B | 0/20,000 |
| +20 pp stored, opt-in Off | 0.0713% | 0.0765% | 0.0814% | 0.2805% | 26.8556% | 144,176 B | 0/20,000 |
| +20 pp enabled | 0.0709% | 0.0885% | 0.1118% | 0.2625% | 1.0260% | 144,176 B | 0/20,000 |
| Short rotating, high-feedback stereo | 0.0709% | 0.0761% | 0.1155% | 0.3506% | 4.1079% | 9,776 B | 0/20,000 |
| Maximum-time/ratio stereo | 0.1519% | 0.1763% | 0.3116% | 0.4755% | 5.4718% | 1,536,176 B | 0/20,000 |
| Default mono | 0.0263% | 0.0349% | 0.0465% | 0.2239% | 0.7609% | 144,176 B | 0/20,000 |

All observed maxima were below the callback deadline; the 26.8556% disabled-offset maximum is a single desktop scheduling outlier. The mix controls add 8 B to the native/browser processor state and no ring-buffer storage; the existing zero-allocation test covers `process()`. These figures are not full-chain, tablet, or Fractal DSP measurements. Human listening, physical comparison, installed firmware, exact Mark II Turbo applicability, and target-device timing remain manual.

## Fractal FM9-family Multitap Delay Global Mix subset (2026-11-27)

The November 2026 Blocks Guide lists Multitap Delay for Global Mix on printed p. 4 and lists its Mix-page controls on p. 68. The page establishes a per-block opt-in to the shared Effects Mix offset, but neither the guide nor the general Mix section gives the exact Multitap combination equation or most control defaults/ranges. FM9 downloads list v12.0 for Original/Turbo only; Mark II Turbo applicability and installed firmware are unverified. The existing project-authored Multi-Delay remains a Delay-category processor at picker ID 72. Its default-off control stores a separate −50…+50 percentage-point offset (default 0); when enabled the project adds it to base Mix and clamps the result to 0…1. This is a project interpretation, not a Fractal setting or parity claim.

`make test-fractal-multitap-global-mix` passes focused defaults, disabled-offset identity, positive/negative offsets, clamping, non-finite fallback, storage separation, and zero-process-allocation coverage. `make web-test-fractal-multitap-global-mix` passes HeadlessChrome 151 Play on the local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI: category Delay, effect index 72, Off/0 pp defaults, disabled-offset RMS 0, and enabled +20 pp RMS 0.0152142 over 48,000 frames. `make windows-cross-build` compiles the MinGW shared core and offline renderer.

The isolated Release benchmark used AMD Ryzen 9 5950X, 48 kHz, stereo 128-frame callbacks (2.6667 ms), 3,000 warm-up and 20,000 measured calls. CPU values are processor-call time as a percentage of one callback deadline; each profile reports 524,920 B state and 0/20,000 deadline misses:

| Profile | p50 | p95 | p99 | p99.9 | Max |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Default stereo | 0.1159% | 0.1748% | 0.2115% | 0.6008% | 1.1903% |
| +20 pp stored, opt-in Off | 0.1155% | 0.1691% | 0.1834% | 0.4455% | 0.7812% |
| +20 pp enabled | 0.1166% | 0.1819% | 0.1901% | 0.4560% | 0.5325% |
| Default mono | 0.1095% | 0.1534% | 0.1845% | 0.3968% | 0.5614% |

The stored-but-disabled option has no measurable path difference by design; the offset is combined during configuration, outside the sample loop. These desktop figures do not predict full-chain or target-device performance. Manual listening, FM9 hardware comparison, exact firmware applicability, and tablet timing remain open.

## Fractal FM9-family Phaser Global Mix subset (2026-11-27)

The current November 2026 Fractal Audio Blocks Guide, printed p. 4, lists Phaser among blocks eligible for the shared Global Mix offset up to ±50%. Printed pp. 64–65 describe the Phaser block and list Mix, Level, Balance, Bypass, Bypass Mode, and Global Mix; the block supports 2–12 stages and stereo I/O. The guide does not state the Phaser-specific offset equation or most parameter ranges/defaults. The current FM9 downloads page lists v12.0 for FM9 Original and Turbo, not Mark II Turbo separately. Exact Mark II Turbo applicability and installed firmware remain unknown. [November 2026 Blocks Guide](https://www.fractalaudio.com/downloads/manuals/fas-guides/Fractal-Audio-Blocks-Guide.pdf) · [FM9 downloads](https://www.fractalaudio.com/fm9-downloads/)

TabletFX adds a project-authored, default-off Global Mix option to the existing Modulation Phaser at picker ID 18. The offset is −50…+50 percentage points (default 0); when enabled, the project uses `clamp(base Mix + offset, 0, 1)` and preserves the stored base Mix. The offset is combined during parameter configuration, outside the sample loop. It adds no picker row, audio buffer, fixed latency, or processing allocation. This is not a Fractal setting, equation, DSP, or sound-parity claim.

Focused native tests and HeadlessChrome 151 Play on the local prerecorded stereo Guitar-TECHS DI pass. Browser checks confirm Modulation/ID 18, Off/0 pp defaults, disabled-offset RMS 0, and enabled +20/−20 pp RMS difference 0.0164916291. `make test` passes 123 native CTests and 13 fixture checks; `make windows-cross-build` compiles the MinGW core and offline renderer. At 48 kHz stereo/128-frame callbacks on a Ryzen 9 5950X Release build, the whole Phaser processor reports 360 B state and 0/20,000 deadline misses. Isolated p50/p95/p99/p99.9/max callback loads are 0.0720/0.0724/0.1076/0.3124/0.4384% at default, 0.0720/0.0724/0.1091/0.3218/0.4354% with +20 pp stored but disabled, 0.0720/0.0896/0.1084/0.2741/1.8184% at +20 pp enabled, and 0.0720/0.0724/0.0904/0.2629/0.7440% at −20 pp enabled. Maximum values vary with desktop scheduling; the mix offset itself is configuration-time. Human listening, physical comparison, exact firmware applicability, and target-device timing remain open.
